# 发布前审计 · 内审 round 2

> 本报告由主进程从 workflow 的**结构化 finding + verdict** 生成，而非综合 agent 的散文——
> 那份输出回来时从句中截断，宁可报告准确也不要它好看。

## 0. 覆盖与方法

12 条只读审查 lane，每条 pipeline 接一个**对抗性 verifier**（默认立场 REFUTED，必须自己读码、跑测试才能确认），
外加 1 个完备性 critic 与 1 个报告综合器，共 26 个 agent、0 错误、耗时 56 分钟。

审查范围是批 A–K 的未提交工作树 diff。**审查开始时四道硬闸是绿的**，所以这些 lane 找的不是编译错误，
而是「一套全绿的测试证明不了的东西」：修错了/修了一半、守卫无法失败、注释被同一次提交证伪、
N-1 契约被打破、并发与生命周期缺陷。

**结果**：91 条 finding —— BLOCKER 3 / MAJOR 25 / MINOR 62 / INVALID 1；
裁决 CONFIRMED 74 / PARTIAL 15 / REFUTED 2。

**审查覆盖不到的**：任何需要真实机器的东西。install.sh 的 broker 半边（`install_broker` 硬编码
`/etc/tether` 与 `/etc/systemd/system`，没有 PREFIX seam，`go test` 够不到）、simcluster drill、
多机 route mTLS。critic 的 CC-5 明确点了这一条。

## 1. 存活的 BLOCKER（3）

### A-F1 · `internal/auth/permissions.go:248 (member), internal/auth/permissions.go:318 (agent)`

- **lane**：_INBOX authorization surface (batch A) — internal/auth/permissions.go, internal/authcallout/handler.go, internal/cli/natsconn.go, internal/agent/agent.go (CustomInboxPrefix), cmd/tether/alert_gate.go, test/d3/inbox_isolation_acl_test.go
- **裁决**：CONFIRMED（原报 BLOCKER → 订正 BLOCKER）
- **主张**：The L1-F1 fix does not close the disclosure it claims to close: member and agent templates still grant bare `Sub "_INBOX.>"`, and a stranger self-promotes into the member template with two unauthenticated requests — so tunnel tokens, every subscriber PSK, the print-once /sub token and all `tether exec` output remain harvestable by an outsider.
- **失败场景**：Attacker mints an nkey locally. (1) CONNECT name="tether-cli" — roleCtlUnactivated is an unconditional allow (handler.go:227), no PIN, no DB lookup. (2) Request `tether.v2.ctrl.by.<own-actor>.session.create.req` with {Name:"x",PIN:"1234"} — handleSessionCreate (internal/broker/sessions.go:24) has ZERO admission control; it hashes the attacker-chosen PIN and makes the attacker the owner. (3) Reconnect name="tether-cli:x", Token="1234" — ensureMember passes, PermissionsForActivatedMember is minted, which contains `_INBOX.>` in Sub.Allow. (4) `Subscribe("_INBOX.>")` — accepted by nats-server; the attacker now receives every other connection's replies on the shared account, including each agent's register reply ProxyDirective{Token, Keys[].Secret}. Separately and with no session-create step at all: any agent in ANY session already holds `Sub _INBOX.>` and reads every other agent's register reply.
- **verifier 理由**：Every load-bearing claim survived. (1) CODE: permissions.go:248 (member Sub) and :318 (agent Sub) literally still contain bare `_INBOX.>`; only PermissionsForUnactivated lost it. (2) EMPIRICAL: I reproduced it in a scratch copy of the tree against a real routed nats-server with the real auth_callout responder — an ACTIVATED member (`tether-cli:lab` + PIN) and an AGENT (`tether-agent:lab:attacker-node` + PIN) both subscribed `_INBOX.>` with no async error and both received a payload published to a THIRD identity's derived subtree (`_INBOX.3b5738709b7f5d02.x.1` -> "TUNNEL_TOKEN+PSK"). (3) ESCALATION LADDER: handler.go:228 `case roleCtlUnactivated: return h.allow(...)` is an unconditional allow — no PIN, no DB lookup; internal/broker/sessions.go:24 handleSessionCreate has zero admission control beyond actor-token validity and a non-empty name, and requirements.md §5.2 explicitly documents `
- **对修法的批评**：The framing ("implement the adjudicated design OR re-adjudicate in writing") is right, but three parts of the proposed fix are wrong or under-specified. (a) "handleSessionCreate must not be reachable by an unauthenticated self-minted nkey" DIRECTLY CONTRADICTS docs/requirements.md §5.2, which grants `session create` / `session list` / `session login` to 任意身份 — that is the entire first-run onboarding path, not an oversight. Gating it is a requirements amendment, not a bug fix, and must not be smuggled in under an ACL finding; drop it from this finding's scope or raise it separately. (b) The plan's version-aware agent minting has a hole the reviewer repeats uncritically: `nodes.release_version` is SELF-REPORTED by the agent in NodeRegisterReq (internal/proto/messages.go:29, persisted by node.Register). An agent that wants to keep the blanket read simply reports an old version and the broke
- **建议修法**：Either implement the adjudicated design or re-adjudicate it in writing. Adjudicated design: (a) agents — in Handle's roleAgent arm read `nodes.release_version` for (sid,nid) and pass a flag into PermissionsForAgent so a proven-new agent gets only inboxSubjectFor(actor); no row / older release ⇒ keep the wildcard, converging automatically as the fleet upgrades; (b) members — drop `_INBOX.>` from PermissionsForActivatedMember by default and gate it behind a default-false broker config. Additionally close the escalation ladder itself: handleSessionCreate must not be reachable by an unauthenticated self-minted nkey (that is what makes the member template an anonymous surface at all). Whatever is

### C1 · `docs/architecture.md:351-361 (+ internal/broker/proxy.go:33-42, internal/cli/natsconn.go:61-66, internal/agent/agent.go:2226-2230)`

- **lane**：comments, documented invariants, and repo conventions
- **裁决**：CONFIRMED（原报 BLOCKER → 订正 BLOCKER）
- **主张**：The three prose sites written to correct the old false "reply inboxes are private" premise assert a NEW premise that is equally false: the retained bare `Sub "_INBOX.>"` grant is a strict superset of every per-identity `_INBOX.<tok>` subtree, so nothing is isolated from an authenticated agent or member.
- **失败场景**：Agent N1 in session A connects (it holds `Sub "_INBOX.>"`, permissions.go:318) and subscribes `_INBOX.>`. Agent N2 in session B registers; the broker's register reply lands on `_INBOX.<tok(N2)>.<nuid>.1`, which `>` matches. N1 receives ProxyDirective{Token, Keys[].Secret} — B's raw tunnel token plus every subscriber's Shadowsocks PSK — and, by the same subscription, every `tether exec` stdout/stderr chunk and every print-once `/sub` bearer token belonging to any session on the deployment. That is precisely the leak architecture.md:359 now says is closed ("auth_callout 只授予每个连接自己那棵子树"), that proxy.go:39 says "It is safe NOW because…", and that natsconn.go:63 says are "no longer readable by every other connection on the account". Only the anonymous/unactivated ctl was actually fixed. The repo's own home_delivery.go:369 still states the true fact — "EVERY agent (any session) is granted `Sub _INBOX.>` … so a token is DISCLOSED on the shared bus" — so the tree now contains two comments in direct contradiction, and the newer, more confident one is the wrong one. This is the second generation of the exact failure mode the correction was written to end: the plan's stated reason for touching these two sites was "不改这两处，下一个人会再次基于错误前提做设计."
- **verifier 理由**：I tried to refute this three ways and could not. (1) The code says what the reviewer claims: `internal/auth/permissions.go:248` (PermissionsForActivatedMember.Sub) and `:318` (PermissionsForAgent.Sub) both still list a bare `"_INBOX.>"`, and `InboxPrefixFor` (:40-42) returns `"_INBOX."+hex16` — its own doc at :28-33 says 'THE SHAPE IS LOAD-BEARING: the prefix is a SUBTREE OF `_INBOX`', which is exactly what makes the retained wildcard a strict superset. NATS `>` matches one-or-more tokens, so `_INBOX.>` covers `_INBOX.<tok>.<nuid>.<n>`. Only `PermissionsForUnactivated` actually lost the wildcard. (2) The three prose sites do assert the stronger claim: architecture.md:359 says 'auth_callout 只授予每个连接自己那棵 `_INBOX.<tok>` 子树' and the B.4 table cell now reads '否（靠 ACL）' with no scope qualifier; proxy.go:38-39 says 'It is safe NOW because auth_callout grants each connection only its own `_INBOX.
- **对修法的批评**：Fix (a) is the right direction but the plan's mechanism has a hole neither the plan nor the reviewer names: `nodes.release_version` is SELF-REPORTED by the agent (`proto.NodeRegisterReq.ReleaseVersion`, messages.go:29; filled at internal/agent/agent.go:1458 and persisted by node.Register / internal/node/plan.go:53). Minting the bare wildcard on the strength of a value the connecting principal chose means any provisioned agent can re-grant itself `_INBOX.>` by reporting an old version — and a provisioned agent is precisely the principal class the residual leak concerns. It still raises the bar (needs a valid nkey + provisioning row, and leaves a visible wrong version in `node ls`), but it must not be described as closing the hole. Two further constraints on (a): the version row is written by register.req, which rides the connection auth_callout just minted, so the FIRST connect after an u
- **建议修法**：Two acceptable resolutions, but not the current one. (a) Finish the adjudicated design: gate the bare `_INBOX.>` in PermissionsForAgent on the agent's persisted `nodes.release_version`, and in PermissionsForActivatedMember on a default-false broker config, so the prose becomes true. (b) If the wildcard stays, rewrite all three sites to state the actual boundary — "the ANONYMOUS reader is gone (PermissionsForUnactivated no longer grants the wildcard); an authenticated agent or activated member can still read every other connection's replies until the wildcard is dropped" — and set architecture.md's table cell back to 是 with that scope, rather than 否. Either way, reconcile with home_delivery.g

### G1 · `internal/auth/permissions.go:248 and :318`

- **lane**：ARE THE NEW GUARDS REAL? (adversarial audit of every test function added/modified by the uncommitted prerelease-audit diff)
- **裁决**：CONFIRMED（原报 BLOCKER → 订正 BLOCKER）
- **主张**：B1 (`_INBOX.>` credential leak) is closed only for the anonymous principal; activated members and agents still get bare `Sub "_INBOX.>"`, the guard tests only the anonymous template, and the comments/doc this same commit writes state the opposite as fact.
- **失败场景**：Any member of session A (a PIN-activated ctl) or any agent (a machine credential, on the least-trusted host in the fleet) connects and subscribes `_INBOX.>`. It receives every other connection's replies across ALL sessions: `tether exec` stdout/stderr, `ProxyDirective{Token, Keys[].Secret}` on every agent's register reply (tunnel token + every subscriber's Shadowsocks PSK), and `ProxySubCreateResp.SubURL` (the print-once /sub bearer). Nothing in `make test`/`make e2e-parallel` turns red, and a reader of `docs/architecture.md` B.4 or `internal/broker/proxy.go:34` is told the channel is now private.
- **verifier 理由**：I tried to refute this three ways and could not. (1) The code says what the reviewer claims: `internal/auth/permissions.go` has bare `"_INBOX.>"` at Sub lines 248 (PermissionsForActivatedMember) and 318 (PermissionsForAgent), directly below `inboxSubjectFor(actor)`. Only PermissionsForUnactivated dropped it. Since the new private prefix is deliberately a SUBTREE of `_INBOX` (InboxPrefixFor's own comment calls that shape 'load-bearing'), a `Sub "_INBOX.>"` holder matches every scoped inbox including upgraded peers' — the leak is fully intact for authorised principals, not partially. (2) The guard is anonymous-only: test/d3/inbox_isolation_acl_test.go's forbidden-subject loop calls `connectCLIWithErrCapture` (test/d3/acl_test.go:29), whose own doc comment says 'connects a fresh unactivated CLI (callout-issued PermissionsForUnactivated JWT)'. No test anywhere subscribes `_INBOX.>` as an act
- **对修法的批评**：The proposed fix is 'implement §决策A as adjudicated', and the adjudicated agent half is unsound on this repo's own precedent. `nodes.release_version` is SELF-REPORTED by the agent: proto.NodeRegisterReq.ReleaseVersion (internal/proto/messages.go:29) is a field the agent fills in. This repo already adjudicated that exact anti-pattern against itself — the same struct's Capabilities comment says 'the broker gates proxy allocation on the presence of CapProxyV1 here rather than guessing from ReleaseVersion — every source build shares "v0.0.0-dev", so a version string can't prove P13 support (external review round-2 F5)', pinned by TestExternalReviewCapabilityGateDoesNotTrustArbitraryDevLabels. So version-aware minting (a) hands a hostile or compromised agent an opt-out it controls — under-report the version, keep the bare wildcard, keep harvesting — which makes the 'fix' security theatre rathe
- **建议修法**：Implement §决策A as adjudicated, or state in the plan why it was abandoned. Agent template: pass the version the broker already persists (`nodes.release_version`, keyed by (sid,nid) which `PermissionsForAgent` already has) into the mint and append `"_INBOX.>"` only when the row is absent or pre-feature. Member template: drop `"_INBOX.>"` by default and add the `broker.auth.legacy_ctl_inbox` escape hatch (default false) the plan specified. Until that lands, fix the three comments and the doc so they say what is actually true. Extend test/d3/inbox_isolation_acl_test.go with the two missing principals — an activated member and an agent, each on its own connection (the file's own header explains w

## 2. 存活的 MAJOR（25）

### A-F2 · `internal/cli/completion_transport.go:134-141`

- **lane**：_INBOX authorization surface (batch A) — internal/auth/permissions.go, internal/authcallout/handler.go, internal/cli/natsconn.go, internal/agent/agent.go (CustomInboxPrefix), cmd/tether/alert_gate.go, test/d3/inbox_isolation_acl_test.go
- **裁决**：CONFIRMED（原报 MAJOR → 订正 MAJOR）
- **主张**：A production ctl connection builds its own nats.Option list with `nats.Nkey(...)` but no `nats.CustomInboxPrefix`, so under the new unactivated template it subscribes outside its grant and every session-name shell completion silently returns nothing.
- **失败场景**：Operator types `tether session rm <TAB>` or `tether login --session <TAB>`. cobra calls CompleteOwnedSessions/CompleteVisibleSessions → natsTransport.ListSessions (completion_transport.go:227) → t.dial(ctx, CtlNameUnactivated) → connectFn, which signs with the real identity nkey but sets no inbox prefix. auth_callout mints PermissionsForUnactivated, whose Sub.Allow no longer contains `_INBOX.>` — only `_INBOX.<sha256(nkey)[:16]>.>`. nats.go's response multiplexer subscribes `_INBOX.<nuid>.*`; the server refuses it; the reply never arrives; RequestWithContext burns the 1s completion budget and returns an error, so completion is empty with no message. Same class for ListNodes/ListPorts (activated) — those still work today ONLY because the member template kept the bare wildcard (A-F1), so they break the moment A-F1 is actually fixed.
- **verifier 理由**：Reproduced. internal/cli/completion_transport.go's production connectFn builds its own option list (Name/Timeout/RetryOnFailedConnect/MaxReconnects/NoEcho/Nkey at lines ~135-142, plus proxydial opts) and never sets nats.CustomInboxPrefix; `grep -rn CustomInboxPrefix --include=*.go` finds exactly two production sites (internal/cli/natsconn.go:66 and internal/agent/agent.go:2230) and completion_transport is not one of them. I replicated that option list verbatim in a scratch test/d3 test against the real server + responder: `RESULT: reply=<nil> err=nats: timeout asyncErr=nats: permissions violation: Permissions Violation for Subscription to "_INBOX.4EsFluBqlcKPSAc0VsZlU1.*"`. Production reachability confirmed: NewCompletionTransport leaves `connect` nil (so connectFn runs), is wired at 9 cmd/tether call sites, and ListSessions (completion_transport.go:220) is the only user of CtlNameUnacti
- **对修法的批评**：The primary proposal — route connectFn through cli.ConnectNATSWithNkey — is the right one and is cheap (nats.go takes last-option-wins, so passing Timeout/RetryOnFailedConnect/MaxReconnects/NoEcho as opts is safe). One real caveat the reviewer missed: ConnectNATSWithNkey appends its OWN proxydial options with `proxyDialTimeout = 10s` (natsconn.go:17), while completion deliberately uses `completionDialTimeout` for the proxy hop so a tab-completion can't outlive its budget. Routing through the shared helper must either thread the proxy timeout through or the completion path silently regains a 10s proxy dial — a regression in the opposite direction. The secondary proposal, an AST gate requiring every non-test `nats.Connect` reached with `nats.Nkey` to also carry `nats.CustomInboxPrefix`, would go red immediately on internal/broker/authcallout.go:59: the broker connects with its own nkey and
- **建议修法**：Make connectFn go through cli.ConnectNATSWithNkey (adding nats.Timeout/RetryOnFailedConnect/MaxReconnects/NoEcho as opts) so there is exactly ONE place that knows about the prefix, instead of duplicating the option list. If the two must stay separate, add `nats.CustomInboxPrefix(auth.InboxPrefixFor(pubKey))` at line 141 plus a static guard that every non-test `nats.Connect` reached with `nats.Nkey` also carries `nats.CustomInboxPrefix` (an AST gate in test/architecture, same shape as tls_verify_pairing_test.go). A behavioural guard belongs in test/d3: dial CtlNameUnactivated through the production helper and assert session.list.req round-trips.

### A-F3 · `internal/authcallout/handler.go:435`

- **lane**：_INBOX authorization surface (batch A) — internal/auth/permissions.go, internal/authcallout/handler.go, internal/cli/natsconn.go, internal/agent/agent.go (CustomInboxPrefix), cmd/tether/alert_gate.go, test/d3/inbox_isolation_acl_test.go
- **裁决**：CONFIRMED（原报 MAJOR → 订正 MAJOR）
- **主张**：The new process-wide argon2 budget is charged BEFORE the checks that decide whether argon2 runs at all, so one unauthenticated IP can drain it at zero CPU cost and permanently deny every new agent bootstrap and every PIN member join.
- **失败场景**：Attacker sends CONNECT name=`tether-agent:ghost:node-x`, Token=`x` from a single address, ~2/s. ValidateSID/ValidateNID pass ("ghost" is a legal identifier); agentprov.Lookup returns ErrNotProvisioned; pin != ""; pinRateLimited is false at first; spendPINBudget() consumes one global token; ProvisionWithPIN then returns ErrSessionMissing at internal/agentprov/agentprov.go:120 — BEFORE verifyPIN — so no argon2 runs, and the handler's switch routes ErrSessionMissing to a branch that does NOT call recordPINFailure, so the attacker's per-IP bucket is never charged either. Global bucket is burst 10 refilling 1/s, so >1 req/s keeps it at zero indefinitely. From then on every honest first-time agent (`tether agent --pin`) and every honest `tether login` PIN join gets ErrPINRateLimited. For an agent this is its FIRST connect, so everAuthed is false, isAuthFailure treats it as terminal and the unit exits. Net effect: one unauthenticated source, negligible cost, permanently blocks all new provisioning fleet-wide on that broker.
- **verifier 理由**：Reproduced end to end in a scratch copy. Ordering is exactly as claimed: handler.go calls `h.spendPINBudget()` unconditionally immediately before `provision(...)`, and agentprov.ProvisionWithPIN returns ErrSessionMissing at the `sql.ErrNoRows` arm BEFORE `verifyPIN`, so no argon2 runs; the `errors.Is(err, agentprov.ErrSessionMissing)` arm returns without recordPINFailure (only ErrInvalidPIN charges the per-IP bucket). My scratch test in package authcallout: 40 CONNECTs as `tether-agent:ghost:node-x` from 198.51.100.7 completed in 39.7ms total (vs ~0.15-0.3s each if argon2 had run), each denied `session "ghost" does not exist`; the attacker's per-IP bucket was NOT blocked afterwards; and then an honest FIRST-TIME agent bootstrap for the real session from a clean address 203.0.113.99 was refused with `rate_limited: too many PIN attempts from your network address; retry shortly`, as was an
- **对修法的批评**：The MINIMAL fix (hoist the session existence/ACTIVE check above pinRateLimited/spendPINBudget in ensureAgentProvisioned, mirroring ensureMember) is correct, cheap, and introduces no new oracle — the deny text already discloses `session "ghost" does not exist`. The 'better and drift-proof' wrapper is NOT drift-proof and should not be preferred: the seam signatures are `ProvisionAgentWrite func(sid, nid, fp, pin string, now time.Time) error` and `JoinMemberWrite func(sid, fp, pin string, now time.Time) error` (handler.go:84-85) — neither takes a verifier, so in CLUSTER MODE the verify happens behind raft where the handler cannot wrap it, and the charge would silently vanish exactly where the broker is most loaded. Adopting the wrapper therefore requires changing both seam signatures and the broker/FSM wiring that supplies them, which is a much larger blast radius than the finding warrants;
- **建议修法**：Charge the budget only where argon2 actually runs. Minimal and symmetric with ensureMember (which already checks session.IsActive first): in ensureAgentProvisioned, do the session existence/ACTIVE check before pinRateLimited/spendPINBudget, so a nonexistent or deleting sid is refused for free on both sides. Better and drift-proof: wrap the injected verifier — `verify := func(pin, hash string) error { h.spendPINBudget(); return auth.VerifyPIN(pin, hash) }` — so the charge is structurally tied to the one call that costs 0.15-0.3s (the cluster-mode write seams need the same wrapper passed through). Also split the deny text: `rate_limited_global: broker PIN-verification budget exhausted` vs the

### AC-1 · `internal/agent/agent.go:2271-2278 (attributeDialFailure), call site 1322`

- **lane**：agent connection and session lifetime (batch I: AC-F1/AC-F2/AC-F3, plus the everAuthed arm and the new CustomInboxPrefix that lands in the same function)
- **裁决**：CONFIRMED（原报 MAJOR → 订正 MAJOR）
- **主张**：AC-F3's per-URL attribution is gated on `errors.Is(poolErr, nats.ErrNoServers)`, which is only true when every pool entry after the denying one failed with literally "connection refused"; the ordinary down-broker error (i/o timeout) leaves the auth denial hidden exactly as before, and the `// already attributed` comment on that early return is false.
- **失败场景**：Cluster roster = [brokerA, brokerB]. brokerA is mid-restart and its auth_callout responder is not subscribed yet, so it answers `-ERR 'Authorization Violation'`. brokerB's host is down / firewalled DROP / partitioned, so the dial ends in `dial tcp <B>:4222: i/o timeout`. nats.go's connect() only rewrites the last error to nil for "connection refused" (nats.go@v1.52.0 nats.go:2815-2818), so the pooled error is the timeout, not ErrNoServers. attributeDialFailure returns nil on its first line, isAuthFailure(err) is false, and connectNATS falls through to the plain transient-retry tail. A leased agent therefore keeps re-presenting the lease name the broker just refused, forever — the precise defect AC-F3 was written to close. It is also order-dependent: move the denying broker to the end of the pool and it works; anywhere else and it silently does not.
- **verifier 理由**：Reproduced empirically. I built pool=<denying>,<silent-blackhole> and ran nats.Connect: the pooled error is `read tcp ...: i/o timeout`, errors.Is(err, nats.ErrNoServers)=false, isAuthFailure=false, and attributeDialFailure returns nil on its first line. The same pool with a refused address last yields ErrNoServers and attribution succeeds. Upstream confirms the mechanism: nats.go@v1.52.0 nats.go:2790-2820 keeps the LAST server's error and nils it out only for `strings.Contains(err.Error(), "connection refused")`; ErrNoServers is set only when err ended nil. So the gate holds exactly when every pool entry after the denying one failed with a literal connection-refused, and silently does not hold for timeout / TLS / any other tail failure. The `// already attributed` comment on that early return is therefore false as written — a timeout is not attributed at all. It is also order-dependent:
- **对修法的批评**：Directionally correct and I could not find a case where `isAuthFailure(poolErr) -> return nil` plus `len(urls) >= 2` is wrong (denying-broker-last is exactly the already-attributed case). But it must not land alone. (a) It widens the probe from `ErrNoServers only` to EVERY multi-URL failure, i.e. every retry iteration of an ordinary all-brokers-down outage. Each probe dial goes through the connTracker, which bounds it at teardownDialTimeout = 2s (conn_teardown.go:63), so a 5-broker roster adds up to 10s of serial dialling per retry iteration, forever, on the most common transient. (b) Each probe carries nats.Token(PIN), so the widened trigger multiplies argon2id auth_callout decisions against the process-wide budget batch D just added (rate.Every(1s)/burst 10) — AC-5's cost, amplified by AC-1's fix. The two findings must be fixed in one change, not separately. (c) attributeDialFailure ta
- **建议修法**：Drop the ErrNoServers gate and drive attribution off the thing that actually matters: `if isAuthFailure(poolErr) { return nil }` (already attributed) plus `len(urls) >= 2`. Every other multi-URL failure — ErrNoServers, i/o timeout, x509, ErrNoResponders — is a pool-flattened error whose per-URL truth is unknown, which is what the probe exists to discover. Fix the `// already attributed` comment to say what the condition really tests. Then extend TestDialFailureIsAttributedAcrossAMultiURLPool with a third pool shape whose last entry times out (a listener that accepts and never writes INFO, or 192.0.2.1) and assert the auth error is still returned.

### C2 · `internal/tunnel/tunnel.go:159 (+ internal/cluster/lock_lease.go:249, internal/agent/exec.go:421, cmd/tether/main.go:110)`

- **lane**：comments, documented invariants, and repo conventions
- **裁决**：CONFIRMED（原报 MAJOR → 订正 MAJOR）
- **主张**：Four new declarations were inserted directly beneath an existing declaration's doc comment with no blank line, so each one steals a doc comment written about a different symbol and leaves the original symbol undocumented. One of the stolen comments now names an identifier that does not exist anywhere in the repo.
- **失败场景**：A reader (or `go doc`) opening `internal/tunnel/tunnel.go` sees a doc comment beginning "yamuxLogger routes yamux's own logging into slog instead of a bare fd 2" attached to `func muxConfig`; `grep -rn yamuxLogger --include=*.go` finds exactly one hit — that comment line. The type is `yamuxWriter`. The reader either hunts for a symbol that was never written, or concludes the comment describes something else and stops trusting it. In the other three, the theft is worse because the orphaned comment is the load-bearing one: `startBounded`'s doc records the gotcha-#81 near-miss and why the wrapper must not be inlined ("a second copy is a second place to forget it"), and after this diff `startBounded` at exec.go:479 has no doc at all while `reapAbandonedExecChild` at :438 carries it; `isAgentDaemonInvocation`'s doc records the boot-budget rationale and its conservative exclusions, and after this diff the function at main.go:128 has no doc while `var agentNonDaemonSubcommands` at :116 carries it; `upgradeMarkerExists`/`growMarkerOwnedBy` (lock_lease.go:268, :272) lose the paragraph explaining why a renewal must be gated on them, which now sits atop `upgradeMarkerHeldByOther` at :249. The next person to change `startBounded` or `isAgentDaemonInvocation` reads no rationale and re-learns it the expensive way — the exact cost CLAUDE.md §5 names when it says "注释是资产，任何重构必须整段搬运".
- **verifier 理由**：All four sites reproduce exactly. exec.go: the `startBounded` doc block (the gotcha-#81 'a second copy is a second place to forget it' rationale) runs with no blank line into `// reapAbandonedExecChild ends a child whose spawn window we already gave up on.`, so the whole block is reapAbandonedExecChild's doc; `func (a *Agent) startBounded` at :479 has no comment at all. main.go: the `isAgentDaemonInvocation` doc runs into `// agentNonDaemonSubcommands are the ...` with no separator (visible verbatim in `git diff cmd/tether/main.go`); `func isAgentDaemonInvocation` at :128 has no doc. lock_lease.go: 'upgradeMarkerExists / growMarkerOwnedBy are the ownership predicates a renewal is gated on' runs into `// upgradeMarkerHeldByOther renders ...` (the diff's `+` block starts on the line immediately after it), so both named predicates are left undocumented and the paragraph now sits on a third
- **对修法的批评**：The fix is right in substance but 'insert a blank line' alone is wrong at three of the four sites and would make them worse: in exec.go, main.go and lock_lease.go the orphaned block sits ABOVE the new declaration, so a bare blank line leaves a floating comment attached to nothing while the original symbol stays undocumented. Each block must be MOVED to sit above its own declaration — the startBounded paragraph down to exec.go:479, the isAgentDaemonInvocation paragraph down past the `var` block to :128, and the ownership-predicate paragraph down to `upgradeMarkerExists` at :268. Reject the tunnel.go alternative 'rename `yamuxWriter` to `yamuxLogger`': the type is an `io.Writer` adapter feeding `log.New`, not a logger, and renaming it to match a wrong comment inverts the fix. Split the block in two as the reviewer's second option says. No structural-budget risk: `structural_budget_test.go`
- **建议修法**：At each of the four sites, insert a blank line between the pre-existing comment block and the new declaration's own doc, and move the new doc below the new declaration's rightful position. In tunnel.go additionally rename the stolen opening line's subject: either rename `yamuxWriter` to `yamuxLogger`, or make the block two comments — one on `yamuxWriter` about slog routing and the Debug-level choice, one on `muxConfig` about `VerifyConfig` requiring exactly one of Logger/LogOutput.

### C4 · `internal/broker/xfer_inflight.go:865-869`

- **lane**：comments, documented invariants, and repo conventions
- **裁决**：CONFIRMED（原报 MAJOR → 订正 MAJOR）
- **主张**：`commitSyntheticTransferTerminal`'s doc comment was left describing forward-to-leader-only behaviour while the same commit added a single-mode branch that forwards nothing, retries nothing, and returns true with no sink attached.
- **失败场景**：A reader asking "can this function emit a terminal without a committed forward?" reads the doc — "SYNCHRONOUSLY forwards a #57 synthetic terminal to the leader and reports whether it durably COMMITTED", "uses transferAuditForwardSync … with the same bounded transient-retry as the async sink", "Returns false when the sink is not yet attached (never delete the ledger on an unconfirmed emit)" — and answers no. On a single broker (`b.cl == nil`) all three sentences are false: the guard is now `if fwd == nil && b.cl != nil`, the single-mode arm does one `b.publishAudit(...)` with no retry loop, and returns true. The caller then deletes the ledger row on the strength of a doc-comment promise the code no longer keeps. The accurate description exists only as an inline comment inside the new branch, where nobody grepping for the function's contract will see it.
- **verifier 理由**：Verified line by line. The doc at xfer_inflight.go:865-869 is unchanged context (`git diff` shows the changed guard at :871-872 and the new single-mode arm at :880-896, not the doc). All three of its sentences are false when `b.cl == nil`: nothing is forwarded to a leader, `transferAuditForwardAttempts`'s retry loop is not entered (the single-mode arm does one `b.publishAudit` and returns), and 'Returns false when the sink is not yet attached' is inverted — the new guard is `if fwd == nil && b.cl != nil`, so an unattached sink in single mode returns TRUE. The stakes are real, not rhetorical: both callers (:527 and :793) delete the object and consume the ledger row on true, and the caller's own comment at :789-792 restates the doc's promise ('the ledger may be deleted ONLY after the synthetic terminal is durably COMMITTED'). Single-broker is the shape this whole change exists to fix, so i
- **对修法的批评**：The proposed replacement text is itself not quite true and would need one more clause. `publishAudit` (broker.go:749-762) only goes to JetStream when `b.js != nil`; with no JetStream it falls back to a core `nc.Publish` that returns nil on a best-effort, lossy send — so in that sub-case `commitSyntheticTransferTerminal` returns true and the caller deletes the ledger on an emit that was never durable. The branch's own inline comment argues this cannot happen ('a single broker supporting tier-B necessarily has JetStream'), which is plausible for tier-B but is an argument, not a guard, and the replacement doc should carry it rather than assert 'publishes... straight to the audit stream'. Suggested wording: '...publishes the deterministic terminal through publishAudit, which is durable whenever JetStream is present (the only configuration in which tier-B can run) and best-effort otherwise'.
- **建议修法**：Rewrite the doc comment to describe both modes: "In cluster mode, synchronously forwards to the leader with bounded transient retry and returns false on a persistent failure or an unattached sink. In single mode (b.cl == nil) there is no leader to forward to: publishes the deterministic terminal straight to the audit stream and returns whether the publish succeeded." Keep the deterministic-Ts argument — it is the load-bearing half and currently only lives inside the branch.

### E2 · `internal/broker/broker.go:2007 (mayRelease default) with effects at 2022, 2027 and 2068`

- **lane**：Batch E — lease farewell + roster adoption (BC-F1 / BC-F3 / #26-#27)
- **裁决**：CONFIRMED（原报 MAJOR → 订正 MAJOR）
- **主张**：BC-F3's holder check is unenforced whenever leaseHolder has no entry for the name, and that map is repopulated only by that agent's next REGISTER — so after any broker restart, re-exec or leader change a non-holder's farewell still lands all three effects on a live incumbent, for as long as the incumbent's connection stays up.
- **失败场景**：Broker restarts (an ordinary `broker upgrade` re-execs the process; a leader election moves the register handler to a broker whose map never had the entry). leaseHolder is now empty for `lab/gpu1-02`, while instance B is still connected and serving under that name. Any sibling clone of the same image can authenticate as `gpu1-02` — that is what the auth suffix fallback is for, and it is the exact threat model TestANonHolderFarewellCannotTakeTheIncumbentOffline describes — and publishes one farewell on its own legal register subject. `mayRelease` defaults to true because `b.leaseHolder.Load` misses, so the probe cache is invalidated (2027), and markNodeOfflineOnRelease (2068) writes `status=OFFLINE, proxy_ready=0` on B's row. B now fails admitACL's node-ONLINE check (exec/run/kill/expose refused), vanishes from /sub, and its name is re-issuable to another clone. The next heartbeat restores ONLINE but NOT proxy_ready. Critically, the window is not one heartbeat: grep shows leaseHolder is written only from adjudicateLease and assignLeaseName (broker.go:1851/1877/1910/1960/2190), never from the heartbeat path, so a stable agent that does not reconnect leaves the name unprotected for days.
- **verifier 理由**：I tried to refute this four ways and failed on all four. (a) The code says what is claimed: broker.go:2007 `mayRelease := true`, overridden only inside `if v, ok := b.leaseHolder.Load(...); ok`. Cold map => probeCache.Delete (2027) and markNodeOfflineOnRelease (2068) both run for a non-holder, with `leaseRow` true in the production shape (binding on the basename, none on `<base>-NN`). (b) The map really is only warmed by the register path: Store appears at broker.go:1851/1877/1910/1960/2190 and nowhere else; the heartbeat handler (2531-2573) never touches it. The agent runs a FULL register only at session start (agent.go:1029) and on nats.go reconnect (proxy.go:902); the <=3-min roster refresh carries no InstanceID and is short-circuited at broker.go:2362 before adjudication. So a stable connection never re-warms the entry. (c) The durable cold window is real and is not just a restart ra
- **对修法的批评**：The diagnosis is right; the proposed fix has one fatal defect and one dangerous phrase. FATAL: 'reply the transient code the agent already retries' cannot work — the farewell is fire-and-forget. instance.go:723 does `nc.Publish(SubjNodeRegister(...), body)` + FlushTimeout, with no reply subject and no retry loop, and b.replyJSON/replyErr both return immediately when msg.Reply == "" (sessions.go:210). So the probe-pending arm silently DROPS the release. That arm is not rare: inlineProbeGrace is 15ms (broker.go:1546) and the sender is a process in the middle of shutdown whose subscription is often still in the interest table but no longer answering claim-probe, which is precisely the 'interest exists, nothing answered' shape that defers. The result would be that legitimate cold-map releases are lost exactly where TestAFarewellStillReleasesWhenTheHolderMapIsCold says they must not be — F11
- **建议修法**：Do not skip the check on a cold map — resolve it with machinery already in this file. When `leaseHolder.Load` misses, call `leaseProbe(b, sid, nid, now)` (the same call adjudicateLease makes at broker.go:1864/1885); if it comes back ready and `heldByOther(req.InstanceID)` says a live process with a different instance id answers, log and reply OK but change nothing. If the probe is not ready, reply the transient code the agent already retries rather than acting on a name whose holder is unknown. Keep the in-memory `released` flag unconditional — it is not remotely visible and is what makes the successor's restart fast. Then amend broker.go:508-520 to say what the map is now used for, and add

### F-T1 · `internal/broker/xfer_inflight.go:510`

- **lane**：transfer subsystem (batch F: BT-F1..F5 + #57)
- **裁决**：CONFIRMED（原报 BLOCKER → 订正 MAJOR）
- **主张**：#57's single-broker enablement is wired for only ONE of the two ledger dispositions: a STAGED terminal can never be replayed on a single broker, because replayStagedTerminal bails on `b.cl == nil`, which is always true there — so the row is retained forever and its terminal is never emitted.
- **失败场景**：Single broker in the shape install.sh renders (data_dir commented out, db: /var/lib/tether/tether.db). A tier-B push finishes; emitTerminalTransferAudit (transfer.go:1043) stages the exact `complete` record into the NEW /var/lib/tether/xfer-inflight/<hash>.json, then the process is SIGKILLed before emitTransferAuditWithCommit's onCommitted runs removeXferInflight. On restart the row is present and start-plus-terminal. Every `xfer-inflight-finalize` pass (XferReapInterval, default 5 min, reconcile_passes.go:295) reads it, ledgerRowDisposition returns ledgerReplay ('exact terminal staged in place'), and replayStagedTerminal hits `if serr != nil || b.cl == nil` -> Warn + `return false`. Outcome: (a) the audit stream keeps a `start` that no terminal ever answers — verbatim the #57 defect this batch claims to close, on the deployment shape the batch was written for; (b) a Warn every 5 minutes forever, ~288 lines/day per stuck row, which is the log-churn/evidence-destruction class the same plan flags as BDP-F3. Before batch F this branch was unreachable in a default single broker (xferInflightDir() returned "", so no rows existed); the fallback at line 84 made it live without fixing it.
- **verifier 理由**：Reproduced. Probe in a scratch copy: Broker{cfg:{DBPath:<tmp>/tether.db}}, clusterMode=false, b.cl==nil, xferInflightDir()="<tmp>/xfer-inflight"; wrote a row carrying Terminal; three consecutive finalizeStrandedXfers calls each returned finalized=0, err=nil, and the row file survived all three. Every structural claim checks out: b.cl is assigned only at broker.go:955 inside `if b.clusterMode`, so it is nil for the whole life of a single broker; ledgerRowDisposition returns ledgerReplay/'exact terminal staged in place' (xfer_inflight.go:608); replayStagedTerminal:510 is `if serr != nil || b.cl == nil` -> Warn + return false; and the git diff shows line 510 was NOT touched by this batch while line 84 (the fallback that makes the branch live) and line 872 (`fwd == nil && b.cl != nil`) were. transferTerminalAlreadyCommitted's own doc at :934-936 does say a nil cluster runtime 'answers unknow
- **对修法的批评**：Direction is right and cheap — dropping `|| b.cl == nil` changes no signature, no method count, no wire format, so there is no N-1 or structural-budget exposure, and it restores consistency with the helper's documented contract. Three things the fix must carry that the reviewer did not say. (a) It MUST land together with F-T8's `b.js == nil` precondition. As written it hands the row to commitSyntheticTransferTerminal, whose single-mode arm returns true on publishAudit's nil error — and publishAudit falls back to a fire-and-forget core nc.Publish when b.js is nil. So the fix would trade 'row retained forever' for 'row deleted on an unacknowledged publish', which is strictly worse (it destroys the only durable evidence). (b) It does NOT give single mode exactly-once, and the comment must say so rather than inherit the false dedup claim from F-T2: when the crash was post-publish, the replay
- **建议修法**：Drop `|| b.cl == nil` from xfer_inflight.go:510, leaving `if serr != nil { retain }`. transferTerminalAlreadyCommitted already returns (false, nil) for a nil cluster runtime, so the row then falls through to commitSyntheticTransferTerminal, which batch F already taught to publish in single mode. That makes the two dispositions consistent and matches the helper's documented contract. Pin it with a test in the shape of the probe above: single-mode broker, staged terminal, one finalize pass, assert finalized==1 and the row gone.

So reverting BT-F5's entire production effect leaves the suite green. The reference census is exactly as claimed — grep over internal/ test/ cmd/ returns the declaration at transfer.go:227 plus transfer.go:1099, transfer.go:1269 and transfer_budget_test.go:497 (a direct call with literal arguments), and no test anywhere pushes an oversized path or transfer_id through a handler. - **对修法的批评**：Sound, and it correctly reuses fixtures that already exist and are already proven in this same batch (xfer_provision_test.go:290 pushWiringBroker + TestATierAPushCannotNameAnotherSessionsBucket, which drives handlePushReq over real NATS and tolerates nats.ErrNoResponders). Ordering checks out: in handlePushReq the bounds call sits after the emptiness check and BEFORE the tier check, and in handlePullReq it is the first check after json.Unmarshal, so a long path with an otherwise valid request reaches it in both. Three notes. (1) The existing fixture wires a subscription for push only; the pull case needs its own subscribe on handlePullReq. (2) countingJS gained a Publish method in this batch specifically so a push can run to completion without segfaulting inside publishAudit — the pull case should confirm it does not need the same. (3) Adding two Test functions appends to test/determinis
- **建议修法**：The fixture already exists and the shape is already proven in the same batch: xfer_provision_test.go:290's pushWiringBroker + the real-NATS drive used by TestATierAPushCannotNameAnotherSessionsBucket. Add two cases there — a push with path=strings.Repeat("x", transferPathMaxLen+1) and a pull with transfer_id=strings.Repeat("x", transferIDMaxLen+1) — asserting the reply is code request_invalid whose detail contains 'path length' / 'transfer_id length', and that b.transfers.get(id) is nil (no slot taken).

### G-1 · `internal/broker/clusterwrite.go:527-552 (+ internal/broker/audit_publisher.go:511-541)`

- **lane**：cluster operations (batch G: #13/#14/#15/#16) — retire step bounding, post-removal readiness gate, drain phase ordering, alert-ack validation
- **裁决**：CONFIRMED（原报 MAJOR → 订正 MAJOR）
- **主张**：The #15 relaxation compares the PRE-removal replica count against the POST-removal target while discarding nodeID, so a caught-up replica living on the node about to be removed is counted toward the floor it is supposed to survive; the comment's claim that "no conservatism was traded away" is false.
- **失败场景**：3-voter cluster ctl-1 (raft leader, and the broker running the observation), ctl-2, ctl-3 — all three alive and voting. A session was created recently, so stream history-<sid> is configured R3 but ctl-3's replica is still copying (Current=false). ActualReplicas therefore returns 2 (ctl-1 self + ctl-2 Current). Operator runs `tether cluster retire ctl-2` — a planned decommission of a perfectly healthy node. At OpStateStreamsAtTarget: NumVoters()=3, so the gate asks ReplicasFor(2)=2, and Actual=2 >= 2 → ready. LEADER_TRANSFERRED is a no-op (ctl-2 isn't leader), RAFT_REMOVED re-checks with the same predicate and gets the same answer, then RemoveServer(ctl-2) + roster delete run. Post-removal the stream has exactly ONE caught-up replica (ctl-1) against a 2-voter target of 2, with ctl-2's peer entry permanently stranded in the stream's raft group (tether never issues a JS peer-remove — see ProbeMetaCanPlace's comment). The old gate asked AllAtTarget (Target=ReplicasFor(3)=3 vs Actual=2) and held until ctl-3 caught up, so this outcome was structurally impossible before this change. The gate's own stated purpose — "retire removes a voter, and doing that on an unverifiable stream posture is the one outcome this gate exists to stop" (cluster_operation_controller.go:1075-1080) — is exactly what now happens.
- **verifier 理由**：I tried to refute this four ways and could not. (1) The code says what is claimed: clusterwrite.go:527 is `func (b *Broker) clusterStreamsReady(string) (bool, error)` — the parameter is genuinely unnamed and unused, and line 548-552 reads NumVoters() and compares every stream's Actual against jsstream.ReplicasFor(nv-1). (2) The report is node-blind: jsstream/replicas.go:119-137 ActualReplicas = 1 (the stream's own leader replica) + each peer that is Current && !Offline, with no identity retained — CollectStreamState (jsstream.go:290-304) reads info.Cluster.Replicas but discards the names, so a ReplicaReport cannot distinguish 'the missing replica is the node we are retiring' from 'the missing replica is elsewhere'. (3) The scenario is reachable, not exotic: cluster-runbook.md:279/314 documents 'retire one at a time down to N=1' as the supported downgrade, i.e. retiring perfectly healthy
- **对修法的批评**：The direction is right; two of the three concrete proposals have problems. (a) The 'minimal correct interim' is WRONG as stated: 'apply ReplicasFor(nv-1) only when the target is provably absent from the raft config' re-deadlocks #15 exactly, because a dead voter stays in the raft configuration until the RemoveServer this very gate is guarding — absence from the config is the post-condition, not the pre-condition. Only the 'marked down' disjunct is usable, and tether's down-ness is a leader-tick observation (pollClusterHealth), not a committed fact, so making it load-bearing adds a new fail-open surface (a node wrongly judged down gets contributes=0 and passes). (b) 'peer names ... already carry' understates the plumbing: CollectStreamState reads info.Cluster and throws the names away, so its return shape (StreamReplicaState) changes and all 7 production call sites plus the XferState seam
- **建议修法**：Use the nodeID that is already passed in. Subtract the retiring node's own contribution before comparing: pass a per-stream "does <nodeID> hold a Current, non-Offline replica of this stream" fact through StreamReplicaState (StreamInfo.Cluster.Leader + Cluster.Replicas[].Name already carry the peer names CollectStreamState reads), and gate on `Actual - contributes(nodeID) >= ReplicasFor(nv-1)`, with contributes defaulting to 1 when the peer identity cannot be resolved (fail-closed). That keeps #15 fixed — a dead voter is neither Current nor present, so contributes=0 and 2-0 >= 2 passes — while the healthy-node case correctly gives 2-1=1 < 2 and holds, exactly as before. If threading peer name

### G-2 · `internal/broker/retire_step_bounds_test.go:170-214`

- **lane**：cluster operations (batch G: #13/#14/#15/#16) — retire step bounding, post-removal readiness gate, drain phase ordering, alert-ack validation
- **裁决**：CONFIRMED（原报 MAJOR → 订正 MAJOR）
- **主张**：The guard for #15 is a guard that cannot fail: it exercises the pure helper `replicaReportAllAtLeast` directly and nothing anywhere asserts that `clusterStreamsReady` — the only production consumer — actually calls it, so reverting the fix leaves the entire suite green.
- **失败场景**：Revert exactly one line, clusterwrite.go:552, back to `return rep.AllAtTarget(), nil` (and drop the NumVoters read above it). The deadlock #15 exists to fix comes straight back — retiring a dead voter is refused on every 5-second tick forever. `replicaReportAllAtLeast` becomes referenced only from the test, which is legal Go (an unused *function* is not a compile error, and golangci's `unused` does not report a function that test code calls). TestRetireReadinessAsksAboutTheClusterAfterRemoval still passes all four table cases plus both fail-closed checks, because it never touches clusterStreamsReady. `make test` stays green, and so does every other gate. The one behaviour the finding was about is unprotected.
- **verifier 理由**：Empirically proven, not just argued. In a scratch copy of the working tree I reverted exactly the mutation described — clusterwrite.go:546-552 collapsed back to `return rep.AllAtTarget(), nil` — and ran the ENTIRE internal/broker package: `ok github.com/LinZiyang666/tether/internal/broker 353.880s`, exit 0. TestRetireReadinessAsksAboutTheClusterAfterRemoval passes, and so does every other test in the package. - **建议修法**：Assert against the production probe, not the helper. - **建议修法**：A real broker install cannot run unprivileged, but the dry-run path can be made to carry the assertion: give install.sh a `--etc-dir`/`--systemd-dir` override (the harness already overrides `TETHER_JOURNALD_ROOT` for exactly this reason in TestInstallShJournaldStaleDropinRemoved) and add the agent test's shape for the broker: write a sentinel into nats.d/nats.conf and tether-broker.service, re-run, assert both are byte-unchanged, assert `<file>.new` exists, assert the banner names them. ### G3 · `internal/broker/broker.go:398`

- **lane**：ARE THE NEW GUARDS REAL? (adversarial audit of every test function added/modified by the uncommitted prerelease-audit diff)
- **裁决**：CONFIRMED（原报 MAJOR → 订正 MAJOR）
- **主张**：BC-F2 (the `b.js` data race) was adjudicated with a specific design in the plan and is not implemented at all — `js` is still a plain field, and there is no guard.
- **失败场景**：`Run` assigns `b.js = js` at broker.go:1202, which the surrounding comment says happens deliberately AFTER all subscriptions are installed. Any NATS callback goroutine already dispatching calls `publishAudit`, which reads `b.js` at broker.go:753 with no synchronisation. Under `-race` this is a reportable race today; without it the reader can observe a torn/stale interface value and audit events silently take the core-publish fallback (lossy, no ACK) or fault.
- **verifier 理由**：Could not refute. internal/broker/broker.go:398 is `js jetstream.JetStream` — a bare field, sitting between `tunnelSrv atomic.Pointer[tunnel.Server]` (:389) and `manifestCache atomic.Pointer[manifestSnapshot]` (:422), whose comments both cite the same reason ('so the Run shutdown path … can't race with subscription callbacks still flushing through publishAudit'). The write at :1202 is unsynchronised and the surrounding comment confirms it is deliberately placed AFTER all subscriptions are installed ('try JetStream AFTER all subscriptions are installed so the probe round-trip doesn't head-of-line block test code that's already firing requests at us'), which is precisely what makes a concurrently-dispatching callback's read at :753 (`if b.js != nil`) a live race rather than a theoretical one. Plan §2 lists BC-F2 as MAJOR, plan §-1 A specifies the exact modification ('改用 `atomic.Pointer` +
- **对修法的批评**：Directionally right, with three cautions. (1) jetstream.JetStream is an INTERFACE, so `atomic.Pointer[jetstream.JetStream]` stores a *pointer to interface*: writers do `p.Store(&js)` and every reader becomes `if p := b.jsPtr.Load(); p != nil { (*p).Publish(...) }`. That is uglier than the nc/tunnelSrv precedent (both concrete structs) and is what the plan's own '或 holder struct' hedge is about; whichever shape is chosen, `atomic.Value` must NOT be used — it panics on inconsistent concrete types. (2) It is a sweep, not a one-liner: besides publishAudit at :753 the nil-ness of b.js is a PREDICATE at broker.go:582 (reaper gating), :1230 (clusterMode precondition with an actionable error) and :1287 (a status field), plus the read sites in admin.go, audit.go, transfer.go, transfer_reconcile.go and sessions.go. Each `b.js != nil` must keep identical semantics after the conversion or the change
- **建议修法**：Implement the adjudicated design: `js atomic.Pointer[jetstream.JetStream]` (or a small holder struct, since the field is an interface), with package-level free functions `brokerJS(b)` / `setBrokerJS(b, js)` so the structural-budget method-count ratchet is not disturbed — the same shape `liveConn(a, captured)` and `brokerUptime(b)` already use in this diff. Guard it the way the repo guards this class elsewhere: the `-race` matrix plus an explicit test that installs the subscriptions, fires a `publishAudit` from a handler goroutine, and assigns the JS handle concurrently. If the fix is being deliberately deferred, it must move to plan §6 with a reason, not stay silently listed as MAJOR work in

### G4 · `internal/agent/session_generation_test.go:128`

- **lane**：ARE THE NEW GUARDS REAL? (adversarial audit of every test function added/modified by the uncommitted prerelease-audit diff)
- **裁决**：CONFIRMED（原报 MAJOR → 订正 MAJOR）
- **主张**：TestDialFailureIsAttributedAcrossAMultiURLPool calls `attributeDialFailure` directly; nothing asserts it is wired into `connectNATS`.
- **失败场景**：A refactor of `connectNATS`'s error handling drops the `if aerr := attributeDialFailure(dial, err, connOpts); aerr != nil { err = aerr }` block (agent.go:1322). The helper becomes dead code, `internal/agent` stays green, and AC-F3 returns exactly as reported: with a multi-URL roster pool, nats.Connect reports ErrNoServers, `isAuthFailure` answers no, and a leased agent re-presents the refused name forever.
- **verifier 理由**：Could not refute. `attributeDialFailure` is called exactly once in production (internal/agent/agent.go:1322) and three times in tests, all direct calls in session_generation_test.go:133/147/150 with `opts=nil`. Both tests in auth_denial_classification_test.go and both connectNATS tests in connect_auth_lease_test.go use a SINGLE URL (`denyingNATS(t)` / `startDenyingNATS(t)`), and `attributeDialFailure` returns nil immediately when `len(urls) < 2` — so no test drives connectNATS through the attribution branch. I checked every other multi-URL site: roster_adopt_test.go / manifest_adopt_test.go / roster_boot_test.go assert on `effectiveDialURLs()` as a STRING and never dial it. So removing the call site leaves internal/agent green (the package vets clean on this tree, so their mutation run is plausible) and the helper becomes dead code with AC-F3 fully reopened. This is the lane's canonical
- **对修法的批评**：The proposed test's URL ORDER is backwards and would produce a vacuous test — the very defect this finding reports. connectNATS sets `nats.DontRandomize()` (agent.go:2150), so dial order is honoured, and nats.go v1.52's connect loop (nats.go:2789-2823) returns the LAST iteration's error after nulling out anything containing 'connection refused', falling back to ErrNoServers only when that leaves err==nil. With the proposed `"nats://127.0.0.1:1," + denyingNATS(t)` the dead server is tried first (refused → nulled) and the DENYING server last, so nats.Connect returns the authorization error directly; `attributeDialFailure` then short-circuits on `!errors.Is(poolErr, nats.ErrNoServers)` and returns nil, and connectNATS classifies the denial terminal WITHOUT the wiring. The test would pass against the un-wired build. The pool must be denying-FIRST, dead-LAST (`denyingNATS(t) + ",nats://127.0.
- **建议修法**：Add one behavioural case to auth_denial_classification_test.go: build an Agent whose `NATSURL` is `"nats://127.0.0.1:1," + denyingNATS(t)` (dead first so nats.go's last-seen error is the refused dial), leave `everAuthed` false, and assert `connectNATS` returns the terminal `auth_callout rejected` error rather than an ErrNoServers-shaped one. That fails against the un-wired build for the same reason the finding exists, and it costs one dial. The single-URL and non-ErrNoServers arms already in the unit test stay as they are.

### G6 · `internal/natsconf/takeover.go:122`

- **lane**：ARE THE NEW GUARDS REAL? (adversarial audit of every test function added/modified by the uncommitted prerelease-audit diff)
