- **verifier 理由**：Could not refute. The guard at internal/natsconf/takeover.go:122-126 is new and has no test. I checked every ClientListen mention in internal/natsconf/*_test.go: harvest_test.go:61, golden_merged_test.go:151/167/182/204, harvest_fallback_test.go:52/74, cutover_reconciler_agreement_test.go:61/107 and secrets_dir_identity_test.go:114 all assign `own.ClientListen()` (equal by construction); takeover_test.go:20 (sampleClusterConfig, used by jetstream_guard_test.go and the agreement shapes), render_config_test.go and reconcile_phase_fluidity_test.go all set a non-empty literal. The one test whose cfg leaves ClientListen empty — TestBuildMergedConfFailsClosedWithoutClusterTLS (harvest_test.go:74) — never reaches line 122, because the ClusterMTLS harvest at takeover.go:67-70 returns first, and it asserts only `err == nil` is a failure, so it could not distinguish the guards anyway. Deleting the
- **对修法的批评**：Sound, with one ordering hazard the proposal must design around and one it already handles. BuildMergedConf runs three fail-closed checks in sequence — ClusterMTLS harvest (:67), JetStream store_dir (:104), then ClientListen (:122) — so a naive fixture ('a live conf that binds 127.0.0.1:4222' plus a Config with ClientListen empty) will trip one of the first two and the test will pass for the wrong reason. The fixture must either carry cluster tls{} in its cluster{} block (or set cfg.Standalone / the CA/Cert/Key triple) and either omit jetstream or supply a resolvable JSStoreDir. The proposal already contains the thing that saves it: assert the error TEXT names the live bind, not merely that an error occurred — keep that, it is what stops this becoming another TestBuildMergedConfFailsClosedWithoutClusterTLS. The two negative controls are correctly chosen and are exactly what the guard's o
- **建议修法**：Add `client_listen_failclosed_test.go` mirroring js_store_failclosed_test.go: an `Ownership` harvested from a live conf that binds `127.0.0.1:4222`, a `Config` with `ClientListen: ""`, assert `BuildMergedConf` returns an error naming the live bind; then the two negative controls the guard's own comment promises — a live conf with no client bind plus an empty cfg must still SUCCEED (a routes-only or callout-only fixture must not be failed), and a matching non-empty cfg must succeed. Because the check sits in the one function all five assembly paths share, that single test covers the reconciler loop and the grow cutover as well.

### H-1 · `internal/cluster/restore_identity_test.go:226-249 (comment at :228-230)`

- **lane**：cluster FSM and locks (batch H: #40/#41/#42) — internal/cluster/{snapshot,fsm,node,offline,membership_ops,lock_lease}.go + restore_identity_test.go, membership_test.go
- **裁决**：CONFIRMED（原报 MAJOR → 订正 MAJOR）
- **主张**：TestBothStagingCorrectionsRideOneTransaction — the only test of the atomicity claim that is the whole point of the L3-F1 fix — passes identically against a non-transactional implementation, and its explanatory comment is factually wrong about where the failure occurs.
- **失败场景**：Someone rewrites prepareRestoreStaging as two bare db.Exec calls with no transaction and no rollback (the exact defect this test names: "a partially-corrected staging file can still be installed"). The chmod-0400 fixture makes the FIRST statement fail — sqlite cannot open the file read-write at all — so neither statement ever runs, self_node_id is still "leader-1", and the test is green. The staging file can then be left half-corrected (joiner's id stamped, source's restore_in_progress still present) and installed, and nothing in the suite notices.
- **verifier 理由**：Reproduced in an isolated copy of the tree, both directions. (a) The comment is false: with the 0400 fixture the real implementation errors at `cluster: stage self_node_id: attempt to write a readonly database (8)` — statement ONE, not COMMIT, so nothing 'ran' and there was nothing to roll back. (b) The test is vacuous: I rewrote prepareRestoreStaging as two bare db.Exec calls with no Begin/Rollback/Commit — the exact defect the failure message names — and TestBothStagingCorrectionsRideOneTransaction passed, as did TestTheStagingFileIsCorrectedBeforeItIsInstalled, TestAnUnknownIdentityLeavesTheSnapshotsOwnValue, TestRestoreCorrectsTheStagingFileBeforeInstallingIt, and the whole of `go test ./internal/cluster/` (90.4s, ok). So it is not merely 'the only test of the atomicity claim' — nothing anywhere in the owning package kills the mutant, and prepareRestoreStaging is unexported with no o
- **对修法的批评**：The fix works and is deterministic (verified above), and dropping the t.Skip is correct — but as written it can rot back into the same vacuity, so pin two more things. (1) Assert the error identifies the SECOND statement (e.g. `strings.Contains(err.Error(), "strip restore_in_progress")`). Today the fixture only discriminates because self_node_id is staged first; anyone who swaps the two Exec calls, or changes the strip from DELETE to an UPDATE/upsert, silently returns the fixture to 'statement 1 failed' and the test goes green against a non-transactional implementation again — exactly the state it is in now. (2) Fail loudly if CREATE TRIGGER itself did not take, so a sqlite driver change cannot quietly disarm the probe. No other cost: the change is test-only, the test function keeps its name (so the append-only test-inventory golden is untouched), and structural_budget_test.go skips *_te
- **建议修法**：Replace the chmod fixture with one that fails the SECOND statement while the first succeeds — arm `CREATE TRIGGER ... BEFORE DELETE ON cluster_meta WHEN OLD.key='restore_in_progress' BEGIN SELECT RAISE(ABORT,'staging rollback probe'); END` on the staged snapshot, then assert prepareRestoreStaging errors AND self_node_id is still the leader's. That is deterministic on every filesystem, so the t.Skip at :236-239 can go away too (it currently also skips whenever the test runs as root). Fix the comment at :228-230 to say which statement is being made to fail and why that is the shape the tx protects against.

### H-2 · `cmd/tether/cluster_upgrade_drive.go:31-35 and :226 (behaviour changed in internal/cluster/membership_ops.go:432-455)`

- **lane**：cluster FSM and locks (batch H: #40/#41/#42) — internal/cluster/{snapshot,fsm,node,offline,membership_ops,lock_lease}.go + restore_identity_test.go, membership_test.go
- **裁决**：CONFIRMED（原报 MAJOR → 订正 MAJOR）
- **主张**：The upgrade-lock guard change inverts the documented HALT recovery contract — a re-run of a halted roll is now refused — but the co-located doc comment and the operator-facing HALT message still promise the old self-heal, and the plan's adjudicated fix (an additive `cluster_upgrade_owner` key) was silently replaced by one that gives up self-retry.
- **失败场景**：A `cluster upgrade` rolls 2 of 4 brokers and HALTs (a converge timeout, a snapped SSH, Ctrl-C). Per the code's own design the marker is deliberately left held; `defer keeper.Stop()` stops renewing. The operator reads the printed message — "the cluster is left in a safe partial state; fix and re-run to resume" (:226) — fixes the cause and re-runs. plan.Upgrades() is still > 0, so driveUpgrade runs, and the acquire is now refused: handleUpgradeTrigger's new preflight `if upgradeActive(b.cl.node.RODB())` returns CodeBadRequest "another rolling upgrade already holds the cluster lock", the drive HALTs again, and it will keep HALTing for the full LockLeaseTTL (15 min) plus a reap interval. The same refusal blocks the emergency `--to-version <older>` rollback, which is the path an operator reaches for while the fleet is sitting mixed-version. The only fast out is `tether cluster unlock --upgrade --force` — cluster_unlock.go:214-220 refuses without --force while the lease is still in the future — and neither the refusal string nor the HALT message names it, so an operator following the printed guidance is stuck.
- **verifier 理由**：Every load-bearing fact checks out. Old guard was `NOT growMarkerExists()` alone (git show HEAD:internal/cluster/membership_ops.go:404) and markerAcquireStmts is INSERT-OR-IGNORE + UPDATE (lock_lease.go:176-182), so a re-run's UPDATE fired and the old read-back (`upgradeActive`, existence-only, cluster_health.go:251-255) returned true — the roll resumed. The new guard adds `AND NOT upgradeMarkerHeldByOther(valLit)` plus a hard preflight refusal at cluster_upgrade_trigger.go:208, and upgradeActive is existence-only, so it refuses even after the lease has expired, until the leader's 30s `upgrade-lock` reap pass (reconcile_passes.go:319) deletes the marker — i.e. up to LockLeaseTTL 15m + 30s. driveUpgrade acquires unconditionally at cluster_upgrade_drive.go:43-53 and turns a non-OK acquire into haltUpgrade, so a partial-roll re-run (including `--to-version <older>` rollback) HALTs again. Me
- **对修法的批评**：Part (1) is right and is the part to do; extend it to the two sites the finding missed — the NEW refusal string at cluster_upgrade_trigger.go:208-215 must stop naming `tether cluster upgrade release-lock` (no such command) and name `tether cluster unlock --upgrade [--force]`, and docs/cluster.md:312 carries the same stale 'idempotent UPSERT, 重跑重新取锁' promise in Chinese. Part (2) does NOT work as specified: IssuedAt is stamped fresh on every request (cluster_upgrade.go:400 `req.IssuedAt = now...`) and is replay-bounded to 5 minutes (cluster_upgrade_trigger.go:26,101-104), while LockLeaseTTL is 15 minutes — so a re-run necessarily presents a DIFFERENT IssuedAt and is still refused, and re-presenting the original is rejected by the replay guard before the acquire is even reached (and the drive is a stateless process with nowhere to persist it anyway). A workable owner token must be roll-scop
- **建议修法**：Two parts. (1) Sweep the contract, which is the part that is unambiguously wrong today: rewrite cluster_upgrade_drive.go:31-35 to state the new rule (a HALT leaves the lock held AND a re-run is refused until the lease expires or `cluster unlock` clears it), and change the haltUpgrade message at :226 from "fix and re-run to resume" to name the actual precondition ("...fix, then clear the roll lock — `tether cluster unlock --upgrade` once its lease expires, or --force if you can confirm this orchestrator is gone — before re-running"). Add the same to broker's refusal string in cluster_upgrade_trigger.go:208-210, and to docs/cluster-runbook.md 6.1, whose "usually you just wait" paragraph now un

### I-F1 · `internal/agent/exec.go:465-479`

- **lane**：agent exec, PTY and transfers (batch I: L3-F1 / L3-F2 / L3-F3 and push --force)
- **裁决**：CONFIRMED（原报 BLOCKER → 订正 MAJOR）
- **主张**：reapAbandonedExecChild's `startErr != nil` early return skips the ONLY abandon path that actually occurs, so the wedged child is neither killed nor reaped — and the wedge slot is now released immediately, disarming the too_many_wedged_spawns ceiling.
- **失败场景**：`tether exec` on a host with a hangable mount; spawnPolicy decision is Active so the child goes through spawnexec. `cmd.Start()` of the /proc/self/exe helper succeeds, the helper writes {Ready:true} on fd 4, then wedges inside `syscall.Exec` of the target on the dead mount. Handshake.Start is now blocked on its second `dec.Decode` waiting for the CLOEXEC EOF (helper.go:134). a.spawnTimeout() fires -> RunStartWithCleanup calls onAbandon -> `handshake.Cancel()` (exec.go:200) closes h.statusR -> the blocked Decode returns os.ErrClosed -> helper.go:142-146 sees `h.wasCanceled()` true, deliberately does NOT Wait, and returns `spawnexec: helper handshake: ...`. RunStartWithCleanup's goroutine then calls reapOnReturn(thatError) -> reapAbandonedExecChild returns on line 466 without doing anything, and `p.ReleaseSpawnSlot()` runs immediately after. Outcome: (a) the helper/target process stays ALIVE holding the mount it wedged on — the exact resource the abandon was supposed to reclaim, and the exact thing the fix's own comment says it now prevents; (b) it is never Wait()ed, so it becomes a permanent zombie when it eventually dies — a regression against the pre-existing `if cmd.Process != nil { _ = cmd.Wait() }` this patch replaced (review M4's fd/proc reclamation); (c) the wedge slot is freed while the wedged process still exists, so N successive wedged execs accumulate N live helpers without ever reaching ErrTooManyWedged — that ceiling is the protection added after the 5,789-process / ~307 GiB global-OOM incident (#81).
- **verifier 理由**：Mechanism verified end to end. exec.go:198-206 is the only startBounded call site and it is only taken when decision.Active, i.e. always through spawnexec.Handshake.Start — so the reviewer is right that the guard's stated justification ('a NON-nil startErr means Start itself failed, so os/exec has already cleaned up') describes exec.Cmd.Start, not the function actually passed. On abandon, onAbandon calls handshake.Cancel() (helper.go:152-160) which closes h.statusR; I ran a standalone probe (/tmp/pipecheck) confirming that closing an os.Pipe read end unblocks a parked json Decode with 'file already closed', so Start reaches helper.go:141-147, sees wasCanceled(), deliberately does NOT Wait, and returns a non-nil error. reapAbandonedExecChild then returns at line 466 having done nothing, and spawnsafe.go:1163-1167 releases the wedge slot immediately after. helper.go:155-156 states the oppo
- **对修法的批评**：The core of the fix is right and safe: I checked every path where Handshake.Start internally Waits (helper.go:117, 126, 144, 149) and each leaves cmd.ProcessState non-nil, so the surviving `cmd.ProcessState != nil` guard covers them and no double-Wait can occur; the happens-before argument for reading ProcessState (result delivered over spawnsafe's done channel) is sound. With Wait restored, ReleaseSpawnSlot already runs after it in spawnsafe.go:1163-1167, so the reviewer's 'separately decide whether the slot should be held' is moot — restoring the Wait restores exactly the behavior helper.go:156 promises. Two problems with the rest. (1) The proposed guard test is flaky by construction: the helper writes {Ready:true} and immediately execs, so the window in which Cancel can catch an outstanding handshake is sub-millisecond unless the target's execve actually wedges, which a hermetic test
- **建议修法**：Delete `startErr != nil` from the guard on exec.go:466. `cmd.ProcessState != nil` (line 469) already covers every path where Handshake.Start internally Waited (helper.go:117, 126, 144, 149) and is race-free for the reason the comment already gives, so kill+Wait unconditionally otherwise. Then rewrite the TWO GUARDS paragraph: the startErr==nil case is the rare one, the canceled non-nil case is the common one. Guard test: drive the real path — spawnexec.Prepare a target, Start it in a goroutine, Cancel() while the handshake is outstanding, feed the returned non-nil error to reapAbandonedExecChild, and assert `syscall.Kill(helperPid, 0) == ESRCH` inside the same time budget the existing test u

### I-F2 · `internal/agent/run.go:291`

- **lane**：agent exec, PTY and transfers (batch I: L3-F1 / L3-F2 / L3-F3 and push --force)
- **裁决**：CONFIRMED（原报 MAJOR → 订正 MAJOR）
- **主张**：The comment "pty.<pid>.out is a publisher (master→bus) and resolves its conn per flush" is false — pumpMasterToBus publishes on the conn captured at spawn — so L3-F2 fixed the input direction and left the output direction, and 4 of 5 replyRunChunk sites, on the dead conn.
- **失败场景**：Operator is inside `tether run -- bash` on a node. An ordinary drain or roster change triggers rebuildOntoVoter, which closes the session conn; Run re-dials and stores a new conn in a.ncBox. The PTY run deliberately survives (that is the design choice recorded at run.go:293-295, and restampProcKA at run.go:954 forgives the rebuild window so the liveness reaper does not hang it up). handleRunForwarded is a detached goroutine still blocked in sess.Wait(), and its pump goroutine keeps calling `nc.Publish(outSubj, out)` on the CLOSED conn (run.go:407), whose error is discarded. Every byte the child writes from the rebuild onward is silently dropped. Keystrokes now arrive (the fix works), so the operator types into a terminal that answers nothing — the exact inverse of the failure the comment describes, and equally silent. The same applies to `pubPtyFailed` and to the started/ready/failed chunks, which go through replyRunChunk with the raw captured nc.
- **verifier 理由**：The comment at run.go:291 is false as written. pumpMasterToBus (run.go:376-418) publishes with `_ = nc.Publish(outSubj, out)` and `nc.FlushTimeout(...)` on its parameter, and the call site at run.go:300-303 passes the captured spawn conn; `git diff -- internal/agent/run.go` shows the function was not touched by this batch, so the sentence 'resolves its conn per flush' was untrue the moment it was written. The conn really is dead after a rebuild: agent.go:1066-1081 closes over nc in the subFwd callback, exec.go:59-62 dispatches `go a.handleRunForwarded(nc, msg)` as a detached goroutine, rebuildOntoVoter (roster.go:479-506) drives the session finalizer whose teardown calls nc.Close() (conn_teardown.go:227), and the run is deliberately kept alive across it (run.go:293-295 rejects the Hangup alternative; run.go:760-766 restamps the ctl-liveness deadline while the conn is down). replyRunChunk
- **对修法的批评**：Direction is right and the ACL does not block it: the agent's Pub template still grants `_INBOX.>` (permissions.go), which is what this batch's new InboxPrefixFor scheme was explicitly shaped to preserve, so publishing a reply-inbox chunk from the rebuilt conn works — exec's replyChunk already relies on this. Four caveats. (1) Do not delete the sentence at run.go:291, correct it, and carry over exec's honest disclaimer: per-flush resolution means the stream RESUMES, not that it is complete — whatever the old conn had buffered is gone, so a hole at the switchover remains. Claiming a fix without that caveat repeats the defect being fixed. (2) liveConn's fallback to the captured conn must be kept for the pump too, or in-process tests that never populate ncBox publish on nil. (3) The proposed e2e (rebuild mid-run, write .in, assert echo on .out) is the right discriminator but needs a multi-v
- **建议修法**：Make replyRunChunk resolve `nc = liveConn(a, nc)` at its head, exactly as replyChunk does, and have pumpMasterToBus's flush closure call `liveConn(a, nc).Publish(outSubj, out)` (and the matching FlushTimeout) so the comment at :291 becomes true rather than aspirational. Then add the test L3-F2 never got: an e2e that starts a PTY run, forces a session rebuild, writes a byte to `.in` and asserts it is echoed back on `.out` after the rebuild — one test that discriminates both directions, where the current source grep discriminates neither.

### I-F3 · `cmd/tether/transfer.go:569-604`

- **lane**：agent exec, PTY and transfers (batch I: L3-F1 / L3-F2 / L3-F3 and push --force)
- **裁决**：CONFIRMED（原报 MAJOR → 订正 MAJOR）
- **主张**：The permission-preservation fix landed only on the tier-A inline pull path; the tier-B path (every file over 8 MiB) still opens its tmp at 0600 and renames it over the destination, so `pull --force` still resets the mode.
- **失败场景**：A 50 MB `deploy.sh` sits at ~/bin/deploy.sh mode 0755 on the ctl host. Operator runs `tether pull node:/srv/deploy.sh ~/bin/deploy.sh --force` to refresh it. Size > XferTierAMaxBytes (8 MiB, internal/proto/xfer.go:43) so the transfer goes tier B: cmd/tether/transfer.go:570 opens `localAbs + ".tmp." + transferID` at 0o600, streams, and commits with `commitLocalTemp(tmp, localAbs, force)` at :600, which is a bare `os.Rename` under --force. No Lstat, no Chmod anywhere on this path. The file comes back mode 0600 and the next `./deploy.sh` is Permission denied — the precise defect L3-F1 is written up as fixing, on the file sizes where a pull is most likely in the first place.
- **verifier 理由**：Verified by call graph: `grep writeLocalAtomic cmd/tether/*.go` yields exactly one production caller, transfer.go:519, inside finishPullTierA; the whole diff to cmd/tether/transfer.go is the 16-line Lstat+Chmod block inside writeLocalAtomic. finishPullTierB (transfer.go:569-604) hand-rolls its own os.OpenFile(tmp, ..., 0o600), streams with io.Copy, and commits via commitLocalTemp, which under --force is a bare os.Rename — no Lstat, no Chmod anywhere on that path. XferTierAMaxBytes is 8 MiB (internal/proto/xfer.go:43), so every pull above 8 MiB still resets the destination to 0600. The agent side genuinely lacks this asymmetry: both call sites (transfer.go:187 tier A, transfer.go:327 tier B) go through OpenForWriteAtomic. Not an adjudicated scope decision either — docs/reviews/prerelease-audit-plan.md scopes this fix as '`push --force` 丢权限位' in batch I (internal/agent/*), and the ctl-side
- **对修法的批评**：'Simply repeat the Lstat+Chmod in the tier-B branch' is the wrong shape and would create a worse bug than it fixes: on tier B the chmod would land on a tmp that is then streamed for minutes, i.e. exactly the exposure I-F4 raises, and far wider on the ctl side than on the agent side where the reviewer noticed it. Fix the two together: capture the destination mode at open, apply it after f.Sync() and BEFORE f.Close() (the ctl path still holds the fd there, unlike the agent path — see the I-F4 critique), so the tmp is 0600 for the whole transfer. If you factor the shared helper, keep tier B's tmp NAME as `localAbs + ".tmp." + transferID` — it is used in the os.Remove cleanup on every failure branch and in operator-facing error text — and keep commitLocalTemp's no-force link semantics, which are what makes the mode work reachable only on --force. The proposed 2x3 table test is right, and it
- **建议修法**：Factor one `openLocalTempPreservingMode(localAbs string) (*os.File, string, error)` used by both ctl paths, or simply repeat the Lstat+Chmod in the tier-B branch. Cover it with a table test over {tier A inline, tier B streamed} x {destination absent, destination 0755, destination 0644} asserting the resulting mode — a single-tier test would have passed against this bug.

### J1 · `cmd/tether/main.go:121-146`

- **lane**：ctl exit classes and CLI surface (batch J)
- **裁决**：CONFIRMED（原报 MAJOR → 订正 MAJOR）
- **主张**：`tether agent join <invite> --start` IS the long-running daemon, but the new agentNonDaemonSubcommands exclusion classifies it as a subcommand, so agent.BootUpgradeCheck no longer runs on that launch shape — every `node upgrade` against such an agent is now structurally unable to commit and gets force-rolled-back at the register deadline.
- **失败场景**：An agent is onboarded the documented C2 way and left running: `nohup tether agent join '<invite>' --nid agt1 --pin <pin> --start &` (exactly test/simcluster/drills/82-agent-onboarding-invite.sh:160, and the path image/provision-node.sh:74 points at). Operator runs `tether node upgrade agt1 --url ... --sha256 ...`. The agent stages the binary, writes a PENDING marker (BootCount=0), and reExecInPlace (internal/agent/upgrade.go:301-314) re-execs with UNCHANGED os.Args — still `tether agent join <invite> ... --start`. In the new image main() calls isAgentDaemonInvocation(os.Args): args[2]=="join" is in the table, so it returns false and BootUpgradeCheck is skipped. BootCount therefore stays 0 and upgradeBootProof stays "", so agent.New gets bootProofID=="" (internal/agent/agent.go:746-763). commitUpgradeAfterRegister (internal/agent/upgrade_state.go:763) and upgradeRegisterReport (:708) both bail on `m.BootCount == 0 || a.upgradeBootProofID != m.UpgradeID`, so the healthy new binary can never report or commit. armUpgradeWatchdog still arms (instanceID survives the exec via execEnv, so markerTargetsThisAgent is true), and at upgradeRegisterDeadline watchdogRollback -> executeRollback restores the PREV binary and execs it. Net: a perfectly healthy upgrade is rolled back every time, and `tether node upgrade` reports it unconfirmed. Before this change isAgentDaemonInvocation returned true for that argv and the whole state machine worked.
- **verifier 理由**：Reproduced the predicate directly: isAgentDaemonInvocation(["tether","agent","join","invite://x","--nid","agt1","--pin","p","--start"]) = false, while root.Find(["agent","join","invite://x","--start"]) resolves to `tether agent join` -> agent_join.go:100 -> runJoinedAgent -> agent.New + ag.Run(ctx), i.e. the long-running daemon. Every link in the chain checks out: internal/agent/upgrade_state.go:449 (m.BootCount++ inside decideBoot) is the ONLY writer of BootCount and it is reached only from bootUpgradeCheck, which main.go:77-78 gates on this predicate; upgrade_state.go:708 and :763 both require BootCount!=0 AND upgradeBootProofID==m.UpgradeID; reExecInPlace (upgrade.go:301-314) freezes os.Args, so the post-upgrade image relaunches with the same `agent join ... --start` argv; markerTargetsThisAgent (:555-588) still returns true because TargetInstance==a.instanceID survives via execEnv/mi
- **对修法的批评**：The direction is right and it is the only direction that works: making BootUpgradeCheck argv-aware for `join` keeps it pre-Cobra, which external review F2 requires (moving the check into runJoinedAgent would leave a staged binary that dies during parse with no budget tick and no supervisor on the nohup path). Three things the fix must handle that the proposal does not spell out: (1) the reconciliation loop in the new test (exit_class_family_test.go:148) errors on ANY agent subcommand missing from agentNonDaemonSubcommands, so the alternative 'move join out of the flat table into the switch' also requires adding an explicit daemon-like list to that test — otherwise the guard turns red on join itself; (2) the shim would now depend on the literal spelling `--start`, which nothing pins — add a tree-walk assertion that the `join` command really has a bool flag named `start` (same style as the
- **建议修法**：Make the exclusion argv-aware for `join`: exclude `tether agent join …` ONLY when no `--start` (or `--start=true`) appears in args[3:], mirroring the existing bool-flag handling in the loop below (`--start=false` => still excluded). Equivalently, move `join` out of the flat table into the same switch that already handles `--help`/`--install-user-service`. Then change exit_class_family_test.go to assert BOTH directions: `agent join <inv>` is not the daemon, `agent join <inv> --start` IS.

### K-F1 · `internal/clusterupgrade/plan.go:67`

- **lane**：deploy and release surface (batch K): scripts/install.sh, internal/proto.SameRelease and its call sites, cmd/tether/node.go, cmd/tether/cluster_upgrade_drive.go, internal/clusterupgrade/plan.go, internal/broker/reexec.go, docs/broker-ops.md, docs/usage.md, test/p10/config_preservation_test.go
- **裁决**：CONFIRMED（原报 MAJOR → 订正 MAJOR）
- **主张**：AtTarget got SameRelease on its broker leg and NOT on its agent leg one line below, so every host that runs a co-located agent is now permanently "not at target" for a v-prefixed --to-version.
- **失败场景**：Fleet is entirely on released v0.5.2 (goreleaser stamps ReleaseVersion=0.5.2, no "v" — build/goreleaser.yaml:40 uses {{ .Version }}). Operator runs `tether cluster upgrade --to-version v0.5.2` exactly as the command's own Example at cmd/tether/cluster_upgrade.go:45 shows — the documented idempotent re-run after a HALT, or a routine no-op check. plan.go:61 now accepts BrokerVer "0.5.2" against target "v0.5.2", but line 67 evaluates AgentVer "0.5.2" == "v0.5.2" → false, so AtTarget is false for every agent-bearing host. Compute emits StepUpgrade for all of them instead of StepSkip: the roll takes the cluster-wide upgrade lock, transfers leadership off the leader, and (with K-F3) re-execs the co-located agent on every broker host — a full rolling restart of a cluster that needed nothing. Before the fix both legs failed and the roll HALTed loudly; the half-fix converts a loud refusal into a silent quorum-touching restart.
- **verifier 理由**：Verified verbatim: internal/clusterupgrade/plan.go:61 is `!proto.SameRelease(n.BrokerVer, target)` while :67 is still `return n.AgentVer == target`. I reproduced the reviewer's probe exactly with an overlay-added test (repo untouched): Node{BrokerVer:"0.5.2",AgentVer:"0.5.2",Agent:AgentPresent} gives AtTarget("v0.5.2")=false, AtTarget("0.5.2")=true, and Compute over two such voters returns Upgrades=2, N2WriteFence=true, steps `upgrade b2` + `upgrade b1 transferTo=b2`. Reachability is real and is the documented path: build/goreleaser.yaml:40 stamps ReleaseVersion={{ .Version }} (no v) while cmd/tether/cluster_upgrade.go:46 Example types `--to-version v0.5.0`, and I confirmed cluster_upgrade.go never normalises toVersion before Compute. Not a documented decision: docs/reviews/prerelease-audit-plan.md §4 batch K says '替换 5 处逐字比较' and nothing adjudicates leaving the agent leg on ==. One corr
- **对修法的批评**：`return proto.SameRelease(n.AgentVer, target)` is the correct minimal fix and is safe for the AgentVer=="" case (SameRelease("", non-empty) is false, so state (b) still fails loudly). Two objections to the surrounding plan: (1) the proposed test row belongs in plan_test.go, not agentless_test.go — that file's subject is the AgentAbsent path, and CLAUDE.md §3 step 5b requires test files named by the unit under test; (2) fixing :67 alone does NOT subsume K-F3: an at-target host becomes StepSkip so the executor never reaches drive.go:107, but a host that genuinely needs a broker reload while its agent is already at target still gets a needless reexec-agent. Both lines must change in the same edit.
- **建议修法**：`return proto.SameRelease(n.AgentVer, target)` at plan.go:67. Add a table row to internal/clusterupgrade/agentless_test.go with target "v2" and BrokerVer/AgentVer "2" asserting AtTarget==true, and a Compute case asserting Upgrades()==0 for an already-at-target agent-bearing cluster (mutation-verify by reverting line 67 to == and confirming red).

### K-F2 · `internal/proto/transfer_test.go:244`

- **lane**：deploy and release surface (batch K): scripts/install.sh, internal/proto.SameRelease and its call sites, cmd/tether/node.go, cmd/tether/cluster_upgrade_drive.go, internal/clusterupgrade/plan.go, internal/broker/reexec.go, docs/broker-ops.md, docs/usage.md, test/p10/config_preservation_test.go
- **裁决**：CONFIRMED（原报 MAJOR → 订正 MAJOR）

- **失败场景**：I copied the tree, changed all six wirings back to the pre-fix operators — cluster_upgrade_drive.go:271 and :313, node.go:437 and :481, broker/reexec.go:55, clusterupgrade/plan.go:61 — leaving proto.SameRelease and TestSameReleaseIgnoresOnlyTheVPrefix untouched, and ran the four packages that own those sites. internal/clusterupgrade PASS (0.004s), internal/proto PASS (0.947s), cmd/tether PASS (81.6s), internal/broker PASS (356.2s). Nothing anywhere asserts the helper is WIRED into any production path. This is exactly why K-F1, K-F3 and K-F6 were missed: there is no test that could have noticed the four siblings were skipped, and there is none that would notice if a future refactor dropped a wiring.
- **verifier 理由**：I reproduced the mutation independently rather than taking the reviewer's word. Using `go test -overlay` (no repo writes) I reverted all six wirings to the pre-fix operators — cluster_upgrade_drive.go:271 and :313, node.go:437 and :481, broker/reexec.go:55, clusterupgrade/plan.go:61 — leaving proto.SameRelease and its unit test intact: internal/proto PASS, internal/clusterupgrade PASS, internal/broker (-run 'ReExec|Reexec|Reload|Upgrade') PASS, cmd/tether PASS except TestAgentBootShimConsumesBudgetOnPreRunEFailures, which I proved is a pre-existing failure of the current half-edited working tree (it shells out to a real `go build`, which fails on unrelated missing imports in internal/brokermetrics and internal/clusteroffline) and not a mutation kill. Corroborating static evidence: every version literal in internal/clusterupgrade/*_test.go is "v1"/"v2" on both sides (agentless_test.go:17
- **对修法的批评**：Proposed test (c) is wrong as written and would fail: proto.ReleaseVersion defaults to "v0.0.0-dev" (internal/proto/version.go:38), so `ToVersion: "v"+proto.ReleaseVersion` = "vv0.0.0-dev", and SameRelease uses strings.TrimPrefix which strips only ONE leading "v" — "0.0.0-dev" vs "v0.0.0-dev" compares false and the AlreadyAtVersion assertion fails. The correct construction is `ToVersion: strings.TrimPrefix(proto.ReleaseVersion, "v")`. Item (a) is subsumed once K-F1 is fixed. The four new tests must be named by unit and must not become `*_round<N>_*` / `p<N>_*` files (CLAUDE.md §3 step 5b, enforced by test/determinism/test_naming_test.go), and each new test function is appended to the only-grows golden in test/determinism/test_inventory_test.go.

Mutation-verify each by reverting its site to ==.

- **对修法的批评**：`ok && proto.SameRelease(av, toVersion)` is correct. ### L-F1 · `internal/broker/clusterwrite.go:1234`

- **lane**：Batch E / B5 — the cross-session process-exit fence (internal/proc/proc.go, internal/proc/plan.go, internal/broker/{exec,clusterwrite,cluster_forward}.go, internal/proc/exit_session_fence_test.go)
- **裁决**：CONFIRMED（原报 MAJOR → 订正 MAJOR）
- **主张**：The forwarded half of the fence has NO guard: deleting `Sid: sid` from the marshalled ProcMarkExitedPayload silently reopens the cross-session escape on the dominant cluster path, and every gate stays green.
- **失败场景**：In cluster mode the proc-exit subject is QUEUE-subscribed (broker.go:1109 + the queue-group branch at broker.go:1167 — isBroadcastClusterSubject does not match it), so on an N-broker cluster roughly (N-1)/N of every agent exit is handled by a FOLLOWER and reaches the leader only through this payload. If Sid is ever dropped from the marshal — a revert, a merge, a refactor that rebuilds the literal — the leader's PlanMarkExited sees sid=="" and takes the documented N-1 hatch, rendering the pre-fence unscoped `WHERE pid=? AND status='RUNNING'`. An agent in session A publishing on its own legal subject `tether.v2.s.A.ev.node.A-1.proc.<B's pid>.exit` then closes session B's RUNNING row with an rc of its choosing; B's next register finds no RUNNING row for a live process, G.1 classifies it as an orphan, and the agent SIGTERM+SIGKILLs the operator's job. The entire BLOCKER is back, in cluster deployments only, with `make test` / `make gates` / `make e2e-parallel` all green.
- **verifier 理由**：I tried to refute this four ways and failed. (1) Mechanism is real: proposeOrForward (cluster_forward.go:874-900) only marshals the payload on the FOLLOWER branch — the leader branch runs the closure, which captures sid directly — and `.proc.*.exit` is queue-subscribed in cluster mode (broker.go:1109 in the table, the QueueSubscribe branch at ~1167; isBroadcastClusterSubject at clusterwrite.go:60-80 lists only push/pull/transfer/expose/run/exec/kill/expose-rm/proxy leaves, so proc.exit is NOT broadcast). So the forwarded marshal is the only carrier of sid for every exit a follower handles. (2) Coverage: grep for VerbProcMarkExited/ProcMarkExitedPayload repo-wide returns only clusterwrite.go, cluster_forward.go, and two NAME ledgers. wire_freeze_test.go derives keys by reflection over struct tags (jsonKeys/payloadSpecimens, lines 269-301, 601-625) — it proves the FIELD exists, never that
- **对修法的批评**：The first half is right in intent but not buildable in the shape proposed. 'Construct a Broker with clusterMode=true and a captured forwarder, call markProcExited' does not reach the marshal: every in-package cluster harness (d7SingleNode, used at events_test.go:80-90, readyz_test.go:47, upgrade_trigger_test.go:219, …) is a single-node raft, i.e. always LEADER, so proposeOrForward takes the Propose branch and the payload is never built; and b.cl.node must be non-nil or IsLeader() nil-derefs (cluster/node.go:511). Two workable shapes instead: (a) an AST gate in internal/broker in the style write_verbs_test.go already uses — parse clusterwrite.go, find the ProcMarkExitedPayload composite literal in markProcExited, require a Sid key bound to the function's sid param (cheap, <1s, no implementation change); or (b) the real end-to-end guard in test/d4, which already owns a 2-node cluster + fol
- **建议修法**：Add a wiring assertion in internal/broker: construct a Broker with clusterMode=true and a captured forwarder (or assert on the bytes json.Marshal produces at that site), call markProcExited(pid, sid, ...) and require the decoded ProcMarkExitedPayload.Sid == sid, with a failure message naming the N-1 hatch. Stronger and cheaper to keep honest: stop letting an omitted field mean 'N-1 peer'. Make the hatch explicit — e.g. PlanMarkExited(db, pid, sid, ...) fails closed on an empty sid exactly like proc.MarkExited, and the forward decoder converts a legacy payload (a payload whose raw JSON has no "sid" key) into an explicit `PlanMarkExitedUnscoped` call. Then a forgotten field is a loud error rat

### A-F4 · `internal/authcallout/ratelimit_test.go:117`

- **lane**：_INBOX authorization surface (batch A) — internal/auth/permissions.go, internal/authcallout/handler.go, internal/cli/natsconn.go, internal/agent/agent.go (CustomInboxPrefix), cmd/tether/alert_gate.go, test/d3/inbox_isolation_acl_test.go
- **裁决**：CONFIRMED（原报 MINOR → 订正 MINOR）
- **主张**：Three existing per-IP throttle guards are now satisfied by the NEW global bucket regardless of the per-IP bucket, and the file's own mutation-verification note is now false in the form it states.
- **失败场景**：Delete the per-IP half of pinRateLimited (`return p.entry(ip, now).TokensAt(now) < 1` → `return false`) and rerun: TestPINRateLimitAgentRole, TestPINRateLimitBehindProxy and TestCodexReviewPINBudgetIsPerBrokerByDesign part (a) all still PASS. Each does exactly 10 wrong-PIN attempts on a FROZEN clock; each attempt now calls spendPINBudget, so after 10 the process-wide bucket (burst 10, no refill) is at zero and the 11th is refused by globalBlocked alone. The assertion `want rate_limited` is satisfied by both behaviours, which is the failure mode the repo has been burned by repeatedly.
- **verifier 理由**：Verified by actually running the mutation. In a scratch copy I replaced the per-IP half of `pinRateLimiter.blocked` with `return false` and ran the three named tests: TestCodexReviewPINBudgetIsPerBrokerByDesign PASS, TestPINRateLimitAgentRole PASS, TestPINRateLimitBehindProxy PASS. The arithmetic is exactly as described — frozen clock, precisely 10 wrong attempts, each spending one global token before argon2, so the global bucket (burst 10, no refill) is at zero and the 11th assertion `want rate_limited` is satisfied by globalBlocked alone. Part (b) of the Codex test also survives the mutation (each handler has its own limiter, 5 tokens left, so the further wrong attempt still denies with "invalid PIN"). The reviewer's own caveat is also correct and I confirmed it: running the FULL package under the mutation goes red on TestPINRateLimitSingleIPHighFrequency (`11th correct-PIN connect fro
- **对修法的批评**：The fix is correct and safe — pinLimiterFor() is lazy (`h.limiterOnce.Do`), so calling relaxGlobalPINBudget(t) after freshHandler(t) does take effect, which is why the existing three relaxed tests work. Two things to add. First, relaxGlobalPINBudget mutates PACKAGE-LEVEL vars with only t.Cleanup for restoration; spreading it to three more tests deepens a hazard that will bite the first time anyone marks these t.Parallel() — worth a one-line note on the helper (or making the ceiling a Handler field instead of a package var, which is the structurally right answer and costs about the same). Second, the correction to the file-header mutation note should name BOTH mutations explicitly, and per feedback-mutation-verify-every-new-guard both must actually be run once — I have now run the per-IP one and recorded the result above; the global mutation (make spendGlobal a no-op and confirm TestPINGl
- **建议修法**：Add relaxGlobalPINBudget(t) to TestPINRateLimitAgentRole, TestPINRateLimitBehindProxy and TestCodexReviewPINBudgetIsPerBrokerByDesign, so each measures the identity ceiling alone, and correct the header mutation note to name the two ceilings separately (per-IP mutation → those tests red; global mutation → TestPINGlobalBudgetBoundsArgon2RegardlessOfSourceAddress red). Then actually run both mutations once, per feedback-mutation-verify-every-new-guard.

### A-F5 · `internal/auth/permissions.go:40`

- **lane**：_INBOX authorization surface (batch A) — internal/auth/permissions.go, internal/authcallout/handler.go, internal/cli/natsconn.go, internal/agent/agent.go (CustomInboxPrefix), cmd/tether/alert_gate.go, test/d3/inbox_isolation_acl_test.go
- **裁决**：CONFIRMED（原报 MINOR → 订正 MINOR）
- **主张**：The isolation unit is the NKEY, but the cloned-credential lease feature deliberately runs many machines on ONE nkey, so those instances share a single inbox subtree — contradicting the comment that a connection's reply lands "somewhere no other connection may subscribe to".
- **失败场景**：An operator bakes one image containing one agent credential and boots N clones (the shipped lease feature: handler.go's SUFFIX FALLBACK arm states "the credential is bound to the BASENAME, one row for the whole clone family"). All N connections compute the same InboxPrefixFor(pubkey). Today this is masked because agents also hold bare `_INBOX.>`. Once A-F1 is fixed and the wildcard is removed, clone A can `Subscribe("<sharedprefix>.>")` and receive clone B's register reply — ProxyDirective{Token, Keys[].Secret} — i.e. the exact payload the fix exists to protect, leaked between physically distinct machines that the operator believes are isolated.
- **verifier 理由**：The factual basis holds. auth.InboxPrefixFor takes only the actor nkey (permissions.go:41: `sha256.Sum256([]byte(actor))`), and the lease feature's own commit message (1e9d32a) states the premise plainly: "An agent baked into a VM/container image and launched many times presented one credential and one configured nid". handler.go's SUFFIX FALLBACK arm confirms the binding is per-BASENAME for the whole clone family and is honoured only for the fingerprint that already owns it — i.e. all clones present the same nkey, hence the same derived subtree. The two comments the reviewer names do overclaim: permissions.go:11-13 calls it "this connection's PRIVATE reply-inbox prefix" and :306-311 says the register reply is "delivered somewhere no other connection may subscribe to", which is false for a clone family (and, per A-F1, false for everyone today). MINOR is right — it is currently masked by
- **对修法的批评**：The FIRST option — derive the prefix from (nkey, nid) for agents — is worse than the problem and should be rejected. Two reasons. (1) It does not actually separate clones at the moment that matters: on a clone's FIRST connect there is no lease yet, so `nidOf(a)` is the configured basename for every instance in the family (agent.go builds the CONNECT name from nidOf(a)); all clones would still share one subtree for exactly the contested register whose reply carries the ProxyDirective. Only the post-adjudication reconnect under `<base>-NN` would be separated. (2) Changing the derivation is a LOCKSTEP client/broker change with no compatibility window — an agent computing sha256(actor+nid) against a broker granting sha256(actor) (or the reverse, during a rollback) loses every reply, which is the same N-1 trap the whole batch is trying to avoid and is strictly harder than A-F1's, since the br
- **建议修法**：Either derive the prefix from (nkey, nid) for agents — auth_callout already knows nid at mint time, and the agent already knows its own effective nid (nidOf(a)) when it builds conn options, so both sides can compute `sha256(actor + "\x00" + nid)` with no new wire field — or state plainly in the InboxPrefixFor doc that the isolation unit is the CREDENTIAL, and that a cloned-credential family is one principal by construction, so the fix does not separate clones. The second is acceptable only if it is written down; today the comment says the opposite.

### A-F6 · `docs/requirements.md:308`

- **lane**：_INBOX authorization surface (batch A) — internal/auth/permissions.go, internal/authcallout/handler.go, internal/cli/natsconn.go, internal/agent/agent.go (CustomInboxPrefix), cmd/tether/alert_gate.go, test/d3/inbox_isolation_acl_test.go
- **裁决**：PARTIAL（原报 MINOR → 订正 MINOR）
- **主张**：The one deliberate N-1 compatibility break this release ships (old ctl cannot use the unactivated path against a new broker) was never written into requirements §6.7 as the plan required, and produces no diagnostic.
- **失败场景**：Rolling upgrade, broker first (the mandated order). An operator still on the previous release runs `tether session ls`, `tether session create` or `tether login`: the ctl sets no CustomInboxPrefix, the new PermissionsForUnactivated grants only `_INBOX.<hash>.>`, its response-mux subscription is refused and the command dies with a bare `nats: timeout`. Nothing in requirements.md, usage.md or the error hints says an upgrade is required, so the operator reads it as "broker is down" and may roll the broker back — the opposite of the correct action. The only record of the decision is docs/reviews/prerelease-audit-plan.md:111, a process artifact that CLAUDE.md §3 step 7 requires to be archived under docs/reviews/.
- **verifier 理由**：The documentation half is CONFIRMED and mechanically verifiable: `git diff --stat -- docs/requirements.md` is empty, §6.7 (line 308ff) still carries exactly one explicit exemption (the cluster-join release-equality gate), and the plan at line 111 mandates "这是唯一的 N-1 例外 ... 写进 §6.7". docs/usage.md's upgrade section gained only the agent.yaml/install.sh note, and cmd/tether/error_hints.go adds nothing for this symptom. The break itself is real — session.go:43/103 and login.go:51 all connect with cli.CtlNameUnactivated, whose template no longer grants a bare inbox. But the DIAGNOSTIC half is REFUTED. The N-1 peer is v0.5.1 (`git tag --sort=-v:refname`), and `git tag --contains` shows the ctl's async-error handler (cmd/tether/ctl_connect.go:58, added in 71e4943) is present in BOTH v0.5.0 and v0.5.1. My A-F2 repro confirms the server does deliver this exact failure to the ErrorHandler: `nats:
- **对修法的批评**：Adding the exemption to §6.7 and a usage.md §9 row is correct and should be done. Two corrections. First, SCOPE: if A-F1 is fixed as adjudicated (member wildcard removed by default), the exemption is much wider than "session create/list, login, and shell completion" — it covers every activated-member verb an old ctl runs (exec, run, ps, expose, push/pull, history, alert ack). Writing the narrow version now produces a §6.7 clause that is wrong the moment A-F1 lands; write it once, scoped to "any ctl request/reply against an upgraded broker". Second, the proposed hint site is the wrong one: the CONNECT SUCCEEDS — the failure is a subscription permissions violation on the response multiplexer, surfaced asynchronously and then as a request timeout — so a hint on cmd/tether's connect-error path (connectError) can never fire. The correct hook already exists: connectCtlOpts' nats.ErrorHandler,
- **建议修法**：Add the exemption to requirements.md §6.7 alongside the cluster-join one, naming the affected verbs (session create/list, login, and shell completion) and the required action (upgrade the ctl). Add a row to docs/usage.md §9's error table. Optionally give the operator a distinguishable signal: because the ctl sets the prefix and the broker derives it, a NEW broker can detect an old ctl only by its lack of a scoped subscription — the cheap alternative is a hint in cmd/tether's connect-error path saying "if the broker was upgraded, upgrade this ctl".

### A-F7 · `cmd/tether/alert_gate.go:51`

- **lane**：_INBOX authorization surface (batch A) — internal/auth/permissions.go, internal/authcallout/handler.go, internal/cli/natsconn.go, internal/agent/agent.go (CustomInboxPrefix), cmd/tether/alert_gate.go, test/d3/inbox_isolation_acl_test.go
- **裁决**：CONFIRMED（原报 MINOR → 订正 MINOR）
- **主张**：The comment justifying the nats.NewInbox → nc.NewInbox change is false for every connection that actually calls the function, and no test would fail if the change were reverted.
- **失败场景**：A maintainer reads lines 51-56 ("the package-level helper mints `_INBOX.<nuid>`, which this connection is no longer granted — the SubscribeSync below would fail and probeClusterHealth would silently return nil, i.e. the destructive-operation gate would stop gating"), believes the gate is protected by that grant, and reasons about a future ACL change from a false premise. In fact probeClusterHealth is only ever reached from gateDestructive, whose seven call sites (expose.go:84/320, session.go:171, run.go:93, transfer.go:167/446) all run on an ACTIVATED-member connection, and PermissionsForActivatedMember still grants bare `_INBOX.>` (A-F1). Reverting line 57 to nats.NewInbox() therefore changes nothing observable and every gate stays green.
- **verifier 理由**：Verified. The comment at alert_gate.go:51-56 asserts in the present tense that `_INBOX.<nuid>` is a subject "this connection is no longer granted", but every connection that reaches probeClusterHealth is an activated member, and PermissionsForActivatedMember retains bare `_INBOX.>` at permissions.go:248. I checked all seven gateDestructive call sites (expose.go:84/320, run.go:93, session.go:171, transfer.go:167/446) against the connect calls in the same files: every one of them connects with cli.CtlNameForSession(sid) (expose.go:77/313, run.go:86, session.go:163, transfer.go:160/439). The unactivated template cannot reach the code at all — its Pub.Allow has no cluster-health subject, only session.create/list, cluster-roster and _INBOX. So reverting `nc.NewInbox()` to `nats.NewInbox()` is observably inert today, and the reviewer is right that nothing would fail: cmd/tether/alerts_test.go:
- **对修法的批评**：The reword is the right remedy and should say plainly "correct in preparation for the member wildcard being removed (A-F1); inert until then" rather than asserting a present-tense grant that does not exist — this is the same class of defect as A-F1's three false comments and should be fixed in the same pass, or it will be fixed twice. The proposed source-level guard is low value but harmless: a grep for the literal `nats.NewInbox(` does not collide with `nc.NewInbox(`, and cmd/tether already has that pattern (ack_alert_error_test.go). Two caveats: (1) prefer the behavioural guard once A-F1 lands — a d3/e2e assertion that probeClusterHealth still corroborates under scoped-inbox-only grants proves the thing the comment cares about, whereas the grep only proves a token is absent; (2) per CLAUDE.md §5, if this becomes a listed gate it needs a `// gate-control:` anchor and a negative control,
- **建议修法**：Reword to state the conditional truth — the call is correct in preparation for the member wildcard being removed, and is inert until then — or fix A-F1, after which the comment becomes true. Either way, if the line is worth a comment it is worth a guard: a source-level assertion in cmd/tether (same shape as ack_alert_error_test.go's existing text check) that alert_gate.go contains no package-level `nats.NewInbox(` costs nothing and is the only thing that can keep it from regressing.

### AC-2 · `internal/agent/agent.go:1353-1372 (lease branch) vs 1383-1401 (everAuthed arm)`

- **lane**：agent connection and session lifetime (batch I: AC-F1/AC-F2/AC-F3, plus the everAuthed arm and the new CustomInboxPrefix that lands in the same function)
- **裁决**：PARTIAL（原报 MAJOR → 订正 MINOR）
- **主张**：The everAuthed rule — "a denial after this process authenticated is ambiguous, resolve it in favour of not acting" — is applied to the die-or-retry decision but NOT to its sibling, the unconditional dropLease that runs first; AC-F3 simultaneously widened the set of failures classified as auth denials.
- **失败场景**：An agent running under lease name gpu1-02 has been up for days (everAuthed=true) on a shared/NFS home directory — the clone scenario the lease feature exists for; its stateStore is detached so it cannot write the incumbent's state.json, and its tunnel adapter dials as gpu1-02. The cluster restarts (rolling `cluster upgrade`, or a whole-cluster reboot). For a few seconds every broker either refuses TCP or, being mid-restart with the auth_callout responder not yet subscribed, answers `Authorization Violation` — the exact transient nats-server behaviour buildConnOptions' own IgnoreAuthErrorAbort comment documents. connectNATS reaches `if nidOf(a) != a.cfg.NID` BEFORE the everAuthed check and calls dropLease unconditionally: stateStore.reattach() hands this clone write access to the incumbent's state.json, and ExposeAdapter.SetNID(basename) retargets the tunnel to a REGISTER line that matches the incumbent's allocation row (adoptRoutingNID's own comment: "would present a name that matches the INCUMBENT's row and evict it — the exact defect this increment exists to close"). everAuthed then keeps the process alive retrying under the basename instead of exiting, so the contention persists until a lease is re-adopted rather than ending with the process. Before batch I the same denial killed the process on the next attempt; now it survives and contends.
- **verifier 理由**：The structural fact is true: agent.go:1353 `if nidOf(a) != a.cfg.NID { ... dropLease(a); continue }` runs before the everAuthed arm at 1383, with no history condition, and dropLease (instance.go:555-574) does routingNID.Store(base) + SetNID(base) + stateStore.reattach(). The claim that AC-F3 widened the trigger set is also true and is the one genuinely new-in-this-batch part: a pooled failure that previously read as ErrNoServers (transient, lease kept) can now be re-attributed to an auth denial and reach dropLease. But the finding's framing as an oversight is refuted. The unconditional drop is the adjudicated non-negotiable of the lease increment: connect_auth_lease_test.go:60-88 quotes docs/reviews/cloned-credential-instances-plan.md §6 — "The plan's non-negotiable is DEGRADE, NEVER REFUSE. The degrade target exists and is free: drop the lease and re-present the basename" — and TestLeas
- **对修法的批评**：The primary proposed fix is structurally impossible and would delete the feature. A lease name is only ever held after adoptRoutingNID, which is only reached from a register REPLY (instance.go:363 / 413), which requires a successful connect — and that connect sets a.everAuthed.Store(true) (agent.go:1327). So `nidOf(a) != a.cfg.NID` implies everAuthed==true, always. Gating the drop on `!a.everAuthed.Load()` makes dropLease unreachable, and the scenario it exists for — a broker rollback or one un-upgraded member of the auth_callout queue group denying a lease name — is BY CONSTRUCTION an agent that has been up and authenticated for days. That directly reverses the adjudicated "DEGRADE, NEVER REFUSE" and would turn a rollback back into an agent that re-presents a refused name forever. Only the secondary variant (N consecutive denials, or a denial surviving one full backoff, before degrading
- **建议修法**：Gate the lease drop on the same history signal the die/retry decision uses: drop the lease only when !a.everAuthed.Load() (a first connect under a lease name really is an un-admitted name), and when everAuthed is true require N consecutive denials, or a denial that survives one full backoff, before degrading — otherwise take the transient arm. Add a test that is the mirror of TestLeaseAdoptionDoesNotOutliveAnAuthDenial: same setup, everAuthed=true, assert nidOf(a) is STILL the lease name after the first denial, and that it does degrade once the denials persist.

### AC-3 · `internal/agent/session_generation_test.go:120-150 (TestDialFailureIsAttributedAcrossAMultiURLPool)`

- **lane**：agent connection and session lifetime (batch I: AC-F1/AC-F2/AC-F3, plus the everAuthed arm and the new CustomInboxPrefix that lands in the same function)
- **裁决**：CONFIRMED（原报 MAJOR → 订正 MINOR）
- **主张**：The AC-F3 guard calls attributeDialFailure directly with a hand-constructed nats.ErrNoServers and nil options; nothing anywhere asserts the helper is WIRED into connectNATS, and the hand-made error is what hides AC-1.
- **失败场景**：Delete the whole `if err != nil { if aerr := attributeDialFailure(...) }` block from connectNATS (agent.go:1305-1325) and the package still compiles and every test in it still passes — the only caller left is the test. The fix can regress to a no-op silently. Separately, because the test supplies `nats.ErrNoServers` itself rather than letting nats.Connect produce the pooled error, it can never observe that the real pooled error for a partitioned peer is an i/o timeout and the gate rejects it (AC-1). And it passes `nil` opts, so it never exercises the option set production actually hands the probe (identity, Name, PIN, the session's connTracker, the live session's callbacks — see AC-5).
- **verifier 理由**：Facts check out. `grep -rn attributeDialFailure --include=*.go .` returns the definition and one production call (agent.go:1322) plus three direct calls in session_generation_test.go:133/147/150 — no source-shape guard, no AST check, and no behavioural test drives connectNATS with a multi-URL pool (every lease/auth test in the package uses a single denying URL, where `len(urls) < 2` short-circuits). Deleting the call block from connectNATS leaves the helper referenced only by the test, so the package still compiles and stays green: the wiring can regress silently. The second half is also right and is the more useful observation — the test hands the helper a hand-made nats.ErrNoServers, which is precisely why AC-1 shipped undetected. Downgraded from MAJOR to MINOR: there is no live defect (the production wiring is present and correct today), and the substantive consequence is AC-1, which
- **对修法的批评**：The proposed connectNATS-level test is the right shape and is the same test AC-1 needs, so build it once. Two cautions. (1) It must use `denyingNATS` FIRST and a silent-accept listener LAST — with the refused address last the pooled error is ErrNoServers and the test passes against today's buggy gate, i.e. it would be a green test over an open defect. (2) Driving connectNATS end-to-end means the agent's real dial pool: `effectiveDialURLs()` is roster-derived, so the test has to seed the pool (or set cfg.NATSURL to the comma-joined string) rather than assume both URLs survive to the dial — otherwise the assertion silently degrades to a single-URL pool and short-circuits at `len(urls) < 2`. Keeping the direct-call unit test for the len<2 and already-attributed edges is right; its ErrNoServers literal must change to whatever the corrected gate keys on, or it will keep asserting the old cont
- **建议修法**：Add a connectNATS-level test: start a denying nats-server plus a listener that accepts and never speaks (or 192.0.2.1), set the agent's dial pool to both with the denying one FIRST, adopt a lease name, and assert connectNATS drops the lease / returns the auth-classified terminal error. That single test is both the wiring receipt and the regression guard for AC-1. Keep the direct-call unit test for the len<2 and already-attributed edges.

### AC-4 · `internal/agent/session_generation_test.go:56-97 (TestEachSessionsCallbacksOnlyActForThatSession)`

- **lane**：agent connection and session lifetime (batch I: AC-F1/AC-F2/AC-F3, plus the everAuthed arm and the new CustomInboxPrefix that lands in the same function)
- **裁决**：CONFIRMED（原报 MINOR → 订正 MINOR）
- **主张**：The AC-F2 guard exercises only the DisconnectErrHandler and only through the redial timer; the ReconnectHandler's generation check — the half that causes the double-dispatch the finding is actually about — is never invoked, and armFailClosed is never observed.
- **失败场景**：Remove `if a.sessionGen.Load() != gen { return }` from the ReconnectHandler (agent.go:2168) and leave the disconnect one in place: the test stays green. A retired connection's late nats.go reconnect then passes the (now-cleared) `rebuilding` guard, runs onNATSReconnect and re-subscribes the forwarded subjects on a socket nobody reads — the exact double-dispatch the AC-F2 comment names. Likewise, move the disconnect handler's gen check to sit AFTER a.armFailClosed() and before a.armRedialWatchdog(): the test still passes while a retired session keeps arming the SS-teardown countdown, which is the more destructive of the two arms.
- **verifier 理由**：Verified by reading the whole test. TestEachSessionsCallbacksOnlyActForThatSession checks only `o.ReconnectedCB != nil` at line 70 and never invokes it; the only handler driven is DisconnectedErrCB, and the only observable is redialArmed(a) (the redialTimer under redialMu). Both mutations survive: (a) deleting `if a.sessionGen.Load() != gen { return }` from the ReconnectHandler (agent.go:2168) is invisible to the test, so the double-dispatch half of AC-F2 — a retired conn re-running onNATSReconnect and re-subscribing on a dead socket — has no guard at all; (b) moving the disconnect handler's gen check to between armFailClosed() and armRedialWatchdog() also stays green, and armFailClosed is the more destructive arm (it starts the SS-teardown countdown). Nothing in the file reads flcTimer/cancelFailClosed state. MINOR is right: no live defect, a guard that covers half of what its own heade
- **对修法的批评**：The negative control (`o.ReconnectedCB(nil)` on the retired option set, assert reconnectInFlight stayed false) is safe and correct. The positive control as proposed is not: `co.ReconnectedCB(nil)` on the CURRENT generation passes the gen check and runs `go a.onNATSReconnect(nil)`, which reaches `a.ncBox.Store(nil)`, `a.courier.drainStarted(ctx, nil)` and `a.register(ctx, nil)` (proxy.go:878-903) — a nil *nats.Conn deref in a goroutine, which panics the whole test binary, and en route it calls cancelFailClosed and clobbers ncBox on the shared agent. genTestAgent already starts an embedded server (testharness.StartNATS), so hand the handler a real conn dialled against it; register will simply fail and Warn. Also, "assert the opposite" on reconnectInFlight for the current set is racy — the goroutine's `defer a.reconnectInFlight.Store(false)` can land before the assertion. Assert something o
- **建议修法**：In the same test, drive `o.ReconnectedCB(nil)` for the retired option set and assert a.reconnectInFlight stayed false (and that a pre-armed redial timer was NOT stopped), then drive it for the current set and assert the opposite — the same positive/negative control pattern the disconnect half already uses. Add an armFailClosed observation (a.flcTimer under flcMu, mirroring redialArmed) so a check placed below armFailClosed cannot pass.

### AC-5 · `internal/agent/agent.go:1322 (attributeDialFailure(dial, err, connOpts)) and 2287`

- **lane**：agent connection and session lifetime (batch I: AC-F1/AC-F2/AC-F3, plus the everAuthed arm and the new CustomInboxPrefix that lands in the same function)
- **裁决**：CONFIRMED（原报 MINOR → 订正 MINOR）
- **主张**：The attribution probes are dialled with the LIVE option set, so throwaway connections carry this session's DisconnectErr/Reconnect handlers at the current generation, this session's connTracker, and the agent's full CONNECT identity.
- **失败场景**：During a partial outage the probe dial to a momentarily-healthy broker succeeds and the server drops it before nc.Close() (a restarting broker does exactly this). The DisconnectErrHandler fires with sessionGen == gen — the generation guard cannot help, the probe shares the live generation — so armFailClosed() and armRedialWatchdog() run on behalf of a connection the agent threw away. session() clears the watchdog BEFORE connectNATS, never after, so a watchdog armed by a probe survives into the session that connectNATS is about to establish and can fire fireRedial on a healthy new session redialAfter later. Second consequence: each probe presents nats.Name(cli.AgentName(...)) and, on the not-yet-provisioned path, nats.Token(PIN) — so one failed pooled connect now costs up to N full auth_callout decisions instead of one, each an argon2id verify (0.15-0.3s) against the process-wide budget batch D just introduced at rate.Every(1s)/burst 10. Probe conns also accumulate in the session's connTracker registry (capped at 8), displacing the conns poisoning actually needs to reach.
- **verifier 理由**：The wiring is as described: agent.go:1322 passes connOpts — buildOpts()'s output, i.e. buildConnOptions' ReconnectHandler + DisconnectErrHandler closed over the CURRENT gen, nats.Name, nats.Nkey, nats.CustomInboxPrefix, nats.Token(PIN), the proxydial options and a.trackerDialOption — straight into nats.Connect at 2287. So a probe that connects and is dropped by the server before nc.Close() fires the live session's DisconnectErrHandler with sessionGen == gen; the generation guard cannot discriminate because the probe shares the generation. armFailClosed() and armRedialWatchdog() then run for a connection the agent threw away, and session() clears the watchdog BEFORE connectNATS by explicit design (agent.go:964-970, with a comment saying clearing it after races an immediate DisconnectErrHandler) — so a probe-armed watchdog survives into the session about to be established. The PIN cost is
- **对修法的批评**：The direction is right but one concrete form of it is a trap. Any 'build a separate probe option set' must NOT route through a.buildConnOptions() again: that function's first statement is `gen := a.sessionGen.Add(1)` (agent.go:2132), so building probe options bumps the generation and instantly retires the live session's closures — AC-7's hazard, materialized as a real bug on a path where a connection can be live. Prefer the copy-and-override form (append nats.DisconnectErrHandler(nil), nats.ReconnectHandler(nil) to a copy of connOpts), which touches no counter. Second, dropping SetCustomDialer(tracker) removes the only dial bound on that path — nats.go skips Options.Timeout when a CustomDialer is installed, so the tracker's context.WithTimeout(agentCtx, teardownDialTimeout=2s) is what bounds probes today; the replacement must set nats.Timeout explicitly, which the fix does say. That also
- **建议修法**：Build the probe options separately: reuse only what attribution needs (identity, Name, Token, proxy dialer, a short nats.Timeout) and omit ReconnectHandler / DisconnectErrHandler / SetCustomDialer(sessionTracker), e.g. by having buildOpts return a (session, probe) pair or by appending nats.DisconnectErrHandler(nil), nats.ReconnectHandler(nil) to a copy. Also bound the whole probe loop with the caller's ctx and a total deadline so a wide roster cannot double the time each retry spends dialling.

### AC-6 · `internal/auth/permissions.go:99 (PermissionsForUnactivated Sub) — reached from internal/agent/agent.go:2226-2230 / internal/cli/natsconn.go:60-66`

- **lane**：agent connection and session lifetime (batch I: AC-F1/AC-F2/AC-F3, plus the everAuthed arm and the new CustomInboxPrefix that lands in the same function)
- **裁决**：PARTIAL（原报 MAJOR → 订正 MINOR）
- **主张**：N-1 break on the ctl bootstrap path: the unactivated template dropped the bare `Sub "_INBOX.>"` while the two sibling templates in the same diff deliberately kept it "for the N-1 window", so an un-upgraded ctl cannot receive replies from an upgraded broker.
- **失败场景**：Broker is upgraded first (the mandated order); an operator's laptop still runs the previous release. `tether login`, `tether session create` and `tether session ls` all connect with nats.Name(cli.CtlNameUnactivated) and call nc.Request. An N-1 ctl does not set nats.CustomInboxPrefix, so nats.go's response mux subscribes `_INBOX.<nuid>.*`. The new unactivated JWT grants Sub on `_INBOX.<sha256(nkey)[:16]>.>` only, so nats-server refuses the subscription and the request times out after 5s — the user sees `session list: ... (broker unreachable on NATS)`, i.e. a working broker reported as unreachable, on the three commands that bootstrap everything else. Shell-completion's transport (internal/cli/completion_transport.go:227) fails the same way. The agent and activated-member templates are unaffected because they explicitly retained the wildcard.
- **verifier 理由**：The mechanism is factually correct — PermissionsForUnactivated's Sub is exactly [ctrl.version.announce, inboxSubjectFor(actor)] (permissions.go:81-100), Pub still carries `_INBOX.>`, the two sibling templates retain the bare wildcard with explicit N-1 comments, and the unactivated template is what cmd/tether/session.go:43/103, login.go:51 and internal/cli/completion_transport.go:227 connect with — so an N-1 ctl's `_INBOX.<nuid>.*` mux subscription is refused and the requests time out. But this is not an oversight: it is the explicitly adjudicated decision. docs/reviews/prerelease-audit-plan.md §决策A's table reads, for the unactivated ctl, '无条件删除 Sub "_INBOX.>" ... 旧 ctl 的 session create/list/login 需升级。这是唯一的 N-1 例外', and the section immediately above it ('为什么"两步走"不可接受') rejects the keep-both transitional shape on the ground that it buys zero security during the transition, citing a user di
- **对修法的批评**：The primary fix — restore `_INBOX.>` in PermissionsForUnactivated — must not be applied. It directly reverses §决策A, and it reopens precisely the anonymous half of L1-F1: this template is handed to any connection presenting a self-minted nkey and the CONNECT name `tether-cli` with zero DB lookups, so the wildcard there is a grant to the internet, not to an authorized principal (which is the distinction the member template's own comment relies on to justify keeping it). Calling that risk 'the pre-existing one, strictly smaller' understates it — it is the only anonymous instance of the class, and the plan already weighed and rejected carrying it one more release. test/d3/inbox_isolation_acl_test.go asserts the wildcard IS refused for this template, so the fix would also require deleting a guard written for the decision. The remaining half of the proposal is right and should be done: amend r
- **建议修法**：Keep `"_INBOX.>"` in PermissionsForUnactivated's Sub for the N-1 window with the same comment and the same removal-next-epoch note the sibling templates carry — the anonymous-read risk it reopens is the pre-existing one, and it is strictly smaller than making every N-1 ctl unable to log in. If the anonymous exposure is judged unacceptable to carry for one release, that is a ProtoVersion-epoch decision and must be written down as one (CLAUDE.md §5) rather than shipped as a silent grant removal. Either way add an N-1 test: a client with the DEFAULT inbox prefix must still complete session.list.req against the new unactivated template.

### AC-7 · `internal/agent/agent.go:595-596`

- **lane**：agent connection and session lifetime (batch I: AC-F1/AC-F2/AC-F3, plus the everAuthed arm and the new CustomInboxPrefix that lands in the same function)
- **裁决**：CONFIRMED（原报 MINOR → 订正 MINOR）
- **主张**：Comment is false as of this commit: "buildConnOptions runs once per session" — the lease-degrade path calls buildOpts() a second time inside the same connectNATS, so sessionGen counts option BUILDS, not sessions.
- **失败场景**：Not a runtime defect today (the extra build happens only while nc is nil, so no live connection is retired). It is a correctness hazard for the next reader: the invariant the gen guard is documented to rest on — one generation per session — is not what the code maintains, so anyone adding a second buildOpts() call on a path where a connection IS live would reasonably believe the guard still holds while it silently retires the live session's callbacks.
- **verifier 理由**：Exact and verifiable. agent.go:2132 `gen := a.sessionGen.Add(1)` is inside buildConnOptions; buildOpts (which calls it at 1260) is invoked at agent.go:1277 before the retry loop and again at agent.go:1362 inside the lease-drop arm. So a single connectNATS call can produce two or more generations, and the field comment at agent.go:595-596 ('buildConnOptions runs once per session, so the handler closures it creates capture the generation they belong to') does not describe the code. The reviewer is also right that this is not a live defect today: the second build happens only while nc is nil, so no live connection's closures are retired. MINOR is correct, and the hazard framing is validated by AC-5, whose most natural fix would add a third buildConnOptions call on a path where a connection can be live — i.e. the exact mistake this stale comment invites.
- **对修法的批评**：The proposed wording is accurate and better than what is there. Two caveats. First, its load-bearing clause — 'connectNATS rebuilds only on a path where no connection was established' — is itself an unenforced invariant that a comment cannot hold; the same fact is what AC-5's fix would quietly break. If it is worth writing down it is worth pinning, and the cheap mechanical form is a source-shape assertion that buildConnOptions is called from exactly the known sites (the repo already uses this shape in dataplane_lifetime_test.go and session_generation_test.go's readAgentSource guards). Second, the note belongs at the second call site (agent.go:1362) as well as on the field, because that is where the next reader adds the third one. No structural-budget or N-1 concern either way — it is a comment plus, optionally, one assertion in an existing test file, so it must not become a new `*_test.g
- **建议修法**：Reword to what is actually true and what actually makes the guard sound: "sessionGen counts OPTION BUILDS. Every build supersedes the previous one, and only the newest build can be behind a live connection — connectNATS rebuilds only on a path where no connection was established — so comparing against the current value retires exactly the closures that belong to no live conn."

### C10 · `internal/agent/exec.go:438, internal/broker/transfer_reconcile.go:97, and ~78 further sites`

- **lane**：comments, documented invariants, and repo conventions
- **裁决**：CONFIRMED（原报 MINOR → 订正 MINOR）
- **主张**：The new `// origin: prerelease audit <lane>/<id>` anchors are dead pointers: a large share of the cited finding ids resolve to nothing anywhere in the repo, because the per-finding ledger they name was never committed.
- **失败场景**：A future maintainer touching `reapAbandonedExecChild` follows `// origin: prerelease audit agent-exec/L3-F3` to find out what was decided and why. `grep -rn 'agent-exec/L3-F3' docs/` returns nothing; so do `agent-exec/L3-F1`, `agent-run/L3-F2`, `agent-transfer/L3-F1`, `broker-proc/L3-F1`, `cluster-fsm/L3-F1`, `cli-serve-agent-cluster/L4-F1`, `CLI-F1`, `CLI-F3`, `L-BCO-F2`, `DRD-F2`, `DRD-F3`. `docs/reviews/prerelease-audit-plan.md` names lanes and a subset of ids but not these, and its §preamble says the per-finding ledger lives in `$CLAUDE_JOB_DIR/tmp/decisions.md` and `audit_corpus.md` — neither is in the repo. The anchor therefore costs a lookup and returns nothing, which the repo's own gate header calls out as "worse than no pointer at all". Two anchors are actively misleading: `broker-cluster-ops/#15` and `broker-core #27` reuse this repo's established gotcha numbering with a different meaning (`#15` and `#27` already have 14 and 57 hits under docs/ referring to unrelated incidents), so a reader who knows the numbering will chase the wrong record.
- **verifier 理由**：Reproduced. The tree carries 93 `// origin: prerelease audit ...` lines (118 mentions overall across .go/.sh). Grepping each lane-qualified id under docs/ returns zero for every one the reviewer names: agent-exec/L3-F3, agent-exec/L3-F1, agent-run/L3-F2, agent-transfer/L3-F1, broker-proc/L3-F1, cluster-fsm/L3-F1, cli-serve-agent-cluster/L4-F1, CLI-F1, CLI-F3, L-BCO-F2, DRD-F2, DRD-F3. The bare forms that do hit are worse than useless: `L3-F1` appears twice in prerelease-audit-plan.md (:59 InstallSnapshot 身份移植, :63 IsNotLeader) for different lanes, so even a resolvable id is ambiguous without the lane the anchor supplies. The gate is genuinely silent, not satisfied: `test/determinism/origin_line_test.go:41-43` matches only `docs/[A-Za-z0-9._/-]+\.md` on origin lines, so a path-less anchor is invisible — and the file's own header states the rule the anchors break ('A pointer that rots is w
- **对修法的批评**：Both options work; the first is much cheaper and better matches how the repo already behaves. Committing a `docs/reviews/prerelease-audit-findings.md` keyed `lane/id` makes ~93 existing anchors true with no edits and no re-churn of the diff, and it fits the §3 step-7 archive rule (it is a process product, so it belongs under docs/reviews/ from birth — `test/architecture/docs_layout_test.go` would reject it at docs/ top level anyway). The second option — rewriting every anchor to embed a docs path — has a hidden cost the reviewer treats as a benefit: it brings ~93 lines under `TestOriginLinesPointAtDocumentsThatExist`, which then breaks every one of them the day that plan is renamed or consolidated, and CLAUDE.md §3 step 7 explicitly declines to retro-fix frozen references. Prefer the ledger. Do renumber `broker-cluster-ops/#15` and `broker-core #27` either way — those two are not merely
- **建议修法**：Either commit the per-finding ledger (a `docs/reviews/prerelease-audit-findings.md` appendix keyed `lane/id` — the plan §3 already promises one for the internal-review round) and leave the anchors as they are, or make each anchor cite the document that will exist: `// origin: prerelease audit — docs/reviews/prerelease-audit-plan.md §2 (broker-transfer BT-F3)`, which also brings them under the existing origin gate. Rename `broker-cluster-ops/#15` and `broker-core #27` to non-colliding ids.

### C11 · `internal/agent/agent.go:595-596`

- **lane**：comments, documented invariants, and repo conventions
- **裁决**：CONFIRMED（原报 MINOR → 订正 MINOR）
- **主张**："buildConnOptions runs once per session" — the sentence the whole `sessionGen` correctness argument rests on — is not true; the lease-degrade retry calls it again inside the same session.
- **失败场景**：An engineer reasoning about whether a stale callback can be mistaken for a live one reads "buildConnOptions runs once per session, so the handler closures it creates capture the generation they belong to" and concludes generation == session, one-to-one. It is not: `connectNATS`'s auth-denied-under-lease-name arm calls `buildOpts()` again at agent.go:1362, which calls `buildConnOptions()` and bumps `sessionGen` a second time within one `session()`. The behaviour is benign today (no connection exists at that point, so the superseded closures are discarded before any callback can fire), but the invariant as stated is what a future reader will use to judge a new call site — and a third caller added on the strength of it, on a path where a live conn does exist, would silently retire the live session's callbacks.
- **verifier 理由**：The call graph is exactly as claimed. `buildConnOptions` (agent.go:2128) opens with `gen := a.sessionGen.Add(1)`. Its only production caller is the `buildOpts` closure at :1259-1260, and `buildOpts` is invoked twice inside one `connectNATS`: once at :1277 and again at :1362 in the auth-denied-under-assigned-lease-name arm ('REBUILD THE OPTIONS. The CONNECT name is baked into them...'). So the sentence at agent.go:595-596 — 'buildConnOptions runs once per session' — is false, and it is a `+` line in this diff. The reviewer's benignness analysis holds: at :1362 the preceding `nats.Connect` failed with an auth violation, so the superseded closures were never bound to a live connection, and even if a nats.go teardown callback fired on the failed attempt the closures decline on the generation compare. So this is a comment defect, not a bug — MINOR is correct, and the reviewer says so honestly
