- **对修法的批评**：The proposed sentence is accurate but it only documents the hazard; it does not remove it, and the finding's own stated risk is 'a third caller added on the strength of it'. A comment cannot stop that. The stronger fix, worth putting to the main process, is to make the invariant true by construction: move `sessionGen.Add(1)` out of `buildConnOptions` and up to the session boundary that actually starts a session, leaving buildConnOptions to read the current generation. The :1362 retry would then rebuild options carrying the same generation as the failed first attempt — which is correct, since neither was ever live — and 'one generation per session' stops depending on nobody adding a third call site. That is an implementation change to AC-F2's design and therefore the main process's call, not a comment edit; if it is declined, take the reviewer's sentence as written, but state the CONDITIO
- **建议修法**：Change the sentence to "buildConnOptions runs once per session, plus once more per lease-name degrade retry (agent.go:1362); every call supersedes the previous generation, which is safe there because the superseded options were never bound to a live connection." That preserves the argument and states the condition a new call site would have to satisfy.

### C3 · `internal/authcallout/handler.go:528-532 (+ call sites :400-402 and :488-489)`

- **lane**：comments, documented invariants, and repo conventions
- **裁决**：CONFIRMED（原报 MAJOR → 订正 MINOR）
- **主张**：`pinRateLimited`'s doc comment still describes the per-IP-only behaviour and the fail-OPEN-on-empty-IP promise that the same commit deleted, and both call-site comments still say a refusal here means the SOURCE is over its budget.
- **失败场景**：An operator reports that a legitimate first-time `tether login --pin` / agent first-join is refused on a broker whose per-IP counters are all empty (11 concurrent first-joins exhaust `pinGlobalBurst=10`, refill 1/s). An engineer debugging it reads handler.go:528-532 — "reports whether clientIP is currently over its E.6 PIN-attempt budget. An empty IP … fails OPEN: we would rather never throttle real joiners on a missing address" — and handler.go:400 — "a source over its per-IP budget is refused BEFORE the Argon2 verify" — and concludes the report is impossible, because none of those conditions hold. The body contradicts all three: the very first statement is `if h.pinLimiterFor().globalBlocked(h.Now()) { return true }`, which ignores clientIP entirely and therefore does NOT fail open on an empty IP. The correction exists only as an inline comment inside the body, below the doc comment that denies it.
- **verifier 理由**：Factually accurate on every sub-claim. handler.go:528-532 is unchanged context in `git diff` and still reads 'reports whether clientIP is currently over its E.6 PIN-attempt budget. An empty IP ... fails OPEN'. The body's first statement is now `if h.pinLimiterFor().globalBlocked(h.Now()) { return true }` (added by this diff), which never looks at clientIP and therefore does NOT fail open on an empty IP. Both call-site comments (:400-402 'a source over its per-IP budget is refused BEFORE the Argon2 verify', :488-489 'refuse a source over its per-IP budget') are unchanged context and now describe half the gate. ratelimit.go:10-21 does carry the accurate two-ceiling description, so the honest text exists and simply was not carried to the three landing sites. Severity reduced from MAJOR to MINOR: the correcting comment is not buried — it is the first six lines of a nine-line function body, d
- **对修法的批评**：Rewriting the doc and the two call-site comments is correct and carries no risk. The third suggestion — 'returning a distinguishable reason so the log line at :404/:491 tells an operator which ceiling fired' — is worth doing but is an implementation change, not a comment fix, and it has a consequence the reviewer does not mention: `pinRateLimited` currently returns bool and both call sites map it to the single sentinel `ErrPINRateLimited`. Widening the return means either a second sentinel on the wire-facing deny path (which would need a `brokerCodeExitClass` entry and, per the ACL/exit-class gates, an entry in the code↔class tables) or a log-only field. Log-only is the right scope. Also note the fix should NOT copy ratelimit.go's 'PRIMARY brute-force defense is memory-hard argon2id' framing into pinRateLimited's doc — that is the claim C9 is about, and it belongs to the per-IP half only
- **建议修法**：Rewrite handler.go:528-532 to state both ceilings and that the empty-IP fail-open now applies only to the per-IP half; amend the two call-site comments from "a source over its per-IP budget" to "over its per-IP budget OR the process-wide argon2 ceiling". Consider returning a distinguishable reason so the log line at :404/:491 tells an operator which ceiling fired.

### C5 · `internal/broker/clusterdrain.go:173`

- **lane**：comments, documented invariants, and repo conventions
- **裁决**：CONFIRMED（原报 MAJOR → 订正 MINOR）
- **主张**：The step-2b comment's ordering claim "Ordered BEFORE the phase bump and (for retire) before RemoveServer" is false in this function after the same diff moved `setPhase(DRAINING)` above `awaitHomeConvergence`.
- **失败场景**：An engineer changing `DrainNode` reads the 2b block (clusterdrain.go:161-176), whose whole purpose is to record why the convergence gate must come first, and takes "Ordered BEFORE the phase bump" as a live invariant — then either restores the old order (undoing L-BCO-F2) or writes a new step on the assumption that nothing has bumped the phase yet. What is actually still ordered before the phase bump is only the `pendingRetireConvergence` READ at :178; the blocking gate `awaitHomeConvergence` at :206 now runs after `setPhase` at :203. The new §3 block at :180-202 explains and justifies the inversion but does not amend the older block twelve lines above it, so the file states both orders as fact.
- **verifier 理由**：The stale sentence is exactly where the reviewer says. clusterdrain.go:172-173 reads 'Ordered BEFORE the phase bump and (for retire) before RemoveServer: removing a node while exposes still point at it is the very outage this gate prevents' and is unchanged context; `setPhase(nodeID, phaseDraining, ...)` now runs at :203 and `awaitHomeConvergence` at :206. Only the `pendingRetireConvergence` READ at :178 still precedes the bump. The §3 block at :183-202 explains and justifies the inversion ('The convergence wait used to come first...'), so the file does state both orders. I also confirmed the reviewer's own soundness check on the re-run path — `PlanClusterNodePhase` guards `AND phase IN (...)`, so a second run over an already-DRAINING node is a zero-row UPDATE — meaning this really is confined to the comment. Downgraded from MAJOR to MINOR because the failure scenario is largely self-blo
- **对修法的批评**：The fix is correct and minimal, and the reviewer is right to insist on a pointer rather than a deletion — the 'before RemoveServer' half of the sentence is still live (driveRetire), so deleting the whole clause would lose a true invariant along with the false one. One addition: the sentence's subject is ambiguous between 'the gate' and 'the wait', which is how it decayed in the first place; the amended text should name the two halves explicitly (READ at :178, WAIT at :206) so the next reordering makes one of them obviously wrong instead of silently half-wrong.
- **建议修法**：Amend clusterdrain.go:171-176 to distinguish the two halves: the convergence READ and (for retire, in driveRetire) RemoveServer are still ordered as described; the WAIT is now deliberately after the phase bump, and point at the §3 block for why. A one-line pointer is enough — what must not remain is a bare assertion of the old order.

### C6 · `internal/tunnel/tunnel.go:487-490`

- **lane**：comments, documented invariants, and repo conventions
- **裁决**：CONFIRMED（原报 MAJOR → 订正 MINOR）
- **主张**：`errHandshakeCeiling` is documented as "the damper class for a connection refused because maxRegisterHandshakes is saturated", but it is not a class: `regLogClass` has a fixed three-value taxonomy and maps it to `"other"`, and the line it produces claims a REGISTER was read when none was.
- **失败场景**：The broker hits the new 256-concurrent-handshake ceiling under a flood (exactly the BDP-F1 condition the ceiling exists for). The only evidence emitted is `s.registerReadFailed(conn, errHandshakeCeiling)`, which logs `"tunnel server: read REGISTER" class=other err="tunnel server: concurrent REGISTER handshake ceiling reached"`. Two consequences: (1) the message is false — `handleAgent` was never entered, no read was attempted — so an operator grepping for read failures mis-attributes it, and an operator grepping for a ceiling signal finds nothing under a distinct message; (2) the `"other"` Tracker is shared with every unclassified read failure, so if any unrelated `other` run is already in progress the first ceiling event is suppressed to Debug for up to the 5-minute Cap, and `registerReadOK` resets it on the next authorized register. The one signal that says "this broker is at its admission ceiling" is both mislabelled and cross-suppressed by unrelated traffic.
- **verifier 理由**：The mechanical core is exactly right. `regLogClass` (tunnel.go:496-506) returns only 'eof' / 'timeout' / 'other'; a fresh `errors.New` sentinel matches neither `errors.Is(err, io.EOF)` nor `errors.As(err,&ne) && ne.Timeout()`, so `errHandshakeCeiling` lands in 'other'. `registerReadFailed` (:527-561) emits the literal message 'tunnel server: read REGISTER' and keys `regLogTracker(class)`/`FailReaffirm(now, class)` on that string, so the ceiling shares one Tracker and one run identity with every other unclassified read failure. `registerHandshakeRefused` (:476-481) is the only caller. The same file proves the alternative exists: `registerDenied` (:574-586) uses its own `const class = "denied"` and its own Tracker. So the doc at :487-490 calling the sentinel 'the damper class' is inaccurate, and the emitted line does claim a read that never happened. Downgraded to MINOR because two of the
- **对修法的批评**：Adding a fourth arm to `regLogClass` is safe — it is a boolean `switch`, not an enum switch, so `test/determinism/enum_switch_default_test.go` does not apply, and `regLogTracker` creates classes lazily so no table needs updating. Two things the fix must account for that the reviewer does not mention. First, `registerReadOK` (:599-620) walks every class in `s.regLog` and recovers them together on the first AUTHORIZED register — so under a flood that is BOTH saturating the ceiling and letting legitimate agents through, a new 'handshake_ceiling' class will be reset on each success just like the others; if the intent is a ceiling signal that survives that, the recovery loop needs a decision too, not just the taxonomy. Second, `regLogClass`'s own doc calls itself 'the fixed, coarse REGISTER-read failure taxonomy (review M1)' — a ceiling refusal is not a read failure, so adding it to that func
- **建议修法**：Add a fourth arm to `regLogClass` — `case errors.Is(err, errHandshakeCeiling): return "handshake_ceiling"` — so the sentinel gets the dedicated Tracker its comment promises, and split the emit so a ceiling refusal logs its own message (e.g. "tunnel server: REGISTER admission ceiling") rather than "read REGISTER". Then the comment at :487-490 becomes true as written.

### C7 · `scripts/install.sh:480 and scripts/install.sh:908`

- **lane**：comments, documented invariants, and repo conventions
- **裁决**：CONFIRMED（原报 MINOR → 订正 MINOR）
- **主张**：Two operator-facing success banners were made false by the same commit: both claim files were written that a kept-config re-run did not write.
- **失败场景**：An operator re-runs `install.sh --role agent --session lab` on an existing install to pick up a new release. The banner prints `✔ agent config written to ~/.tether/agent/lab/agent.yaml` — but `config_dest` kept the existing file and the generated content went to `agent.yaml.new`. Three lines later `report_kept_configs` says the opposite. The operator sees a ✔ and a ⚠ about the same path in one screen and has to guess which is true. The broker side is the same shape and worse: `✔ systemd units created and ENABLED for boot (nats-server, tether-broker, caddy)` prints even when all four unit files (nats.conf + three .service) already existed and only `.new` files were written — so an operator re-running specifically to pick up a corrected unit (the G1 #23 `Restart=always` case the FORCE_CONFIG comment cites as the reason not to preserve unconditionally) is told the units were created when they were not. The banner sits directly under a comment that states the invariant it now breaks: "THREE branches (review M4): the banner must match what actually happened." This is the failure mode CLAUDE.md memory `feedback-contract-change-sweep` names verbatim — "改动一个命令/动词的契约后，必须全局扫所有调用点——含产品打印给运维的手抄文案".
- **verifier 理由**：Both banners verified. `config_dest` (install.sh:152-161) sets `CONFIG_DEST` to `<path>.new` and appends to `KEPT_CONFIGS` whenever the file exists and `--force-config` was not passed. The agent banner heredoc at :474-494 prints '✔ agent config written to $SESSION_DIR/agent.yaml' with no condition, and `report_kept_configs` runs only afterwards at :496 — so on a kept-config re-run the operator sees a ✔ and a ⚠ about the same path. The broker side is worse as claimed: `grep -n config_dest` shows all four broker artefacts route through it (:1041 nats.conf, :1063/:1089/:1131 the three .service units), while the ENABLED branch at :908 prints '✔ systemd units created and ENABLED for boot' unconditionally. 'ENABLED' is still true (`enable_broker_units` runs on the existing units); 'created' is not. The banner does sit under the comment at :898-901 stating the invariant it breaks. I could not r
- **对修法的批评**：'Make both banners conditional on `KEPT_CONFIGS`' is too coarse and would introduce a new false statement. `KEPT_CONFIGS` is one flat global fed by every `config_dest` call in the run, so on a broker re-run that kept only the Caddyfile while writing all four units, a KEPT_CONFIGS-conditional unit banner would tell the operator the units were kept when they were written. The fix needs per-artefact state — e.g. have `config_dest` also set a `CONFIG_KEPT=0|1` for the call just made, and let each banner branch on the flag belonging to its own file(s). Second constraint the fix must respect: the broker banners are QUOTED heredocs on purpose (the comment at :893-896 explains that this keeps a future `$` or backtick from expanding — or executing — under root). Do not unquote them to interpolate a status; branch to a different literal heredoc instead. The agent banner is already unquoted, so a b
- **建议修法**：Make both banners conditional on `KEPT_CONFIGS`. For the agent: print `✔ agent config written to …/agent.yaml` only when the real path was written, else `✔ new agent config staged at …/agent.yaml.new (existing file kept)`. For the broker: split "units created" from "units ENABLED" — enabling still happened, creation may not have — e.g. `✔ systemd units ENABLED for boot (some may have been kept; see the list below)`.

### C8 · `docs/usage.md:1732`

- **lane**：comments, documented invariants, and repo conventions
- **裁决**：CONFIRMED（原报 MINOR → 订正 MINOR）
- **主张**：§9.13's statement that `exec`/`run` do not enter the exit classifier, and specifically that "77=权限" applies only to broker-RPC commands, is made false for `exec` by this diff — and usage.md was edited elsewhere in the same batch but §9.13 was not.
- **失败场景**：An automation author follows §9.13 and treats `tether exec`'s exit code as the remote process's status ("判别靠『你跑的是哪个命令』，不是值"). A non-member runs it: `execFailureMessage("not_a_member")` now returns `&ExitError{Class: exitNoPerm}`, so the process exits 77 — and the script records "the remote program exited 77" instead of "you are not a member of this session". The same applies to `argv_required`→64 and `node_offline`→64. The change itself is the right one (the plan's J batch is explicitly motivated by §9.13), but the doc half of the contract was not updated, so the section now tells automation the opposite of what the binary does. Note the sentence was already loose for `run` (runFailureMessage has returned an ExitError since batch A); this diff extends the collision to `exec` and makes the "仅限 broker-RPC 命令" clause plainly wrong.
- **verifier 理由**：Verified on both halves. `execFailureMessage` (error_hints.go:446-462) now returns `&ExitError{Class: brokerCodeExitClass(code), ...}` on all three returns — a `+` change in this diff. `not_a_member` → `exitNoPerm` (:96), `node_offline` and `argv_required` → `exitUsage` (:125, :245). `git diff -- docs/usage.md` produces exactly three hunks (around :175, :1573, :1615); §9.13 at :1732 is untouched and still says exec/run '透传远端进程退出码 ... 不入分类器；判别靠"你跑的是哪个命令"，不是值。所以"77=权限"仅限 broker-RPC 命令'. That last clause is now plainly wrong for exec. The reviewer's own caveat is correct and worth keeping: `runFailureMessage` (:407-427) has returned `&ExitError` with a class since batch A, so the sentence was already loose for `run` and this diff only extends the collision — the fix should not be written as 'this batch broke it'. Sharpest evidence the reviewer did not use: execFailureMessage's own new doc c
- **对修法的批评**：The amendment is correct. Two additions. (1) It should state the residual ambiguity plainly rather than only the rule: a remote process that genuinely exits 77 and a permission refusal of the dispatch are now indistinguishable by exit code alone, which is precisely why '判别靠你跑的是哪个命令' stops working — a script that must tell them apart has to parse the `exec: <hint> (<code>)` prefix on stderr. (2) The fix leaves no mechanical guard: the repo has a wire-code↔exit-class gate on `cmd/tether/` but nothing ties either to usage.md, so this section can decay again the next time a call site is classified. If a cheap gate is wanted, the §9.13 reserved-range bullet is a natural candidate for the same treatment as the docs wire-version scan in test/determinism — but that is a separate increment, not a prerequisite for correcting the prose.
- **建议修法**：Amend the §9.13 bullet: `exec`/`run` pass through the remote process's exit code ONLY once dispatch succeeded; a broker/agent REFUSAL of the command is classified like any other broker RPC (64/69/75/77), so a script that needs to distinguish the two must look at stderr (`exec: <hint> (<code>)`) rather than at the code alone. Adjust the "77=权限仅限 broker-RPC 命令" clause accordingly.

### C9 · `docs/architecture.md:860 (§E.6)`

- **lane**：comments, documented invariants, and repo conventions
- **裁决**：PARTIAL（原报 MINOR → 订正 MINOR）
- **主张**：§E.6 still records the v1 decision that argon2's memory-hardness alone bounds PIN-verify throughput and names only the per-IP limiter, while this batch added a process-wide argon2 ceiling to the very file §E.6 cites — on the ground that the §E.6 reasoning was wrong.
- **失败场景**：A future engineer sizing or removing `pinGlobalPerSecond` consults the authority the code points at. §E.6 says the main defence is memory-hard argon2, that attack throughput is bounded by memory bandwidth "与速率计数无关" (unrelated to rate counting), lists only per-IP limiting as auxiliary, and names `internal/authcallout/ratelimit.go` as the implementation. The audit's L1-F3 finding is that this reasoning is wrong for the reason §E.6 never considered — the verify is serialized on nats.go's single subscription goroutine against a 2 s AUTH_TIMEOUT, so ~7-13 queued attempts black-hole every honest CONNECT. The engineer reads §E.6, concludes the global ceiling is redundant belt-and-braces, and removes it. The same batch corrected architecture.md §B.4 for exactly this class of reason (a承重 argument built on a premise the audit falsified) and did not correct §E.6.
- **verifier 理由**：The documentation-gap half is confirmed and the framing half is refuted. Confirmed: `git diff docs/architecture.md` has exactly one hunk (B.4, around :343), so §E.6 at :860 is untouched; it names only per-IP limiting, points at `internal/authcallout/ratelimit.go` as the implementation, and that file now implements two ceilings — so the doc a future engineer consults describes half of what the file it names does. Refuted: the claim that the ceiling was added 'on the ground that the §E.6 reasoning was wrong'. ratelimit.go:80-82, the very comment the reviewer cites, says the opposite — 'The per-IP bucket above is the E.6 anti-brute-force control and it is the right control for THAT threat, but it is not a bound on cost'. E.6's memory-bandwidth sentence is a claim about brute-force SEARCH throughput; L1-F3 is about per-verify LATENCY on a serialized goroutine. Both are true simultaneously; t
- **对修法的批评**：A dated note is the right shape, but it must not be written in the §B.4 '订正 / 曾经是错的' voice the reviewer asks for — that would record a falsification that did not happen and would contradict ratelimit.go:80-82. Write it as a 补充: E.6's PIN-暴力 row addresses guessing throughput and remains correct for that; a second, process-local ceiling (1/s, burst 10) was added for a different threat — auth_callout's serialization on one nats.go subscription goroutine against a 2 s AUTH_TIMEOUT — and the 'no distributed global counting' decision (外审 H2) is unchanged because this ceiling is process-local. Placement is also worth a moment: §E.6 is the table of 'v1 未处理的威胁（明写不管）', so documenting a control that WAS implemented sits oddly there; the note belongs beside the row as a pointer, with the mechanism described where the per-IP limiter is described. Also amend the reviewer's own overstatement before it
- **建议修法**：Add a dated 订正 note under §E.6's PIN 暴力 row in the same shape as the §B.4 one: record that the memory-bandwidth argument omitted the serialization of auth_callout on one goroutine, that a per-process (not cluster-consistent) argon2 ceiling now exists at 1/s burst 10, and that the 'no distributed global counting' decision is unchanged because this ceiling is process-local.

### E1 · `internal/broker/broker.go:2066 (comment at 2060-2063; contract at internal/node/lease.go:158-167)`

- **lane**：Batch E — lease farewell + roster adoption (BC-F1 / BC-F3 / #26-#27)
- **裁决**：PARTIAL（原报 MAJOR → 订正 MINOR）
- **主张**：The release-the-row gate reads ProvisionedNIDs' second return as "the table could not be read", but it is `len(out) > 0` — so a readable-but-EMPTY agent_provisioning silently degrades the whole server-side lease test back to the client's flag, which is exactly the signal BC-F1 says cannot repair a fielded fleet.
- **失败场景**：Single-mode broker with no seeds dir / no auth_callout — which node/lease.go:163-165 itself calls "the STEADY STATE", nothing ever writes agent_provisioning there. Two clones of one image register as `gpu1`; the second is adjudicated and leased `gpu1-02`. That agent is still on v0.5.1, so its farewell carries no `leased_nid` (this is the entire premise of BC-F1). At broker.go:2065 `ProvisionedNIDs` returns `(map[], false)` → `known=false` → `leaseRow = (false && …) || (true && req.LeasedNID) = false` → markNodeOfflineOnRelease is SKIPPED. The row stays ONLINE for the full OfflineAfter window, the allocator still counts the name as occupied, and the agent's own restart is issued `gpu1-03`, then `-04`, … until the suffix space is exhausted and it is refused outright. That is F11's suffix drift, unrepaired, on precisely the old-agent path the patch was written to reach.
- **verifier 理由**：The mechanical half is right and I could not refute it: node/lease.go:183 returns `out, len(out) > 0`, so a readable-but-empty agent_provisioning yields known=false, and broker.go:2066 `leaseRow := (known && !provisioned[nid]) || (!known && req.LeasedNID)` therefore collapses to the client flag, which an N-1 agent never sets (instance.go's LeasedNID is new in this batch). The comment at broker.go:2060-2063 saying 'cannot be read at all' is imprecise; the zero-binding path has no test (every new test calls seedProvisioning).  But the framing 'silently degrades the whole server-side lease test' and MAJOR are both refuted: (1) UNREACHABLE ON ANY CALLOUT BROKER. agent_provisioning is written ONLY by internal/authcallout (handler.go:425 / cluster_forward.go:652,801) — no other writer exists. Under callout, an agent cannot connect at all without a binding, and a lease name is admitted only thr
- **对修法的批评**：The primary fix is wrong and should be rejected. Falling back to the bare shape test `isLease` on readable-but-empty reinstates exactly the fail-open pattern round 1 adjudicated as the root of the F1/F9/F2 family: `gpu-01 gpu-02 gpu-03` is this repo's own example fleet (lease.go:149-152), so on any callout-less broker a genuinely configured `gpu-02` would take its own row OFFLINE (and proxy_ready=0, lease.go:227) on every clean shutdown — violating the invariant TestAFarewellNeverTakesAConfiguredDevicesRowOffline states, while that test would stay green because it seeds a binding (known=true). The finding also concedes it widens E2's cold-map hole; adopting a change that is only safe once another open MAJOR is fixed is backwards. The API change is also disproportionate: ProvisionedNIDs has 5 call sites (upgrade.go:140, exec.go:257, exec.go:526, broker.go:2065, broker.go:2810) that all de
- **建议修法**：Separate the two facts at the source: give node.ProvisionedNIDs a third return (or a sibling `ProvisionedNIDsErr`) distinguishing "query failed" from "query succeeded, set empty". Then at broker.go:2066 fall back to `req.LeasedNID` ONLY on a genuine query error, and on readable-but-empty use the shape test (`isLease`) — which is what configuredBasename already does on !known and what the comment claims to mirror. Caveat to state explicitly if that is adopted: on a no-callout broker a genuinely-configured `gpu-02` would then take its own row OFFLINE on its own clean shutdown; that is benign in itself (the device IS stopping, and the heartbeat restores it) but it widens what E2's cold-map hole

### E3 · `internal/proc/proc.go:199-206 (comment); defeated at internal/broker/exec.go:145 and :191`

- **lane**：Batch E — lease farewell + roster adoption (BC-F1 / BC-F3 / #26-#27)
- **裁决**：CONFIRMED（原报 MINOR → 订正 MINOR）
- **主张**：The new sid fence is real on the write, but its comment claims an indistinguishability the only caller does not provide: handleProcEvent's audit-dedup pre-reads call the UNSCOPED proc.Get before the fenced writer is ever reached, so the ack still discriminates a foreign pid from an unknown one.
- **失败场景**：A member of session `other` publishes on its own legal subject `ev.other.other-1.<pid>.started` with an arbitrary pid. exec.go:145 runs `proc.Get(b.read().SQL(), pid)` with no sid, so ANY existing row in ANY session short-circuits to ack `already_recorded`; a pid that exists nowhere proceeds and acks `recorded` / `node_missing`. That is a complete cross-session pid-existence oracle over pids the fence was added to hide, reachable without a single write. The exit arm is the same shape one notch narrower: exec.go:191's unscoped Get answers `already_exited` for a foreign EXITED pid where the fenced writer would have said `unknown_pid`. (No cross-session write escapes — MarkExited's predicate holds, and recordProc inserts under the subject's own sid — so this is disclosure, not corruption. The residual write concern, that an attacker who somehow learns a pid before its own start event lands could claim the global PRIMARY KEY and make the victim's later exit ack terminally as unknown_pid, is gated behind guessing a 26-char ULID and is not practical.)
- **verifier 理由**：Every factual claim checks out and I could not refute the core observation. proc.Get (proc.go:217-226) selects `WHERE pid = ?` with no sid; handleProcEvent calls it unscoped at exec.go:145 and exec.go:191 and acks BEFORE the fenced writer is reached, so `already_recorded` / `already_exited` do discriminate a foreign pid from an unknown one, while the fenced MarkExited path would answer unknown_pid for both. The comment at proc.go:199-206 is therefore too broad as written. No test covers the cross-session shape (the dedup assertions at internal/broker/proc_event_ack_test.go:135/158 are same-session), and the reviewer is right that the write fence itself holds (MarkExited's UPDATE and its existence probe are both sid-scoped; recordProc inserts under the subject's own sid). What I would push back on is weight, and the reviewer already grades it MINOR, so this is agreement with a narrower ra
- **对修法的批评**：The preferred fix does not achieve what it claims for the started arm. `processes.pid` is a global PRIMARY KEY (0001_init.sql:46) and proc.Insert is a plain INSERT (proc.go:117-126), so with `existing.SID == sid` added at exec.go:145 a foreign pid no longer short-circuits — it reaches recordProc, hits the PK constraint, and is acked `store_error`, which is still perfectly distinguishable from `recorded`. So the comment at proc.go:199-206 would STILL be false after the change, and worse: store_error is an OK:false code, so a courier that legitimately hit it would retry forever rather than settling. Genuinely closing that arm needs the two cases to return the SAME code (or an INSERT OR IGNORE shape), which the finding does not propose. The exit-arm half does work (foreign EXITED -> markProcExited -> scoped probe -> ErrNotFound -> unknown_pid), at the cost that in cluster mode those message
- **建议修法**：Scope both pre-reads so the handler's answer matches what the fenced writer would say. `existing` already carries `.SID`, so the smallest change is to add `&& existing.SID == sid` to both conditions at exec.go:145 and :191 (a foreign pid then falls through to markProcExited and gets the same `unknown_pid` the fence produces); cleaner is a `proc.GetInSession(db, sid, pid)` used by both. Then the comment at proc.go:199-206 becomes true as written. If instead the pre-reads are deliberately left global, the comment must be narrowed to say the WRITER is scoped while the handler's dedup ack is not, and say why that is acceptable.

### F-T2 · `internal/broker/xfer_inflight.go:888`

- **lane**：transfer subsystem (batch F: BT-F1..F5 + #57)
- **裁决**：CONFIRMED（原报 MAJOR → 订正 MINOR）
- **主张**：The safety argument the new single-mode publish rests on is factually wrong: JetStream dedups on the Nats-Msg-Id header, never on payload bytes, and publishAudit sets no msg-id — the repo documents this in two other files.
- **失败场景**：Single broker, a start-only ledger row past timeout+slack. finalizeStrandedXfers synthesizes the terminal; commitSyntheticTransferTerminal publishes it via publishAudit and returns true; consumeXferLedgerRow then fails (EROFS after an ext4 error-remount, or a momentarily unmounted /var/lib) — xfer_inflight.go:799 logs a Warn and CONTINUES. The next pass re-reads the same row, re-synthesizes the byte-identical record and publishes it again. Nothing collapses these: history-<sid> carries Duplicates=AuditDedupWindow but the publisher stamps no msg-id, so each is a distinct stored message. One duplicate `failed/home_broker_restart` per XferReapInterval accumulates for as long as the directory stays unwritable; because EnsureHistoryStream sets MaxBytes=HistoryMaxBytesPerSession with Discard=DiscardNew, a long-running flood eventually makes the session's audit stream REFUSE all new audit rather than evict old. In cluster mode the raft reqID ledger collapses the repeat; single mode has nothing.
- **verifier 理由**：The factual core is entirely correct. broker.go:749-763: publishAudit calls b.js.Publish(ctx, subject, payload) with no jetstream.PublishOpt. Grep for WithMsgID across internal/ returns exactly one non-test hit, audit_publisher.go:412 (the cluster re-derivable publisher). internal/jsstream/replicas.go:24 states in-tree 'INERT in build-and-prove production (the live publishAudit sets no Nats-Msg-Id, so no dedup happens)' and jsstream.go:153 repeats it on the Duplicates field itself. The new comment at xfer_inflight.go:888-893 nonetheless asserts 'JetStream's own duplicate window collapses it'. That sentence is false, and it is a `+` line in this batch's own diff. The re-publish loop is also real: at the synthesize site, a consumeXferLedgerRow error logs a Warn and does NOT return, so the row survives and each later pass re-marshals and re-publishes the byte-identical record. TWO OVERREACH
- **对修法的批评**：The PRIMARY proposed fix does not fix the stated scenario, and this is decisive. jsstream.AuditDedupWindow is 2 minutes; XferReapInterval defaults to 5 minutes (broker.go:825-826). Consecutive re-publish passes are therefore 5 minutes apart — OUTSIDE the dedup window — so adding WithMsgID(TransferRecordReqID(rec)) would collapse nothing in the exact failure the finding describes, while making the comment true only in a vacuous sense. Widening Duplicates to cover it means editing a constant that jsstream.go documents as the SSOT shared with D9's re-derivable publisher and calibrated against election latency — far out of batch F's scope. Two further objections: publishAudit is the single publisher for ALL audit kinds, so threading an optional msg-id through it changes a shared surface for one caller; and the 'sibling publishAuditDeduped' would add a *Broker method, which test/architecture/
- **建议修法**：The reqID is already content-addressed and already computed in this file — pass it: `b.js.Publish(ctx, subj, payload, jetstream.WithMsgID(xferaudit.TransferRecordReqID(rec)))` (threaded through publishAudit as an optional msg-id, or a sibling publishAuditDeduped). That makes the comment's claim true within AuditDedupWindow. If that is judged out of scope, the comment must be corrected to say there is NO dedup in single mode and that the bound is 'one duplicate per reap interval while the ledger row cannot be unlinked'.

### F-T5 · `internal/broker/xfer_inflight_test.go:207`

- **lane**：transfer subsystem (batch F: BT-F1..F5 + #57)
- **裁决**：CONFIRMED（原报 MINOR → 订正 MINOR）
- **主张**：A pre-existing test whose NAME and doc comment now assert the opposite of what batch F ships survived the change untouched, and still passes — so it pins a case that no longer exists in production under a name that reads like the production one.
- **失败场景**：TestXferInflightNoLedgerWithoutClusterDataDir's doc says 'single-broker mode (ClusterDataDir unset) writes NO ledger — its audit publish is best-effort by design, and #57's durable ledger is cluster-mode only.' Every clause of that is now false for a production single broker (which always has DBPath set). It stays green only because the fixture also leaves DBPath empty, i.e. it is really testing an in-memory test broker. The next person who greps 'does a single broker have a ledger' finds this test, reads its name and its doc, and concludes 'no' — then reasons about the single-mode paths (including F-T1's) on a false premise. The file is not in the batch F diff at all.
- **verifier 理由**：Verified on every point. `git diff --name-only` does not list internal/broker/xfer_inflight_test.go, so the file is untouched by batch F. Lines 206-221 read exactly as quoted: the doc asserts 'single-broker mode (ClusterDataDir unset) writes NO ledger — its audit publish is best-effort by design, and #57's durable ledger is cluster-mode only', and the fixture is `b.cfg = Config{Logger: silentLogger(), Now: time.Now}` with neither ClusterDataDir nor DBPath, so it stays green while testing an in-memory broker rather than a production single one. The contradiction is direct and in-repo: writeXferInflight's replacement comment (xfer_inflight.go:298-306, a `+` block in this diff) says the old excuse 'single-broker audit publish is best-effort anyway' 'was FALSE and it is what kept #57 switched off in the shape it mattered most'. So the batch corrected the excuse in the production file and lef
- **对修法的批评**：Correct, and the added positive half doubles as F-T4(b)'s missing wiring pin, which is a genuine saving. One cost the reviewer did not price: renaming a test function is not free in this repo. test/determinism/testdata/test_function_inventory.txt carries `internal/broker/xfer_inflight_test.go: TestXferInflightNoLedgerWithoutClusterDataDir` at line 1622, the golden is only-increasing, and `-update-test-inventory` refuses deletions — so the rename requires a hand-edit of the golden plus a reason in the commit message (CLAUDE.md §5 gate table). That friction is the gate working as designed, not an objection, but it should be planned rather than discovered. The proposed new name is fine under test/determinism/test_naming_test.go (it is a subject name, not a process-event name). Keep the file where it is: xfer_inflight_test.go is already named after the unit under test.
- **建议修法**：Rename to something like TestXferInflightNoLedgerWithoutAnyDurablePath, rewrite the doc to say what it now pins ('an in-memory broker with neither a data dir nor a DB path keeps no ledger'), and add the positive half in the same function: with DBPath set and ClusterDataDir empty, the dir is non-empty and writeXferInflight/finalizeStrandedXfers actually do work. That second half is also the missing wiring pin from F-T4(b).

### F-T6 · `internal/broker/xfer_inflight.go:493`

- **lane**：transfer subsystem (batch F: BT-F1..F5 + #57)
- **裁决**：CONFIRMED（原报 MINOR → 订正 MINOR）
- **主张**：Four comments in the changed files describe the pre-change world; one of them is the load-bearing justification for the bug in F-T1.
- **失败场景**：(1) xfer_inflight.go:493-495: 'In production the check is always available (the ledger dir exists only in cluster mode, where b.cl is wired), so this branch costs nothing there.' Exactly inverted by this batch: in a single-broker production install the check is NEVER available and the branch costs everything — it is the branch that permanently retains every staged terminal (F-T1). A reader auditing that code is told, in the file itself, that the state cannot arise. (2) xfer_inflight.go:246 and :249-250: 'Returns false when nothing was staged (no ledger in single-broker mode...)' and 'applicable=false means this deployment keeps no ledger at all (single-broker mode), which is NOT a failure' — false as of the same commit; applicable=false now means only 'no DBPath and no data dir', i.e. a test broker. (3) transfer.go:202: 'In cluster mode every put() writes the same strings to disk through temp+write+fsync+rename+fsync(dir)' — written in the batch that made single mode do it too, so BT-F5's own cost rationale misdescribes the deployment it now also applies to (and understates the change: single brokers went from zero disk I/O on the push/pull handler to two fsyncs per request, on exactly the slow-disk boxes). (4) transfer.go:192 names the constant `transferSHA256Len`; the declared constant is `transferSHA256MaxLen`.
- **verifier 理由**：All four verified against the source and the diff. (1) xfer_inflight.go:493-495 says 'In production the check is always available (the ledger dir exists only in cluster mode, where b.cl is wired), so this branch costs nothing there' — inverted by line 84, and it is the in-file justification for the branch that causes F-T1. (2) :246 and :249-250 say 'no ledger in single-broker mode' and 'applicable=false means this deployment keeps no ledger at all (single-broker mode)' — now applicable=false means only 'no DBPath and no ClusterDataDir', i.e. an in-memory test broker. (3) transfer.go:202 'In cluster mode every put() writes the same strings to disk through temp+write+fsync+rename+fsync(dir)' is a `+` line in this very diff, and writeLedgerRecord now runs in single mode too; the reviewer's added observation is also right — single brokers went from zero disk I/O on the push/pull handler to t
- **对修法的批评**：Right on all four, with one sequencing constraint the reviewer did not state: (1) must NOT be rewritten on its own. 'State the real rule — the check is available only in cluster mode, and single mode must therefore take the replay path' describes the code AFTER F-T1's fix; writing it before that fix lands would document the current bug as intended behaviour and make the next auditor stop looking. Bundle it into F-T1's edit. For (3), the correction should keep the head-of-line-blocking argument (which is the paragraph's point) and add the newly-true fact as its own sentence rather than folding it away: a single broker now pays two fsyncs per push/pull request on the NATS callback goroutine, and single brokers are exactly the small/slow-disk boxes this sweep exists to protect. (4) is a pure typo fix with no gate exposure.
- **建议修法**：Rewrite (1) to state the real rule — the check is available only in cluster mode, and single mode must therefore take the replay path (which is F-T1's fix, so the comment and the code get corrected together). Rewrite (2) to 'no ledger only when neither ClusterDataDir nor DBPath is set — an in-memory broker'. Change (3) to 'in every mode with a durable path (cluster data dir, or the DB's directory since the 2026-09-02 audit)'. Fix the constant name in (4).

### F-T7 · `internal/broker/broker.go:1364`

- **lane**：transfer subsystem (batch F: BT-F1..F5 + #57)
- **裁决**：CONFIRMED（原报 MINOR → 订正 MINOR）
- **主张**：The eager boot finalize is still gated on b.clusterMode, so the crash-loop case its own comment exists for is not covered on the single brokers #57 was just extended to.
- **失败场景**：Single broker with a stranded start row. The comment at broker.go:1354-1356 says this call exists 'so a broker that crash-loops faster than XferReapInterval still closes dangling start rows (the periodic pass is anchored at start+interval)'. With the clusterMode gate, a single broker that restarts every 2 minutes (a real supervisor/OOM loop) never reaches the periodic 5-minute pass and never runs the eager one either, so its dangling start rows are never closed — the precise gap the eager call was added to remove, now reachable in the deployment #57 was just enabled for.
- **verifier 理由**：Verified. broker.go:1364 is `if b.clusterMode {` wrapping the eager finalizeStrandedXfers, and the comment two lines above (1354-1356) does justify the call by the crash-loop case: 'so a broker that crash-loops faster than XferReapInterval still closes dangling start rows (the periodic pass is anchored at start+interval)'. finalizeStrandedXfers itself opens with `dir := b.xferInflightDir(); if dir == "" { return 0, nil }`, so the mode gate buys nothing the nil-dir check does not already give. The gap is real and newly reachable: with #57 now live on single brokers, a single broker in a supervisor/OOM restart loop shorter than the 5-minute reap interval never reaches either pass. MINOR is correct — it needs a pathological restart loop to bite, and the periodic pass covers every ordinary restart.
- **对修法的批评**：Safe, and safer than the reviewer argues. Two facts confirm it: the JetStream probe at broker.go:1197-1202 runs well before line 1364, so a single-mode boot finalize publishes through JetStream rather than the lossy core fallback; and forEachLedgerRecord treats os.IsNotExist on the directory as an empty scan (no MkdirAll on the read path), so a single broker that has never staged a row does one ReadDir and returns 0 — no directory is created as a side effect of booting. The PLACEMENT comment is about ordering and must stay, as the reviewer says. One ordering constraint: land this AFTER F-T1. With F-T1 unfixed, removing the gate widens F-T1's reach rather than closing anything — every boot would add one more nil-cl Warn per staged row on top of the 5-minute cadence, and still finalize nothing.
- **建议修法**：Drop the `if b.clusterMode` wrapper (keep the placement-after-admin-socket comment, which is about ordering, not about mode). The function is already a no-op when there is no ledger.

### F-T8 · `internal/broker/xfer_inflight.go:891`

- **lane**：transfer subsystem (batch F: BT-F1..F5 + #57)
- **裁决**：CONFIRMED（原报 MINOR → 订正 MINOR）
- **主张**：In single mode with b.js == nil, commitSyntheticTransferTerminal reports a COMMIT for a fire-and-forget core publish, so the caller unlinks the only durable record of the transfer — violating the function's own stated contract.
- **失败场景**：publishAudit falls back to nc.Publish when b.js is nil (broker.go:759-763). nats.go's Publish writes into the client's pending buffer and returns nil even while the connection is reconnecting, so it is not evidence that anything left the process. commitSyntheticTransferTerminal returns true, and finalizeStrandedXfers then calls deleteXferObject + consumeXferLedgerRow (xfer_inflight.go:797-799), destroying the ledger row. The function's doc header says the opposite: 'Returns false when the sink is not yet attached (never delete the ledger on an unconfirmed emit).' Reachability: the JetStream boot probe (broker.go:1196-1200) uses a 1s AccountInfo timeout and is NEVER retried, so a single slow boot leaves b.js nil for the whole process lifetime while ledger rows written by the previous incarnation (when JS was up) sit on disk waiting to be 'committed' into a core publish nobody is subscribed to.
- **verifier 理由**：Verified, including the part that makes it a genuine contract violation rather than a judgement call. The diff shows the batch changed the guard (`if fwd == nil` -> `if fwd == nil && b.cl != nil`) and added the single-mode arm, but left the function's doc header at :869 untouched: 'Returns false when the sink is not yet attached (never delete the ledger on an unconfirmed emit).' In single mode the sink is never attached and the function now returns true, so the header is self-contradicting as of this commit. publishAudit (broker.go:759-763) does fall back to nc.Publish when b.js is nil, and nats.go's Publish buffers and returns nil while reconnecting, so a nil error there is not evidence anything left the process; finalizeStrandedXfers then calls deleteXferObject + consumeXferLedgerRow and destroys the row. Reachability is as described: the probe at broker.go:1197-1200 is a single Accoun
- **对修法的批评**：The direction is right but the proposed line collides with an adjudicated decision elsewhere in this same audit. `if b.js == nil` is a bare read of the field that plan §-1 A names as a data race (BC-F2: 'b.js data race — nc/tunnelSrv 早已因同样理由改 atomic.Pointer, js 被漏掉'), whose fix is atomic.Pointer plus package-level free functions rather than new *Broker methods. b.js is still a plain jetstream.JetStream field at broker.go:398 today, so adding this read now creates one more site BC-F2 has to convert, and writing it as a new *Broker helper would also push against the exact `type-methods internal/broker.Broker 285` golden the same batch's brokerUptime was written as a free function to avoid. Land it as a package-level predicate taking *Broker, or after BC-F2, and go through whatever accessor BC-F2 introduces. Second: the fix converts F-T8 into F-T1's shape for that deployment — a broker whos
- **建议修法**：In the single-mode arm, require JetStream before treating the publish as a commit: `if b.js == nil { Warn(...); return false }` before the publishAudit call, so the ledger row is retained for a later pass instead of being traded for an unacknowledged core publish. This also makes the new comment's premise ('a single broker supporting tier-B necessarily has JetStream') an enforced precondition rather than an assumption.

### G-3 · `internal/broker/clusterdrain.go:173-174`

- **lane**：cluster operations (batch G: #13/#14/#15/#16) — retire step bounding, post-removal readiness gate, drain phase ordering, alert-ack validation
- **裁决**：CONFIRMED（原报 MINOR → 订正 MINOR）
- **主张**：A comment the same commit falsified: the step-2b block still tells the reader the convergence gate is "Ordered BEFORE the phase bump", which is precisely the ordering L-BCO-F2 inverted twelve lines below.
- **失败场景**：A future editor reads DrainNode top-to-bottom. At line 173-174 they are told, in the imperative voice this repo uses for load-bearing invariants, that the gate must come before the phase bump and that violating it causes an outage ("removing a node while exposes still point at it is the very outage this gate prevents"). Twelve lines later the code does the opposite. The two most likely outcomes are both bad: they "restore" the ordering and silently undo the fix (TestDrainBumpsThePhaseBeforeWaitingForConvergence would catch that one), or — worse, because nothing catches it — they trust the stale comment when reasoning about a neighbouring change and conclude the drain still leaves phase=VOTER on timeout, which is exactly the state the fix removed.
- **verifier 理由**：Verified against the diff. clusterdrain.go:173-174 still reads `Ordered BEFORE the phase bump and (for retire) before RemoveServer: removing a node while exposes still point at it is the very outage this gate prevents.`, and `git diff internal/broker/clusterdrain.go` shows that block was not touched while awaitHomeConvergence was moved from before setPhase to after it. The gate the sentence names — the blocking WAIT — is now second, so the sentence is false about the thing it describes. Two corrections that do not overturn it: (1) the pending-SET derivation (pendingRetireConvergence) is still before the phase bump, so the block is stale rather than wholly wrong; (2) the `(for retire)` clause was ALREADY stale before this commit — DrainNode hard-refuses retire=true at lines 100-105 (the A13 removal), so 'a comment the same commit falsified' is only half accurate. Mitigating: the replaceme
- **对修法的批评**：The proposed rewrite is correct and I would take it. Two refinements: say explicitly that the pending set is derived pre-bump so it is computed from the pre-bump roster (that is the part still worth ordering), and when pointing at the retire ladder note that driveRetire derives its OWN pending set with the F3 `since = op.CreatedAt` scoping — this fleet-wide (zero-time) derivation is not the one that gates RemoveServer, so the cross-reference must not read as 'the same set'. Deleting `(for retire)` is right, but flag it as a pre-existing staleness rather than fallout of this batch.
- **建议修法**：Rewrite 173-174 to describe what is actually ordered where: the convergence SET is derived before the phase bump (so `pending` is computed from the pre-bump roster), the WAIT deliberately follows it, and the property being preserved is that the wait still gates the retire ladder's RemoveServer — which lives in driveRetire, not here. Delete the `(for retire)` clause entirely.

### G-4 · `internal/broker/cluster_operation_controller.go:943-954 (and internal/broker/retire_step_bounds_test.go:80-86)`

- **lane**：cluster operations (batch G: #13/#14/#15/#16) — retire step bounding, post-removal readiness gate, drain phase ordering, alert-ack validation
- **裁决**：CONFIRMED（原报 MINOR → 订正 MINOR）
- **主张**：blockNow's entire stated justification rests on a retry interval that is off by two orders of magnitude: it claims the five bounded retries are "each one 10 minutes apart" / "fifty minutes", but the controller ticks every 5 seconds, so opMaxAttempts costs about 25 seconds.
- **失败场景**：Not a runtime failure — a false load-bearing comment that misprices a design decision, in a repo whose review process reads these comments as the argument of record. Anyone re-litigating whether blockNow (an immediate, no-retries-spent BLOCKED path, i.e. a second routing rule with its own classification table that has to be kept correct) is worth its complexity will read "fifty minutes before the operator learns anything" and conclude obviously yes. The true figure is ~25 seconds, at which point the honest argument for blockNow is only "it names the deterministic cause verbatim instead of prefixing it with (attempt 5/5)" — still defensible, but a much smaller claim, and one that would have been weighed differently.
- **verifier 理由**：The arithmetic is wrong by ~120x and I could not find a reading that rescues it. observability.go:220 observeTickInterval = 5s; clusterwrite.go:485 GoEvery("observe", observeTickInterval, runObserveLoop); observability.go:278 calls driveLeaderMaintenance() on every tick when leader; driveLeaderMaintenance -> driveInFlightOperations (438-471) loops every non-terminal op and calls driveOne unconditionally. I read driveOne (493-542) in full: it re-reads the op, reads the substrate, and dispatches — there is no per-op backoff, no next-drive-at, no rate limit; the only skip is opFrozenByUpgradeLock. blockAfterAttempts embeds '(attempt N/5)' so recordOpError's change-gate does not suppress successive ticks. blockNow's two triggers (ErrRebuildOffExposes, ErrNoMigrationTarget) both come from migrateExposes, which runs on every tick in OpStateRehomeExposes. So five attempts is ~20s wall (t=0,5,10
- **对修法的批评**：The correction is right and the restated justification is the honest one. Two additions worth folding in: (1) express the figure symbolically (opMaxAttempts × observeTickInterval) rather than as a literal, since this repo already has a gate for exactly this failure mode — test/determinism's product-timing sleep guard exists because comments calibrated against leaseGrantWindow went stale for six weeks when the constant changed; a hard-coded '25 seconds' will rot the same way if observeTickInterval moves. (2) The strongest surviving argument for blockNow is not only 'verbatim and immediate': migrateExposes performs raft writes, so four extra re-issues of a refusal that cannot succeed are not free, and a rebuild-OFF refusal is partial-mutation-adjacent. Say that instead of leaning on the interval.
- **建议修法**：Correct both comments to ~5 s per attempt / ~25 s to BLOCKED, and restate blockNow's justification on the ground that actually survives the correction: a deterministic cause should be reported to the operator verbatim and immediately rather than buried under four misleading "(attempt N/5)" rewrites of last_error, and re-issuing an irreversible-adjacent side effect that provably cannot succeed is not free regardless of interval.

### G-5 · `internal/broker/cluster_operation_controller.go:927-940 with 964-982 and 1170/1386`

- **lane**：cluster operations (batch G: #13/#14/#15/#16) — retire step bounding, post-removal readiness gate, drain phase ordering, alert-ack validation
- **裁决**：CONFIRMED（原报 MINOR → 订正 MINOR）
- **主张**：Batch G widened one shared per-op attempt counter from a single retire site to nine spanning the whole ladder, but did not widen the reset points, so a retire is BLOCKED on its fifth CUMULATIVE failure across five DIFFERENT steps — each of which succeeded on retry and made forward progress.
- **失败场景**：3-voter cluster with ordinary leadership churn (a route flap, a broker restart, a `cluster transfer`). A retire of ctl-2 walks NO_NEW_HOME → REHOME_EXPOSES (which alone can re-drive for up to opRehomeConvergeTimeout = 10 min = ~120 ticks while a NATted agent re-dials) → STREAMS_AT_TARGET → LEADER_TRANSFERRED → RAFT_REMOVED. Each of those states issues a raft Propose or a raft membership call. Leadership is lost between driveInFlightOperations' `IsLeader()` check and the Propose five separate times over the op's life — once at "raise broker_draining", once at "phase->DRAINING", once at "stream readiness", once at "phase->RETIRING", once at "roster delete" — and every one of them self-heals on the very next tick and the ladder advances. On the fifth, opAttempts[opID] hits 5 and the op goes BLOCKED, fencing the whole membership plane behind `assertNoActiveOp` until an operator runs `cluster ops confirm`, which then restarts the ladder from DRAIN_REQUESTED. Before batch G, retire's only consumer of this counter was "converge client seeds after retire" — which sits AFTER toNatsRolledOut's clearOpAttempts — so the counter was effectively step-local for the whole ladder and every one of these transients self-healed silently.
- **verifier 理由**：The mechanism is exactly as described: blockAfterAttempts (927-940) keys a.opAttempts on op.OpID alone with no step identity, and `git show HEAD:` confirms that before this batch retire's only consumer was line 1126 'converge client seeds after retire', while the diff routes nine driveRetire sites (raise broker_draining, migrate exposes, check data-plane convergence, phase->DRAINING, stream readiness, transfer leadership off, phase->RETIRING, raft RemoveServer, roster delete) through retireStepFailed -> blockAfterAttempts. So one budget of five now spans DRAIN_REQUESTED..RAFT_REMOVED with no refill on forward progress. Two corrections to the evidence, neither fatal: (1) clearOpAttempts has FOUR call sites, not two — 253 (ConfirmOp), 776 (driveJoin terminal), 1170 (driveRetire's NATS_ROLLED_OUT, after seed converge) and 1388 (toNatsRolledOut); for a retire both 1170 and 1388 sit at or aft
- **对修法的批评**：Option (a) as written introduces a worse bug. `a.opAttempts[op.OpID+"/"+op.OpState]` leaves clearOpAttempts (984-988) deleting only the BARE opID key, so: (i) `cluster ops confirm` would no longer reset the budget — the confirmed retry would re-BLOCK after a single further failure in that state, directly defeating the adjudicated F2 decision that a confirmed retry gets a fresh window; and (ii) the map leaks one entry per (op,state) for the process's lifetime. Composite keying REQUIRES clearOpAttempts to become a prefix sweep, and force_single_finalize_test.go:101-111 asserts on the literal key "op-fs", so that guard has to be re-read too. Option (b) (clear inside transition when ToState != FromState) is cleaner but two things must be said out loud rather than smuggled: it must clear only AFTER the Propose succeeds (transition can fail, and clearing on a failed transition would make a ste
- **建议修法**：Key the counter on the step, not just the op: `a.opAttempts[op.OpID+"/"+op.OpState]` (or clear it inside `transition()` whenever ToState != FromState, which is the same thing expressed once). Both keep TestARetriedRetireStepEndsInBlocked passing unchanged, and both restore the pre-batch-G property that forward progress refills the budget. Then add the case the current guard misses: five failures spread over five different states must NOT block, five failures in one state must.

### G-6 · `internal/broker/alertadmin.go:36 with internal/broker/cluster_health.go:218-222 and internal/broker/cluster_forward.go:686-697`

- **lane**：cluster operations (batch G: #13/#14/#15/#16) — retire step bounding, post-removal readiness gate, drain phase ordering, alert-ack validation
- **裁决**：PARTIAL（原报 MINOR → 订正 MINOR）
- **主张**：The new 256-byte dedup_key cap is enforced only on the NATS ack responder, and is smaller than the key the product's own raise path can mint — so an operator can raise a manual alert that can never be acked, and the cap is absent from the bake surface where this repo's sibling verb deliberately put it.
- **失败场景**：(a) Un-ackable alert: `tether alert raise --kind manual --severity severe --message ... --label <300 chars>` is accepted — handleAlertRaise validates the label against maxAlertTextLen=4096 (alertadmin.go:74) and mints dedup_key `manual:<300 chars>` = 307 bytes. It shows up in `alert ls` and in the severe banner. `tether alert ack manual:<300 chars>` is then refused forever with "alert: dedup_key is too long (307 bytes; max 256)". `alert clear` still works (it validates at 4096), so the operator's only exit is to clear an alert they wanted to acknowledge, not clear. (b) Missing bake-surface enforcement: the leader-side arm re-checks existence but never length, so a forwarded VerbAlertAck envelope — from an N-1 broker that predates this batch, which is exactly the mixed-version window requirements §6.7 promises to support — carrying that same 307-byte key passes the existence check (the alert really exists) and commits a 307-byte alert_acks row into raft, replicated everywhere and replayed on every restore. The stated threat the cap exists to close is therefore still open through the one path a rolling upgrade guarantees will be used.
- **verifier 理由**：Half (a) is fully confirmed and I could not refute it: alertadmin.go:36 caps a dedup key at 256; alertadmin.go:74 validates --label at maxAlertTextLen=4096; alertadmin.go:81-84 mints cluster.DedupKeyNode(manual, label) = "manual:"+label (alert_ops.go:60), so this system can mint a ~4103-byte key; cmd/tether/alert.go:86 exposes --label as free-form with no client cap; cluster_health.go:224 is the only site applying maxAlertDedupKeyLen. So `alert raise --label <300 chars>` succeeds and `alert ack manual:<300 chars>` is refused forever with 'too long', leaving `alert clear` as the only exit. The precedent claim is also exact — PlanAlertRaise (alert_ops.go:76-88) self-validates kind and severity at the bake surface with the stated rationale, PlanAlertAck (119-135) validates nothing, and cluster_forward.go:686-697 adds only the existence SELECT. Half (b)'s FRAMING is overstated, which is why
- **对修法的批评**：Three problems with the fix as proposed. (1) 'Move the length/NUL/UTF-8 check into PlanAlertAck itself' cannot reuse maxAlertDedupKeyLen — that constant lives in internal/broker and internal/cluster must not import it (layering table); the constant has to move to internal/cluster or be duplicated, and duplication is how two bounds drift apart, which is the very defect being fixed. (2) More importantly, capping at the ACK bake surface WITHOUT capping the RAISE bake surface enshrines bug (a) instead of fixing it: PlanAlertRaise validates kind and severity but not dedup_key length, so a >256 key can still be minted into `alerts` by any raise caller and would then be permanently un-ackable at the leader. A consistent fix puts the SAME bound on dedupKey in PlanAlertRaise and PlanAlertAck, one constant, with the responder keeping the fast well-worded rejection as a pre-filter. (3) 'Raise maxAl
- **建议修法**：Pick one bound and enforce it at the bake surface. Either raise maxAlertDedupKeyLen to cover the largest key the raise path can mint, or (better) cap --label so that "manual:"+label always fits in 256 and reject an over-long label at handleAlertRaise with a message naming the real limit. Then move the length/NUL/UTF-8 check into PlanAlertAck itself (or into the VerbAlertAck Propose closure alongside the existence SELECT), keeping the responder check as the fast, well-worded rejection. Extend TestAlertAckRefusesAKeyWithNoAlertBehindIt with an over-long key so the leader-side arm is covered, and add a round-trip case: a key produced by handleAlertRaise must always be accepted by the ack path.

### G-7 · `internal/broker/cluster_forward.go:686-697`

- **lane**：cluster operations (batch G: #13/#14/#15/#16) — retire step bounding, post-removal readiness gate, drain phase ordering, alert-ack validation
- **裁决**：CONFIRMED（原报 MINOR → 订正 MINOR）
- **主张**：The new leader-side existence check reads the leader's local SQLite with no caught-up gate, so it can refuse a legitimate ack for an alert that is committed but not yet applied — the exact hazard this repo already guards against, by name, one file over.
- **失败场景**：ctl-1 raises below_quorum:ctl-3 (leader Propose → applied → committed). Leadership moves to ctl-2 (election, `cluster transfer`, or a restart of ctl-1 — all common in precisely the situation the alert is about). The operator, who saw the alert in `alert ls` a moment earlier, runs `tether alert ack below_quorum:ctl-3`. The responder forwards it to ctl-2, whose Propose closure runs against ctl-2's local DB before ctl-2 has finished replaying its committed tail. `SELECT 1 FROM alerts WHERE dedup_key=?` returns ErrNoRows and the operator is told "alert ack: no alert with dedup_key \"below_quorum:ctl-3\"" for an alert that demonstrably exists and is on their screen. It is self-healing on retry, so the damage is a confusing, wrong-sounding error at a bad moment rather than lost state — which is why I am filing it MINOR rather than higher.
- **verifier 理由**：The mechanism is real and I could not find a gate that closes it. cluster/node.go:440-465 ProposeWithReqID takes applyMu, checks `n.raft.Load().State() != raft.Leader`, then runs the plan closure against n.db — no Barrier, no applied-index gate. cluster_forward.go:564-577 SubscribeClusterApply answers whenever `node.IsLeader()` is true, which in hashicorp/raft becomes true at election, before this term's noop has committed and before the FSM goroutine has applied the committed tail into SQLite. So a freshly elected leader can answer `SELECT 1 FROM alerts WHERE dedup_key=?` with ErrNoRows for a row it simply has not applied, and refuse a legitimate ack with 'no alert with dedup_key %q'. The cited precedent is accurate: reconcile_drain_marker.go:64-71 gates on reaperCaughtUp() with the comment 'a leader still replaying its committed tail can read "no roster row" for a row it simply has not
- **对修法的批评**：The first option is weaker than the finding implies. Node.CaughtUp() (cluster/read.go:149-151) is `CommitIndex() > 0 && RaftAppliedIndex() >= CommitIndex()`, and CommitIndex() is this node's LOCAL raft commit index — on a just-elected leader that has not yet committed its term's noop, the local commit index has not advanced to include entries committed under the previous term, so CaughtUp() can read true during exactly the window it is meant to cover. It narrows the hole, it does not close it. reconcileDrainMarkers tolerates that because it is a periodic reconciler that simply retries next pass; a one-shot operator ack does not have that luxury. The second option (make the refusal name the possibility and tell the operator to retry) is the better buy — two lines, no new failure mode, and it preserves the whole value of the check, which is stopping a typo'd or invented key from becoming a
- **建议修法**：Either gate the negative answer on the leader being caught up (the same predicate reconcileDrainMarkers uses) and treat not-caught-up as "retry", or make the refusal message honest and actionable — name the possibility explicitly, e.g. "no alert with dedup_key %q (if this alert was raised seconds ago and leadership just changed, retry)". The second is a two-line change and preserves the finding's actual value, which is stopping a typo'd or invented key from becoming a permanent replicated row.

### G10 · `internal/broker/xfer_inflight.go:880`

- **lane**：ARE THE NEW GUARDS REAL? (adversarial audit of every test function added/modified by the uncommitted prerelease-audit diff)
- **裁决**：PARTIAL（原报 MINOR → 订正 MINOR）
- **主张**：The new single-mode synthetic-terminal path publishes straight to the audit stream and has no test; its dedup argument is asserted rather than exercised.
- **失败场景**：`commitSyntheticTransferTerminal` on a single broker now calls `b.publishAudit(proto.SubjAuditTransfer(rec.Session), payload)` and returns true — which the caller reads as 'committed, delete the ledger row'. If the determinism the comment relies on ever breaks (a caller stamps `rec.Ts` from a wall clock rather than from `rec.StartedAt + transferTimeoutFor(tier, size)`), every reaper pass emits a fresh terminal for the same transfer and the history stream accumulates duplicates, with the ledger row deleted each time so there is nothing left to reconcile against.
- **verifier 理由**：Half of this is refuted by tests the finding did not look for. The central evidence claim — 'The safety argument is entirely in the comment … Nothing checks that the caller still computes it that way' — is false. TestXferInflightFinalizeOnRecovery (internal/broker/xfer_inflight_test.go:30) asserts exactly that property: `if wantTs := stranded.startedAt.Add(transferTimeoutTierB); !rec.Ts.Equal(wantTs)` with the message 'Ts must be deterministic startedAt+timeout'. That is the precondition the dedup argument rests on, and it is pinned against the ledger row, so the stated failure trigger (a caller stamping rec.Ts from a wall clock) reddens an existing test. Second, the new single-mode branch is not uncovered: TestXferInflightFinalizeRetainsLedgerWhenUncommittable case (a) builds `b := &Broker{transfers: newTransferTracker()}` (so b.cl is nil) and sets `b.transferAuditForwardSync = nil`, so
- **对修法的批评**：Two of the three proposed assertions are redundant with what exists (the Ts determinism is pinned by TestXferInflightFinalizeOnRecovery; the publish-failure-returns-false path is exercised by TestXferInflightFinalizeRetainsLedgerWhenUncommittable case (a)), so adopting the proposal as written would add a third test of an already-guarded property — which is its own small version of the vacuity problem. What is worth writing is narrower: give the single-mode broker a captureJS-style publisher so publishAudit SUCCEEDS, then assert (a) commitSyntheticTransferTerminal returns true, (b) the ledger row is consumed, and (c) two passes over the same row produce byte-identical payloads. Note that (c) needs the second pass to be driven from a re-staged identical ledger row, since the first pass deletes it — calling the method twice directly, as proposed, only proves json.Marshal is deterministic ov
- **建议修法**：Add a test that stages a synthetic terminal on a single-mode broker (`b.cl == nil`, `transferAuditForwardSync == nil`) with a captureJS-style publisher, calls `commitSyntheticTransferTerminal` twice from the same ledger row, and asserts the two published payloads are byte-identical — that is the property the comment relies on, and it is the one a future caller would break. Assert the publish-failure path returns false so the ledger row survives.

### G7 · `cmd/tether/exit_class_family_test.go:119`

- **lane**：ARE THE NEW GUARDS REAL? (adversarial audit of every test function added/modified by the uncommitted prerelease-audit diff)
- **裁决**：CONFIRMED（原报 MINOR → 订正 MINOR）
- **主张**：The alert-ack deadline guard hardcodes the broker's 5s budget as a literal, so raising the broker default silently re-opens CLI-F3 with the guard green — which is the exact failure mode the guard's own message describes.
- **失败场景**：Someone raises the default in `(*broker.Config).ExposeForwardTimeout()` (broker.go:356-361) from 5s to, say, 10s, for an unrelated reason. `alertAckTimeout` stays 6s. TestAlertAckWaitsAtLeastAsLongAsTheBrokerDoes still passes, because it compares 6000ms against its own frozen `const brokerForwardBudget = 5 * 1000`. The ctl is once again the one that gives up first, and reports "no broker forwarded the ack" for an ack that is committing.
- **verifier 理由**：Could not refute. cmd/tether/exit_class_family_test.go:119 is `const brokerForwardBudget = 5 * 1000 // milliseconds` with an `// origin:` comment pointing at internal/broker/broker.go's Config.ExposeForwardTimeout — and that function (broker.go:356-361) returns `5 * time.Second` as a default that nothing couples the test to. `alertAckTimeout = 6 * time.Second` (cmd/tether/alert_gate.go:44). Raising the broker default leaves the test green with the ctl once again giving up first, which is CLI-F3 verbatim. The layering objection does not apply: cmd/tether is package main and serve.go already imports internal/broker, so `(&broker.Config{}).ExposeForwardTimeout()` compiles in the test with no new dependency. The irony the finding points at is real — the failure string it prints is 'A deadline measured against a number you do not control is not a deadline'. MINOR is right: the coupling is one
- **对修法的批评**：Sound and one line. One thing the fix should say out loud so it is not oversold: `(&broker.Config{}).ExposeForwardTimeout()` pins the DEFAULT only. A deployment that sets Config.ExposeForwardTimeoutDur above 6s still out-waits the ctl, and no compile-time guard can see that — so the comment above the assertion should state that the guard tracks the default and that a configured budget larger than alertAckTimeout is an operator-visible tradeoff, not something this test covers. Keep the `alertAckTimeout != alertLsTimeout` assertion as-is; it is the half that catches the original CLI-F3 shape (one constant reused for two different requests).
- **建议修法**：Replace the literal with `(&broker.Config{}).ExposeForwardTimeout()` and compare durations directly. Keep the `alertAckTimeout != alertLsTimeout` assertion. Then a change to the broker default fails this test at the moment it is made, which is the whole point.

### G8 · `cmd/tether/exit_class_family_test.go:66`

- **lane**：ARE THE NEW GUARDS REAL? (adversarial audit of every test function added/modified by the uncommitted prerelease-audit diff)
- **裁决**：CONFIRMED（原报 MINOR → 订正 MINOR）
- **主张**：The abort-refusal scanner walks a hardcoded 5-file list, so a new command file with an unclassified `aborted` refusal is invisible to it.
- **失败场景**：A future `cmd/tether/cluster_shrink.go` (or any new destructive verb) adds `return fmt.Errorf("aborted (type the node_id to confirm)")`. The guard reads only cluster.go, cluster_retire.go, cluster_offline.go, proxy.go and cluster_backup.go, so it reports nothing. The refusal exits 70, which docs/usage.md §9.13 tells automation to retry — CLI-F2 returns in a new file, and the finding's own lesson ("reviewer 把特例当了通例": 10 sites, only 1 classified) is re-learned.
- **verifier 理由**：Could not refute. exit_class_family_test.go:66-68 is a literal five-file list, and the guard has no self-check that would notice it going stale. The finding is also honest about the list being complete today, which I verified: `grep -rn aborted cmd/tether/*.go` shows all 11 error-CONSTRUCTION sites (cluster.go:586/634/860, cluster_retire.go:48/115, cluster_backup.go:111, proxy.go:110, cluster_offline.go:227/386/518/569) inside those five files, with the other hits being an Fprintf in cluster_ops.go:87 and comments in error_hints.go, cluster_join.go and cluster_add_drive.go. So this is a latent staleness gap, not a live hole — MINOR is correct, and the finding correctly notes the guard is otherwise real (the fmt.Errorf-on-the-same-line check does fire on a reverted usageErr).
- **对修法的批评**：Prefer the SELF-CHECK variant over the bare glob the finding puts first. 'aborted' is an ordinary English word and a legitimate non-refusal failure — `fmt.Errorf("grow aborted: %w", err)`, or anything wrapping raft's ErrAbortedByRestore — would be flagged by a whole-directory glob and the natural repair would be to convert a genuinely transient failure into a usageErr, which is a worse outcome than the gap. No such line exists in cmd/tether today, so the glob is safe at this instant, which is exactly why it would be adopted and then quietly become wrong. The self-check the finding offers second ('derive the set of files containing an aborted-plus-fmt.Errorf line and fail when it is not a subset of the list') keeps the intent, forces a new file to be a decision, and does not conscript unrelated error text. Either way the check should key on the refusal SHAPE (a line that both says aborted
- **建议修法**：Drop the list and scan `filepath.Glob("*.go")` minus `_test.go` — the `strings.Contains(line, "aborted")` pre-filter already makes it cheap, and it removes the maintenance obligation entirely. If a fixed list is preferred for speed, add the self-check the rest of this repo's scanners carry: derive the set of files containing "aborted" and fail when it is not a subset of the list, so a new file is a decision rather than a silent gap.

### G9 · `internal/agent/transfer.go:1176`

- **lane**：ARE THE NEW GUARDS REAL? (adversarial audit of every test function added/modified by the uncommitted prerelease-audit diff)
- **裁决**：CONFIRMED（原报 MINOR → 订正 MINOR）
- **主张**：The `--force` mode-preservation change (and its deliberate setuid/setgid drop) has no test on either side of the wire.
- **失败场景**：A future edit changes the mask from `0o777` to `0o7777`, or drops the `unixModeIsRegular` check, or removes the `f.Chmod` entirely. Nothing turns red. In the first case a `push --force` over a setuid-root file hands the uploader whatever privilege the old file carried — the exact scenario the comment says the mask exists to prevent. In the last case a 0755 script silently comes back non-executable, which is the defect L3-F1 was raised for.
- **verifier 理由**：Could not refute. internal/agent/transfer.go:1176 (`_ = f.Chmod(os.FileMode(dst.Mode & 0o777))`, guarded by unix.Fstatat + unixModeIsRegular) and cmd/tether/transfer.go:667 (`_ = f.Chmod(st.Mode().Perm())`) are both new, both carry a security claim in prose ('MASKED TO 0o777, deliberately not 0o7777: setuid, setgid and the sticky bit … re-applying them to bytes that just arrived over the network would be handing an uploader whatever privilege the old file carried'), and neither has any assertion. The six OpenForWriteAtomic call sites in internal/agent/transfer_test.go (592/605/646/682/776/804) never inspect a mode — the only Perm-ish constants in that file are 0o755 arguments to os.Mkdir for fixture directories. So the mask, the unixModeIsRegular gate and the Chmod itself are all revertible in silence. MINOR is right: it is a missing assertion on a correct implementation, and the honest-
- **对修法的批评**：Sound, cheap, and it converts a prose security claim into something mechanical — which is the right instinct for a comment that asserts a privilege property. Two preconditions the test must handle or it will be flaky rather than protective. (1) Platform: the agent-side path is unix.Fstatat/dirFD-based and Linux-only; the assertion belongs beside the existing OpenForWriteAtomic round-trip in internal/agent/transfer_test.go and must not be added to a target that builds on darwin. (2) The setuid arm needs a skip, not a failure, when the PRE-CONDITION cannot be established: creating a 0o4755 file is refused or silently stripped on nosuid mounts and on some filesystems, and a non-root os.Chmod setting the setgid bit on a file whose group the process does not belong to is dropped by the kernel. Verify the fixture actually has ModeSetuid set before asserting the write cleared it, and t.Skip oth
- **建议修法**：Extend internal/agent/transfer_test.go's existing OpenForWriteAtomic round-trip: pre-create the destination 0o755, run the atomic write, assert the result is 0o755; pre-create it 0o4755, assert the result is 0o755 and specifically that `os.ModeSetuid` is clear. Mirror the first case in cmd/tether for `writeLocalAtomic`. Three assertions, and they turn the comment's security claim into something mechanical.

### H-3 · `internal/cluster/snapshot.go:253 (field declared internal/cluster/fsm.go:54-66)`

- **lane**：cluster FSM and locks (batch H: #40/#41/#42) — internal/cluster/{snapshot,fsm,node,offline,membership_ops,lock_lease}.go + restore_identity_test.go, membership_test.go
- **裁决**：CONFIRMED（原报 MINOR → 订正 MINOR）
- **主张**：fsm.localID — the field whose entire justification is that it is "not a copy of anything the restore can damage" — is never set in any test, so the branch that prefers it over the live-DB read has zero coverage and can be deleted with the suite still green.
- **失败场景**：A future refactor deletes `selfID := f.localID` (or the three `localID:` lines at node.go:179, node.go:583, offline.go:508) as dead weight, leaving only the DB fallback. `make test` stays green. The case that then breaks is the repair case the fix exists for: a node already damaged by the old post-install-fixup code has the LEADER's id sitting in its cluster_meta.self_node_id, the fallback reads that damaged value, stamps it back into the staging file, and the node comes up under the leader's raft ServerID — the self-confirming split brain described verbatim in fsm.go:57-63 and snapshot.go:237-244. Nothing detects it.
