- **verifier 理由**：Verified. `grep -rn localID internal/cluster/*_test.go` returns nothing; freshFSM (internal_helpers_test.go:98-107) builds &fsm{db, ro, tmpDir, dbPath, appliers, logger} with no localID, and every Restore call site in the package goes through it (checked all nine: liveness_invariant_test.go:40, restore_identity_test.go:51/102, snapshot_test.go:60/101/133/149/194, crash_invariant_test.go:353 — that last one is `b, _ := freshFSM(...)` too). So only the selfID=="" fallback is ever exercised. Mutation confirms it: replacing `selfID := f.localID; if selfID == ""` with an unconditional live-DB read leaves `go test ./internal/cluster/` green (90.4s, ok). The branch is also the one that runs in production — node.go:179 and offline.go:508 always populate it — so the untested path is the live one and the tested path is the dead one. MINOR is correct: the production code is right, the gap is a guar
- **对修法的批评**：Sound and cheap, with two constraints worth stating so it lands right. The id-taking sibling must go INTO internal/cluster/internal_helpers_test.go, not a new file: the naming freeze (test/determinism/test_naming_test.go) forbids process-named test files and its file-name ledger is pinned at published=0. Budget is a non-issue — structural_budget_test.go:114 skips *_test.go — and a NEW test function is append-only for the test-inventory golden, so nothing has to be hand-edited. One strengthening: also assert the DAMAGED value is gone from the live DB, not just that the result equals localID; a mutant that ignored both and wrote the leader's id would otherwise still need the third arm of the assertion to die. And keep the AST order guard (TestRestoreCorrectsTheStagingFileBeforeInstallingIt) — the new behavioural test does not subsume it, since it cannot see whether restoreFrom calls prepar
- **建议修法**：Add one test in internal/cluster that gives freshFSM's fsm a localID and a live DB whose self_node_id is a DIFFERENT (damaged) value, then Restores a leader's snapshot and asserts the resulting live cluster_meta.self_node_id equals the localID, not the damaged DB value and not the leader's. Name the case for what it repairs (a node already carrying the leader's id from the pre-fix code). Give freshFSM an id-taking sibling rather than changing it, so the existing fallback coverage stays.

### H-4 · `internal/cluster/lock_lease.go:249-266`

- **lane**：cluster FSM and locks (batch H: #40/#41/#42) — internal/cluster/{snapshot,fsm,node,offline,membership_ops,lock_lease}.go + restore_identity_test.go, membership_test.go
- **裁决**：CONFIRMED（原报 MINOR → 订正 MINOR）
- **主张**：The new upgradeMarkerHeldByOther was inserted directly under the existing paragraph documenting upgradeMarkerExists and growMarkerOwnedBy with no blank line, so godoc now attributes a description of two other functions to it, and both of those functions are left undocumented.
- **失败场景**：A reader (or `go doc cluster.upgradeMarkerHeldByOther` internally, or anyone reading top-down) sees the new acquire-guard helper documented as "the ownership predicates a RENEWAL is gated on. A renewal that could run without them would resurrect a lock the orchestrator already released...". That is the opposite role: upgradeMarkerHeldByOther gates an ACQUIRE, is not a renewal predicate, and a renewal gated on it would be wrong (PlanRenewUpgradeLease:285-288 correctly uses upgradeMarkerExists). The paragraph that actually explains why the renewal predicates must exist — the keeper-outlives-its-command hazard — is now silently orphaned onto the wrong symbol, and upgradeMarkerExists (:268) and growMarkerOwnedBy (:272) have no doc at all.
- **verifier 理由**：Verified literally. internal/cluster/lock_lease.go has, with no blank line between them, `// ...one tick would re-fence the whole membership plane.` (:252) immediately followed by `// upgradeMarkerHeldByOther renders ...` (:253), running unbroken to `func upgradeMarkerHeldByOther(valLit string) string {` at :263. So the doc block of the new function now opens with 'upgradeMarkerExists / growMarkerOwnedBy are the ownership predicates a RENEWAL is gated on' — a description of two different functions, and of the opposite role: upgradeMarkerHeldByOther gates an ACQUIRE, and PlanRenewUpgradeLease (:285-288) correctly gates the renewal on upgradeMarkerExists, not on it. upgradeMarkerExists (:268) and growMarkerOwnedBy (:272) are now undocumented. MINOR is right — no behaviour is affected — but it is a real instance of the repo's 'comments are assets, a refactor must carry the whole paragraph'
- **对修法的批评**：Correct and complete; nothing to add beyond doing exactly that — blank line after :252, move the renewal-predicate paragraph down to sit immediately above `func upgradeMarkerExists()` where it was before the diff, leaving the new function with its own origin comment. Note the paragraph names growMarkerOwnedBy as well, which sits below upgradeMarkerExists, so placing it above upgradeMarkerExists restores its original (correct) attachment to both. Zero risk: comment-only, no gate or golden touches comment text here.
- **建议修法**：Insert a blank line after :252 so the renewal-predicate paragraph detaches, and move it back down to sit immediately above `func upgradeMarkerExists()` at :268 where it belongs. The new function keeps its own origin comment (:253-262) as its doc.

### H-5 · `internal/cluster/node.go:345`

- **lane**：cluster FSM and locks (batch H: #40/#41/#42) — internal/cluster/{snapshot,fsm,node,offline,membership_ops,lock_lease}.go + restore_identity_test.go, membership_test.go
- **裁决**：CONFIRMED（原报 MINOR → 订正 MINOR）

The honest conclusion from that reading is that the sentinel is unreachable from Apply and the family entry is over-broad, and it gets removed — reopening the exact hole (handleRegister answers store_error, the agent's register loop exits, systemd's control-group kill takes the workloads with it) that this batch closed.
- **verifier 理由**：Verified against the pinned module at /home/weiland/go/pkg/mod/github.com/hashicorp/raft@v1.7.3. raft.go:691 is `case future := <-r.leadershipTransferCh:`, :693 `if r.getLeadershipTransferInProgress() {`, :694 the Debug, :695 `future.respond(ErrLeadershipTransferInProgress)` — the transfer-vs-transfer rejection. The applyCh arm is :905 `case newLog := <-r.applyCh:`, :907 the check, :909 `newLog.respond(ErrLeadershipTransferInProgress)`, :910 continue, :911 close — exactly what the sentence at internal/cluster/node.go:345-346 claims to be citing ('responds it from the leaderLoop's applyCh arm'). The companion citation is also as described: api.go:845 `select {`, :846 `case <-timer:`, :847 `return errorFuture{ErrEnqueueTimeout}`, :850 `case r.applyCh <- logFuture:` — correct (it sits at node.go:349, not :352 as the finding says, and raft.go:691 rather than :693 is the `case` line; neither
- **对修法的批评**：Correct and minimal — swap `raft.go:693-695` for `raft.go:905-911` at node.go:345 and change nothing else; the rest of the sentence ('rejects EVERY Apply', 'for up to ElectionTimeout while the OLD leader still reports State()==Leader') is accurate against the applyCh arm. Two optional hardenings, neither required: the citation is version-pinned in the text already ('raft@v1.7.3'), so a dependency bump makes it stale rather than wrong, which is the honest failure mode; and if the parent wants this class of citation to stop rotting, the durable form is naming the arm ('the applyCh arm of leaderLoop') and keeping the line numbers as a secondary hint, since function/arm names survive a bump and line numbers do not.
- **建议修法**：Change `raft.go:693-695` to `raft.go:905-911` on node.go:345. The surrounding sentence is otherwise accurate and needs no other edit.

### I-F4 · `internal/agent/transfer.go:1176-1183`

- **lane**：agent exec, PTY and transfers (batch I: L3-F1 / L3-F2 / L3-F3 and push --force)
- **裁决**：PARTIAL（原报 MAJOR → 订正 MINOR）
- **主张**：The destination's permission bits are applied to the tmp file BEFORE any content is written, so for the whole duration of a transfer a group- or world-writable tmp sits in the destination directory — and the sha256 is computed over the stream, not the file, so substituted bytes are committed as verified.
- **失败场景**：A shared 0664 config (`/srv/app/config.yaml`, group `app`) is replaced with `tether push config.yaml node:/srv/app/config.yaml --force`. OpenForWriteAtomic creates `config.yaml.tmp.<rand>` at 0600, then immediately fchmods it to 0664 (line 1181) — before the caller has written a byte. Tier B then streams the object into it (internal/agent/transfer.go:335 `io.Copy(f, tee)`), which for a multi-GB file is minutes. Any local user in group `app` can open that tmp O_WRONLY and pwrite over regions already written. The integrity check at :347-351 hashes the TeeReader — i.e. what the agent read off the network, not what is on disk — so the corruption is invisible, and RenameForWriteAtomic then installs the attacker-modified file and the transfer is reported complete. With a 0666 or 0777 destination the same holds for any local user. Before this change the tmp was 0600 for its whole life and the mode only became permissive at rename, so there was no window at all.
- **verifier 理由**：The mechanism is exactly as described — internal/agent/transfer.go:1130-1133 opens the tmp 0600, :1176-1183 fchmods it to the destination's mode, and the still-EMPTY file is returned to the caller at :1185, which then streams into it (tier B, :326-352, minutes for a multi-GB object); the sha is over io.TeeReader(limited, h) at :334, never over a re-read; the ctl mirror at cmd/tether/transfer.go:666-671 has the same ordering with a microsecond window. But the harm framing does not survive scrutiny, and that is what drives the severity down. The tmp IS the committed file: rename changes neither owner, group, nor mode, so the permission set exposed during the window is bit-for-bit the permission set the destination will carry seconds later, and the tmp is owned by the agent uid with the agent's group — precisely the same principal set that can write the finished file at leisure. Any local u
- **对修法的批评**：The concrete proposal is wrong on its load-bearing detail. 'The fd is still open at commit, so the no-TOCTOU property is fully preserved' is false for the agent: both callers close the file before committing — tier A does `cerr := f.Close()` at transfer.go:201-203 and then RenameForWriteAtomic at :207, tier B does `closeErr := f.Close()` at :339-341 and then RenameForWriteAtomic at :351. An f.Chmod inside RenameForWriteAtomic would return ErrClosed and the mode would be silently lost — a regression on top of a fix, and one that no existing test would catch (see I-F7). Two implementations actually work: stash the mode on AtomicWrite and call unix.Fchmodat(w.dirFD, w.tmpName, mode, 0) inside RenameForWriteAtomic just before the rename — this keeps the dirFD-relative, TOCTOU-free property the OpenForWriteAtomic comment sells and needs no open fd — or have each caller fchmod after Sync and b
- **建议修法**：Do the fstatat at open time (you need the dirFD and the name there anyway) but stash the mode on the AtomicWrite and apply `f.Chmod` after the caller's Sync and before RenameForWriteAtomic — the fd is still open at commit, so the no-TOCTOU property the comment sells is fully preserved while the tmp stays 0600 for the entire transfer. Mirror it in cmd/tether/transfer.go. Test: start a tier-B push, and while it is streaming assert the tmp sibling's mode is 0600 regardless of the destination's mode.

### I-F5 · `internal/agent/run.go:861-862`

- **lane**：agent exec, PTY and transfers (batch I: L3-F1 / L3-F2 / L3-F3 and push --force)
- **裁决**：PARTIAL（原报 MAJOR → 订正 MINOR）
- **主张**：ptySessionFor's waitDone check is a check-then-act: procsMu is released before `sess.Master.Write`, so the handler reads the `Master` field unsynchronized while Session.Close() writes `s.Master = nil` — the exact data race pty.go documents and works around for Hangup.
- **失败场景**：An interactive run is ending. The .in handler for that pid calls ptySessionFor, which finds the record with waitDone still false and returns sess, then releases procsMu (run.go:899-906). On the run goroutine, sess.Wait() returns, waitDone is latched (run.go:313-317), unregisterProc runs (:330) and `sess.Close()` (:331) takes s.mu and executes `s.Master = nil` (pty.go:338). Concurrently the handler evaluates `sess.Master` at run.go:862 holding no lock. That is a Go data race on the field: -race will report it, and it is reachable by any ctl that is still sending keystrokes when the shell exits — i.e. by a normal `exit` typed at the prompt. The write itself is harmless (os.File methods are nil-receiver safe and os.File serializes against Close), so the symptom is a -race report and a wrong assumption, not a crash — which is why it survives a green suite.
- **verifier 理由**：The data race itself is real and I will not refute it: the intake handler at run.go:859-863 dereferences sess.Master with no lock, pty.go:326-339 Close() writes s.Master = nil under s.mu, ptySessionFor (run.go:897-906) releases procsMu before returning the pointer, and the two mutexes give no happens-before — so it is a genuine race on the field, exactly as pty.go:298-306 documents verbatim. But the finding's central escalation is wrong. 'This batch removed the unsubscribe entirely ... so every .in message for the whole session lifetime races it' does not hold: the record check IS the replacement bound. waitDone is latched under procsMu (run.go:313-317) before unregisterProc and Close, so every message arriving from that point on gets nil from ptySessionFor and never touches Master. The residual window is the in-flight-callback window — handler passes the check, then is preempted while t
- **对修法的批评**：WriteInput works, and the reviewer is right that s.mu must not be held across the write (a PTY write blocks on a child that is not draining). But there is a smaller fix the reviewer walked past even while quoting the comment that describes it: make Close() close the master fd IN PLACE and stop writing s.Master = nil, exactly as Hangup already does at pty.go:317-318 for this precise reason. That removes the racing WRITE, which fixes every reader at once rather than only the one caller that remembers to switch APIs, and it leaves the exported field's contract unchanged; the only adjustment is that Close's idempotency must key off s.closed rather than `s.Master != nil` (double-Close of an *os.File is a harmless ErrClosed). Either way the structural-budget objection does not apply here — pty.Session has 8 methods against a >40 threshold, so adding one is free; the 'package-level function, no
- **建议修法**：Add `func (s *Session) WriteInput(p []byte) (int, error)` to internal/pty that snapshots `s.Master` under s.mu and does the Write outside it (never hold s.mu across a PTY write — it can block on a child that is not reading), and have the .in handler call `sess.WriteInput(m.Data)` instead of touching the field. Correct the ptySessionFor doc to say what the record check actually buys (it stops routing to a pid that is gone; it does not fence the write). A -race test that loops registerProc/ptySessionFor-write against Close will fail today and pass after.

### I-F6 · `internal/agent/run.go:851-863`

- **lane**：agent exec, PTY and transfers (batch I: L3-F1 / L3-F2 / L3-F3 and push --force)
- **裁决**：CONFIRMED（原报 MINOR → 订正 MINOR）
- **主张**：The session-scoped `pty.*.in` wildcard is not node-scoped, so every agent in a multi-node session now receives a copy of every other node's raw keystroke stream and drops it; the comment's `.ka` analogy hides that `.in` carries a payload.
- **失败场景**：A session has nodes gpu1 and gpu2. An operator runs `tether run --node gpu2 -- bash` and types a passphrase at a sudo prompt. Every keystroke is published to `tether.v2.s.<sid>.pty.<pid>.in`; gpu1's agent matches it with its own `pty.*.in` wildcard, calls ptySessionFor, finds no record for that pid and discards it. gpu1's process therefore observes, in memory, the plaintext input of an interactive session on a different machine. Before this change gpu1 subscribed only to the exact pids it owned and never saw the bytes. This is not an authorization change — the ACL at internal/auth/permissions.go:290 already grants agents `s.<sid>.pty.*.in` — but it is a change from not-delivered to delivered-by-default, and it also multiplies the input fan-out by the node count.
- **verifier 理由**：Factually correct on every checkable point. run.go:854 subscribes proto.SubjectPrefix+".s."+a.cfg.SID+".pty.*.in" with plain nc.Subscribe, so NATS fans a copy to every agent in the session; the subject grammar (internal/proto/subjects.go:200-224) carries sid and pid but no nid, so there is nothing narrower to subscribe to; and it is not an authorization change — `git log -S` shows the agent's `s.<sid>.pty.*.in` Sub grant dates to a7dc201 (P3) and `git diff -- internal/auth/permissions.go` shows no pty line changed in this batch. The behavior change from not-delivered to delivered-and-discarded is real, and so is the .ka analogy problem: .ka handlers only stamp a timestamp (run.go:915-931) while .in carries the raw keystroke payload. I tried and failed to escalate it: pids are ULIDs (internal/proc/proc.go:58-60), so a foreign node's .in can only ever miss the record lookup and be dropped,
- **对修法的批评**：The comment amendment is right and should be done. The structural half of the proposal is overstated on both counts. (1) A nid token in the pty subject tree is not necessarily epoch-scale: ctl already knows the target nid it dispatched to, so an agent that subscribes BOTH `pty.*.in` and `pty.<nid>.*.in` for one release, with ctl publishing to the nid-scoped subject only against agents that advertise it, is an ordinary additive N-1 window — the ProtoVersion bump the finding invokes is not forced by this. (2) More usefully, there is a third option the finding does not consider that keeps the exact subject AND fixes L3-F2: install the .in/.resize subscriptions per-pid but on the SESSION conn, owned by the session finalizer, and have startPtyIntake re-subscribe for every live entry in a.procs when a session is (re)built. That is rebuild-proof for the same reason the wildcard is, without broa
- **建议修法**：Cheapest honest fix: amend the comment at run.go:295-297 to state that the intake is session-wide, that every agent in the session now sees every node's `.in` traffic, and that the `.ka` precedent differs in that `.ka` carries no payload — so the next reader does not inherit the analogy. The structural fix is a nid token in the pty subject tree (`pty.<nid>.<pid>.in`) with the matching per-nid agent grant; that is a subject-grammar change and therefore epoch-scale under the N-1 rule, so it belongs in a separate increment rather than silently in this one.

### I-F7 · `internal/agent/transfer.go:1155`

- **lane**：agent exec, PTY and transfers (batch I: L3-F1 / L3-F2 / L3-F3 and push --force)
- **裁决**：CONFIRMED（原报 MINOR → 订正 MINOR）
- **主张**：The L3-F1 mode-preservation fix has no test at all on either side, which is how I-F3's one-path miss passed all four gates.
- **失败场景**：Any future refactor of OpenForWriteAtomic or writeLocalAtomic silently drops the Lstat/Fstatat+Chmod block and every gate stays green; `push --force` and `pull --force` go back to resetting destinations to 0600 and nothing reports it. The same absence is what let the tier-B pull path (I-F3) ship without the fix in the first place — a mode assertion on either path would have made the asymmetry visible immediately.
- **verifier 理由**：Verified independently. `git diff -- test/determinism/testdata/test_function_inventory.txt` adds exactly 62 entries and none of them matches mode/perm/chmod. Grepping Chmod|Perm() across internal/agent/*_test.go and cmd/tether/*_test.go returns only state_compliance_test.go (state.json 0600), upgrade_smoke_test.go (prev-slot 0755), and p9/p10 install and admin-socket tests — nothing on the transfer write path; internal/agent/transfer_test.go's OpenForWriteAtomic round-trips (:592, :646, :682, :776, :804) assert path safety and rename semantics only. So the L3-F1 fix has no guard on either side, and prerelease-audit-plan.md §5 requires a mutation-verified guard for every fix in this audit ('注入它声称能抓的缺陷 → 确认变红 → 复原'). The causal claim also holds: a mode assertion parameterised over tiers is precisely what would have made I-F3's tier-B gap visible on the day it was written. MINOR is the righ
- **对修法的批评**：Sound, with three additions. (1) Include the destination-absent case and pin the expected result at 0600 (today's behavior), or a future implementation that derives the mode from the umask will pass a table that only checks the preserve-existing rows. (2) Assert the mode of the COMMITTED file after the rename, not of the tmp — and, if I-F4's reordering lands, assert the tmp is 0600 mid-flight as a separate case, so the two fixes cannot silently undo each other. (3) Placement and bookkeeping: the agent rows belong in internal/agent/transfer_test.go next to the existing OpenForWriteAtomic round-trips (whose ValidatedPath harness they can reuse), the ctl rows in cmd/tether/transfer_compliance_test.go; name by unit, tag with `// origin: prerelease audit agent-transfer/L3-F1`, and append the new function names to test/determinism/testdata/test_function_inventory.txt or the identity gate redde
- **建议修法**：One table test per side: create the destination at 0755 / 0644 / absent, run the writer with force=true, assert the resulting mode. Parameterise it over tier A and tier B (which is what turns it into a regression test for I-F3), and mutation-verify by deleting the Chmod line and confirming it reddens.

### J2 · `cmd/tether/main.go:142`

- **lane**：ctl exit classes and CLI surface (batch J)
- **裁决**：CONFIRMED（原报 MINOR → 订正 MINOR）
- **主张**：The subcommand exclusion inspects only args[2], so a flag-before-subcommand invocation that cobra genuinely routes to a subcommand still consumes the daemon's boot budget — the fix closes one spelling of the hole and not its sibling.
- **失败场景**：An operator runs `tether agent --session lab doctor` during a rollback window. Cobra's Find/stripFlags knows `--session` takes a value, strips the pair, and dispatches to `tether agent doctor` — a read-only diagnosis. But isAgentDaemonInvocation sees args[2]=="--session" (starts with "-"), skips the table check, finds no --help/--install-user-service/--uninstall/help in the loop, and returns true. BootUpgradeCheck runs and spends a boot-budget tick belonging to the staged binary's upgrade transaction — precisely the failure the comment at main.go:133-141 says it is fixing.
- **verifier 理由**：Reproduced end to end. root.Find(["agent","--session","lab","doctor"]) returns `tether agent doctor` with rest [--session lab] (cobra's stripFlags knows --session takes a value, agent.go:264-276), and cmd/tether/agent_doctor.go:54 defines its own --session, so the invocation actually runs the read-only doctor. Meanwhile isAgentDaemonInvocation(["tether","agent","--session","lab","doctor"]) = true, so BootUpgradeCheck runs and spends a boot-budget tick — the exact L4-F3 defect, in a spelling the new exclusion does not cover, and one the new test cannot see (it only probes ["tether","agent",<name>]). I looked for a 'deliberate decision' defence: the comment at main.go:133-141 documents only why args[2] is skipped when it starts with '-' (the `--nid doctor` flag-VALUE case); it does not claim to handle flag-before-subcommand, so this is a genuine residual gap rather than an adjudicated trad
- **对修法的批评**：The conservative variant in the proposal is the dangerous one. 'Return false if ANY arg in args[2:] is an exact member of the table and is not preceded by a value-taking flag' needs a hand-maintained pre-Cobra arity table duplicating the agent command's flag definitions; the day someone adds a value-taking flag to `agent` and forgets that table, `tether agent --newflag doctor`-shaped argv — or worse, a real daemon launch whose flag value happens to be 'join' or 'config' — flips to non-daemon, which is J1's failure, the strictly worse direction. If this is fixed at all, derive the arity from the real command tree (newRootCmd().Flags() walked in the shim, or a table with a reconciliation test that fails when `agent` grows a value-taking flag not listed), and add `tether agent --session lab doctor` plus the existing `--nid doctor` case to the guard so both directions are pinned. Doing nothi
- **建议修法**：Scan args[2:] for the first token that is not a flag and not the value of a value-taking flag (or, simpler and still conservative pre-Cobra: return false if ANY arg in args[2:] is an exact member of agentNonDaemonSubcommands and is not preceded by a `--flag` that takes a value). Add `tether agent --session lab doctor` to the test's negative cases alongside the existing `--nid doctor` positive case.

### J3 · `cmd/tether/exit_class_family_test.go:43-49`

- **lane**：ctl exit classes and CLI surface (batch J)
- **裁决**：CONFIRMED（原报 MAJOR → 订正 MINOR）
- **主张**：The only guard on execFailureMessage's colon split is vacuous: it uses `store_error`, whose class (exitInternal) is byte-identical to the unclassified fallback, so the assertion passes with or without the split.
- **失败场景**：Someone reverts execFailureMessage to look up the whole chunk string (the exact defect CLI-F1 says it fixed for run and this batch fixed for exec). Then brokerCodeExitClass("store_error: disk on fire") misses the table and returns exitInternal=70, while brokerCodeExitClass("store_error") returns exitInternal=70 — withDetail == bare, the assertion is satisfied, and the test is green. The outer loop cannot catch it either: `for code := range brokerCodeExitClasses` feeds only BARE codes, which classify identically with or without a split. So `tether exec node1 -- x` receiving `not_a_member: fp 0xdead …` silently goes back to exit 70 ("back off and retry" per §9.13) with a fully green suite.
- **verifier 理由**：Mechanically true and mutation-verified. Probe: brokerCodeExitClass("store_error: disk on fire")=70, brokerCodeExitClass("store_error")=70, exitInternal=70, so the withDetail/bare comparison at exit_class_family_test.go:43-49 is an identity. I then applied the exact defect the assertion claims to catch — replaced execFailureMessage's colon split with `code := strings.TrimSpace(chunkErr)` — and TestExecRefusalsCarryTheSameClassAsRun passed, and so did the ENTIRE cmd/tether package (83s, ok). Under that mutation classifyExit(execFailureMessage("not_a_member: fp 0xdead")) drops from 77 to 70, exactly as the finding predicts, with nothing in the repo red. Severity is inflated, though, and I verified why: the outer loop is NOT vacuous. Reverting CLI-F1 itself (execFailureMessage back to a bare fmt.Errorf) makes the loop fail loudly ('exec exits 70 for "not_owner" while run exits 77'). So the
- **对修法的批评**：Correct and cheap. Prefer the second half of the proposal (drive execFailureMessage through the same corpus TestRunFailureMessageSplitsCodeFromDetail already uses at error_hints_test.go:223-238) over hand-picking two codes: a shared table means the two verbs cannot drift again, and it removes the trap that made this assertion vacuous in the first place — picking a code whose class happens to equal the fallback. One caveat: assert absolute classes (77, 64), not exec-vs-run equality, for the detail-bearing forms; an equality assertion would go green again the day someone breaks BOTH readers the same way, which is precisely the shape of the original CLI-F1 defect in reverse.
- **建议修法**：Replace the store_error pair with codes whose class is NOT 70, e.g. assert classifyExit(execFailureMessage("not_a_member: fp 0xdead not in session")) == exitNoPerm and classifyExit(execFailureMessage("node_offline: last heartbeat 5m ago")) == exitUsage. Better still, drive the same table error_hints_test.go's TestRunFailureMessageSplitsCodeFromDetail already uses through execFailureMessage, so the two verbs share one adversarial corpus instead of one relative comparison plus one identity.

### J4 · `cmd/tether/exit_class_family_test.go:116-129`

- **lane**：ctl exit classes and CLI surface (batch J)
- **裁决**：CONFIRMED（原报 MINOR → 订正 MINOR）
- **主张**：The alert-ack deadline guard asserts a property of a constant and never that the constant is wired into ackAlert, and it hardcodes the broker budget it claims to derive — so both the wiring and the invariant can rot green.
- **失败场景**：Two independent regressions stay green. (a) Someone changes alert_gate.go:231 back to `nc.Request(..., alertLsTimeout)` while leaving `alertAckTimeout = 6*time.Second` declared: both assertions still hold (6s >= 5000ms, 6s != 500ms) and `tether alert ack` is again giving up at 500ms on an ack the leader is committing. Nothing else in the package references alertAckTimeout. (b) Someone raises Config.ExposeForwardTimeout's default from 5s to 10s in internal/broker/broker.go:356: the guard's private `const brokerForwardBudget = 5*1000` does not move, so it stays green while the ctl once more times out before the broker it is waiting on — the exact defect CLI-F3 was filed for.
- **verifier 理由**：Both halves verified, (a) by mutation. (a) I rewired alert_gate.go:231 to `nc.Request(..., alertLsTimeout)` leaving alertAckTimeout=6s declared: TestAlertAckWaitsAtLeastAsLongAsTheBrokerDoes passed, and the whole cmd/tether package passed (83s, ok). grep confirms alertAckTimeout is referenced nowhere else in the package's tests, and golangci-lint's `unused` will not report it because the test file uses it. So the constant can be fully unwired and every gate stays green. (b) exit_class_family_test.go:119's `const brokerForwardBudget = 5 * 1000` does duplicate a value that is computed, not declared: internal/broker/broker.go:356-360 `func (c *Config) ExposeForwardTimeout()` returns the 5s default, and it really is the budget behind this ack (clusterwrite.go:305 / broker.go:992 build the forwarder that AttachAlertSink uses with exactly that value). cmd/tether already imports internal/broker
- **对修法的批评**：Reading the value instead of copying it is right and free. The proposed live-timing test is weaker than it sounds: a responder that sleeps ~1s proves only that the deadline exceeds 1s, so it would stay green against a 2s regression while the broker still spends 5s — assert the deadline against `(&broker.Config{}).ExposeForwardTimeout()` and express the responder's delay in terms of alertLsTimeout (e.g. 4x) rather than a fresh magic number, or the new test re-creates the exact 'deadline measured against a number you do not control' problem it is closing. It also adds ~1s of wall clock to `make test` for a wiring assertion; a cheaper honest alternative is to make the timeout an explicit parameter of ackAlert (or read it back through a seam) so the wiring is assertable without a real timer at all.
- **建议修法**：Read the number instead of copying it: `if alertAckTimeout < (&broker.Config{}).ExposeForwardTimeout() { … }`. And add a wiring assertion — the cheapest honest one is a table-driven timing test against the embedded nats-server (startD8ReviewNATS already exists in ack_alert_error_test.go): subscribe a responder that sleeps ~1s before replying "ok" and assert ackAlert succeeds; that fails outright against the 500ms wiring and cannot be satisfied by a declared-but-unused constant.

### J5 · `cmd/tether/agent_join.go:159-165`

- **lane**：ctl exit classes and CLI surface (batch J)
- **裁决**：CONFIRMED（原报 MINOR → 订正 MINOR）
- **主张**：The new "ONE FOLD" comment is false twice over: it names a flag `--run` that does not exist, and it claims agentDaemonConfig is the single place the daemon's resolution rules live while three of them still sit outside it and still differ on the join path.
- **失败场景**：A reader chasing this comment greps for `--run`, finds nothing (the flag is `--start`, registered at agent_join.go:106), and then trusts the stronger claim — "the fold is the single place those rules live precisely so a second assembly point cannot drift from it" — and assumes `join --start` and `tether agent` are now equivalent. They are not: (1) the daemon resolves a size-capped rotating log sink plus a process-owned boot.err via resolveAgentLogSink/redirectStderrTo, while runJoinedAgent:145 uses newLogger("info", false) => stderr only, so a long-lived agent onboarded with `join --start` has NO log rotation and its agent.yaml log_file/log_max_size_mb/log_max_backups are still ignored; (2) the daemon defaults tunnel-addr to 127.0.0.1:7000 via pickFlagOrYaml, while runJoinedAgent:182 uses `ay.TunnelAddr` raw, so on a machine joined purely by invite (writeAgentConfig never sets tunnel_addr) the expose data plane is silently disabled; (3) nid-from-yaml fallback is daemon-only.
- **verifier 理由**：All sub-claims check out. agent_join.go:106 registers the flag as "start"; the comment at :163 says `tether agent join --run`, a flag that does not exist anywhere in the tree. The completeness claim ('the fold is the single place those rules live') is contradicted by three resolutions that stay in the daemon's RunE: (1) agent.go:319-333 resolveAgentLogSink + redirectStderrTo vs runJoinedAgent:145's newLogger("info", false) — so a `join --start` daemon has no rotating sink, no process-owned agent.boot.err, and ignores agent.yaml log_file / log_max_size_mb / log_max_backups (agent.go:43-45), plus --log-level/--log-json; (2) agent.go:310 pickFlagOrYaml("tunnel-addr") with the flag default 127.0.0.1:7000 (agent.go:405) vs agent_join.go:182's raw ay.TunnelAddr, and writeAgentConfig (:113-140) never assigns TunnelAddr, so a machine joined purely by invite gets no ExposeAdapter at all; (3) nid-
- **对修法的批评**：Fixing the flag name is unambiguous. On the completeness claim, prefer the downgrade over the code move, or move only the log sink: resolveAgentLogSink calls pickFlagOrYaml(cmd, "log-file", ...) and the join command has no log-file flag, so cmd.Flags().Changed("log-file") returns false and the yaml path still wins — it works, but the fold would then silently depend on flag-existence tolerance and that contract should be stated where it is relied on. Do NOT 'fix' the tunnel-addr asymmetry by carrying the daemon's 127.0.0.1:7000 default into the join path: on a remote invite-joined machine that default is wrong, so the change would swap 'expose quietly off' for 'expose repeatedly dialling a bogus loopback', which is worse for the operator. The honest fixes there are to write tunnel_addr into agent.yaml at join time or to print which data-plane state the operator ended up in — both are sepa
- **建议修法**：Fix the flag name to `--start`, and downgrade the completeness claim to what is true — list the three resolutions that remain daemon-only and why (or move the log-sink resolution into the fold as well, which is the change that actually makes the claim true for a `join --start` daemon).

### J6 · `docs/usage.md:1732`

- **lane**：ctl exit classes and CLI surface (batch J)
- **裁决**：CONFIRMED（原报 MINOR → 订正 MINOR）
- **主张**：§9.13's exec/run bullet — the section this batch says it is honouring — is now false: exec refusals go through classifyExit and can emit 64/75/77 that are not remote process codes. The same false claim is repeated in cmd/tether/exitcode.go:23.
- **失败场景**：A monitoring author reads §9.13, sees "`exec` / `run` 透传远端进程退出码 … 不入分类器；判别靠'你跑的是哪个命令'", and writes a wrapper that reads any exit code from `tether exec` as the remote command's own. After batch J, `tether exec gpu-01 -- ls` refused with `not_a_member` exits 77 from the ctl itself; the wrapper reports "the remote `ls` exited 77". Symmetrically, §9.13's robust-retry rule ("仅 64/77 当终态") now applies to exec refusals but the doc says it does not. §9.13 also gained no row for the destructive gate's new 75 / 64, even though `session rm` / `expose` / `push` / `pull` / `run` all changed from 70 to one of those.
- **verifier 理由**：The doc really is wrong now, and I confirmed the mechanism: exec.go:142 returns execFailureMessage(chunk.Error) to cobra's RunE, which reaches main.go:90-107's classifyExit sink (only the `exit` chunk os.Exit()s, exec.go:134-137), and the git diff shows execFailureMessage now returns &ExitError{Class: ...}. docs/usage.md:1732's closing sentence, '所以「77=权限」仅限 broker-RPC 命令', is therefore false — `tether exec node -- x` refused with not_a_member now exits 77 from the ctl — and §9.13's robust-retry rule now applies to exec refusals while the bullet says it does not. cmd/tether/exitcode.go:20-23 repeats it. git diff -- docs/usage.md confirms the file was not touched here (only lines 175-180, 1573-1580, 1615). Two corrections to the finding's framing, neither fatal: the `run` half of the bullet was ALREADY false at HEAD — runFailureMessage's &ExitError predates this batch (batch-A A1), and ru
- **对修法的批评**：Doc-only, cheap, and correctly aimed at the distinguisher (a refusal prints `exec: <hint> (<code>)` on stderr before any remote process starts; a pass-through value comes from a started process). Two cautions: write it so the retry rule stays exception-free — the whole point of the M17 note above it is that classes get fixed rather than the rule gaining exceptions, so phrase the exec/run split as 'which of two sources produced the value', not as an exception to 69/70/75-are-retryable. And correct exitcode.go:20-23 in the same edit for run as well as exec, otherwise the SSOT comment keeps asserting the half that was already untrue before this batch.
- **建议修法**：Add one bullet to §9.13 splitting the two sources for exec/run: a REFUSAL before the remote process starts carries a taxonomy class (64/69/70/75/77) and is distinguishable because it prints `exec: <hint> (<code>)` / `run failed: …` on stderr, while a value from a started remote process is passed through verbatim. Add a §9.13 row (or a note under 75 and 64) for the destructive gate: quorum_lost => 75, force_single_active => 64. Correct exitcode.go:20-23 to say only the remote-exit path bypasses the sink.

### J7 · `cmd/tether/exit_class_family_test.go:63-84`

- **lane**：ctl exit classes and CLI surface (batch J)
- **裁决**：CONFIRMED（原报 MINOR → 订正 MINOR）
- **主张**：TestEveryAbortRefusalIsAUsageError scans a hardcoded five-file list for `fmt.Errorf(` on the same source line as "aborted", so it cannot fail for a new file, a multi-line construction, or errors.New.
- **失败场景**：A later change adds a typed-confirm prompt to a NEW file (say cmd/tether/cluster_wipe.go) with `return fmt.Errorf("aborted (type the node_id to confirm)")`, or writes an existing one as `return errors.New("aborted (…)")`, or wraps the call across two lines. All three produce a bare error, classifyExit falls through to 70, and docs/usage.md §9.13 tells automation to retry a prompt the operator just declined — with this guard green. The guard has no reconciliation step that would go red when its own five-file ledger stops being the complete set, which is the property every other ledger in CLAUDE.md §5 is required to have.
- **verifier 理由**：Mutation-verified. I added cmd/tether/zz_abort_probe.go containing `return fmt.Errorf("aborted (type the node_id to confirm)")` and TestEveryAbortRefusalIsAUsageError passed — a new file with an unclassified abort refusal is invisible to the guard, exactly as claimed. The predicate at :73-75 is a single-line strings.Contains(line, "fmt.Errorf("), so errors.New and any multi-line construction are equally invisible, and the file list at :66-68 is a literal with no reconciliation step — the one property CLAUDE.md §5 requires of every ledger in the repo ('账本里已不存在的条目会让门变红' has no counterpart here for entries that SHOULD exist). I confirmed the list is complete today: all 11 refusal sites (cluster.go:586/634/860, cluster_retire.go:48/115, cluster_offline.go:227/386/518/569, proxy.go:110, cluster_backup.go:111) are usageErr and live in those five files, which is precisely what makes the missing
- **对修法的批评**：Glob-walking the package directory is the right shape, but the proposed predicate broadening needs go/ast, not more strings.Contains: 'aborted' appears in cmd/tether/cluster_ops.go:87 inside an fmt.Fprintf that PRINTS an operator message, and in prose comments at cluster_add_drive.go:584 and :686 and error_hints.go:182 ('aborted the whole fleet'). A line-based rule that flags 'any construction of an aborted refusal that is not usageErr' will either false-positive on those or need an ad-hoc skip list, and a guard that turns the word 'aborted' into a lint hazard will get worked around. The honest form is to walk return statements and require the returned expression to be usageErr(...) or &ExitError{...} whenever its string literal begins with 'aborted' — that also covers errors.New and multi-line construction for free, and it keeps prose out of scope.
- **建议修法**：Walk the package directory instead of a literal list (filepath.Glob("*.go"), skipping _test.go), and reject any construction of an "aborted" refusal that is not usageErr/&ExitError — i.e. also flag `errors.New(`. If the five-file list is kept for speed, add the reverse assertion: no file outside the list may contain the word "aborted" in a return statement.

### J8 · `cmd/tether/transfer.go:666-667`

- **lane**：ctl exit classes and CLI surface (batch J)
- **裁决**：PARTIAL（原报 MINOR → 订正 MINOR）
- **主张**：The permission-carrying chmod runs BEFORE the content is written, so the temp file sits in the destination directory holding partial content at the destination's (possibly group/world-writable) mode instead of the 0600 it used to hold for its whole life.
- **失败场景**：`tether pull --force node:/x /srv/shared/app.conf` where /srv/shared is group-writable and app.conf is 0664. os.OpenFile creates the temp at 0600, f.Chmod immediately widens it to 0664, and only then does f.Write copy the payload and f.Sync flush it. Between the chmod and commitLocalTemp's os.Rename, any process in that group can open the temp (its name is enumerable by listing or inotify on the directory) and write into it; the rename then installs the attacker's bytes as the atomically-committed result of the operator's pull. The old code gave the temp 0600 until the instant of the rename, so the same directory permissions were not enough. The fix does not need this window: nothing reads the mode until the rename.
- **verifier 理由**：The ordering fact is exactly as described — cmd/tether/transfer.go:649 opens the temp 0600, :666-667 Lstat+f.Chmod widen it, :669 f.Write copies the payload — but the security scenario does not survive. The mode carried onto the temp is by construction the mode the COMMITTED file will have: after commitLocalTemp's os.Rename, /srv/shared/app.conf is 0664 in a group-writable directory, so any process that could have opened the temp for write can open and rewrite the committed file microseconds later with the same credentials. The window therefore grants no capability the attacker lacks after the rename; the increase in exposure comes from carrying the mode at all (the adjudicated L3-F1 fix), not from carrying it early. The temp name also carries a random newTransferID suffix and is O_EXCL, so winning the window requires inotify plus a race that buys nothing. The finding is also wrong that
- **对修法的批评**：The ctl-side half is safe and free: f is still open through :669-673, so moving `_ = f.Chmod(...)` to sit after f.Sync() and before f.Close() is a pure reordering with no behavioural change. The agent-side half as written is wrong and would reintroduce the documented TOCTOU: both callers of OpenForWriteAtomic (internal/agent/transfer.go:198-207 and :335-339) Close the file BEFORE RenameForWriteAtomic, so 'apply it in AtomicWrite's commit step' can only be a path-based chmod after the rename — precisely what the :1160-1164 rationale rejects. If it is done at all it must stay an fchmod on the still-open fd before Close, which means editing both call sites and threading the captured mode through, for a benefit I could not make non-zero. Worth flagging while this file is open, since the reviewer's own scenario touches it: the tier-B pull path at cmd/tether/transfer.go:569-570 opens its temp
- **建议修法**：Move the mode carry to after the successful Write+Sync and before commitLocalTemp (the fd is still open, so it stays an fchmod with nothing to race against). On the agent side, return the captured dst mode from OpenForWriteAtomic and apply it in AtomicWrite's commit step rather than at open. If the ordering is deliberate, say so in the comment and state why a widened partial temp is acceptable.

### K-F10 · `test/p10/config_preservation_test.go:26`

- **lane**：deploy and release surface (batch K): scripts/install.sh, internal/proto.SameRelease and its call sites, cmd/tether/node.go, cmd/tether/cluster_upgrade_drive.go, internal/clusterupgrade/plan.go, internal/broker/reexec.go, docs/broker-ops.md, docs/usage.md, test/p10/config_preservation_test.go
- **裁决**：CONFIRMED（原报 MINOR → 订正 MINOR）
- **主张**：B4 — the BLOCKER — is the broker path, and the new test file exercises only the agent sibling; the broker configs and unit files have no coverage at all and no seam that would allow any.
- **失败场景**：B4 is stated as 'a re-run silently reverts a hardened broker to publicly unauthenticated (the nats.conf authorization block and the unit's --auth-callout-seeds-dir are both wiped)'. All four tests in the new file pass `--role agent`; nothing anywhere writes /etc/tether/broker.yaml, /etc/tether/Caddyfile, /etc/tether/nats.d/nats.conf or the three unit files through config_dest under test. install_broker hardcodes ETC_DIR=/etc/tether (line 741) and SYSTEMD_DIR=/etc/systemd/system (line 744) with no override, so the path is untestable hermetically — even though write_journald_dropin right beside it carries TETHER_JOURNALD_ROOT precisely as a test seam (line 647). A future edit that routes broker.yaml around config_dest, or that reorders the config_dest/chmod pair for a unit file, is invisible to `make test`, `make gates` and `make e2e-parallel`.
- **verifier 理由**：Verified: all four tests in test/p10/config_preservation_test.go pass `--role agent`, and install_broker hardcodes ETC_DIR="/etc/tether" (:741) and SYSTEMD_DIR="/etc/systemd/system" (:745) with no override, next to write_journald_dropin's TETHER_JOURNALD_ROOT seam at :647. The three existing broker tests are all --dry-run, which returns before config_dest. I also checked the deploy tier: drill 32 runs a real root broker install but has zero config-preservation or .new assertions, so the reviewer is right that B4's own path is untested at every tier. Severity held at MINOR rather than raised: the mechanism (config_dest) is shared and IS mutation-covered by the agent tests, so what is uncovered is a future ROUTING regression (a broker write bypassing config_dest), not the fix itself.
- **对修法的批评**：TETHER_ETC_ROOT / TETHER_SYSTEMD_ROOT alone will not make the broker path hermetically testable, and the finding assumes it will. install_broker also runs `useradd` (:747), `install -d -o tether -g tether` (:761-767) and place_binary into /usr/local/bin — all root-only, all under `set -e` via run()/eval, so a non-root `make test` would die before reaching broker.yaml. Either add a root-only skip (which makes the test dead on developer machines and in the non-root CI job) or seam the ownership calls too, which is a much larger change to a root installer than the finding implies. Cheaper and stronger: assert config preservation in drill 32, which already runs the real install as root, and keep the Go tier on the shared helper. Also weigh that each new env var redirecting where a root installer writes is itself surface — TETHER_JOURNALD_ROOT set the precedent for one, not for three.
- **建议修法**：Add TETHER_ETC_ROOT / TETHER_SYSTEMD_ROOT test-only seams alongside TETHER_JOURNALD_ROOT (`ETC_DIR="${TETHER_ETC_ROOT:-/etc}/tether"`, `SYSTEMD_DIR="${TETHER_SYSTEMD_ROOT:-/etc/systemd/system}"`), then add the broker half of the test: seed a broker.yaml containing a broker.cluster block and a nats.conf containing an authorization block, re-run --role broker --skip-download, and assert both survive byte-identical, that the four .new files exist with mode 644, and that the banner names them.

### K-F11 · `scripts/install.sh:845`

- **lane**：deploy and release surface (batch K): scripts/install.sh, internal/proto.SameRelease and its call sites, cmd/tether/node.go, cmd/tether/cluster_upgrade_drive.go, internal/clusterupgrade/plan.go, internal/broker/reexec.go, docs/broker-ops.md, docs/usage.md, test/p10/config_preservation_test.go
- **裁决**：PARTIAL（原报 MINOR → 订正 MINOR）
- **主张**：The dry-run policy line is emitted three times per broker dry-run and is unconditional, so it announces 'existing config/unit files would be KEPT' on a host that has none — while its own comment claims the dry-run now describes the program that runs.
- **失败场景**：`install.sh --role broker --domain x --acme-email y --dry-run --skip-download` on a clean build host prints, verbatim and three times: '+ (dry-run) existing config/unit files would be KEPT; new content written as <file>.new'. There are no existing files; the real run would write all seven real paths. The block's own comment (repeated at :843, :879, :1023) says the duplication exists 'otherwise a --dry-run is describing a different program than the one that runs' — but the message it adds is itself decoupled from what the run would do, because nothing in the dry-run branch ever calls config_dest or tests `-e`. TestDryRunStatesTheConfigPolicy asserts only that the substring is present, which it is either way.
- **verifier 理由**：The mechanical half is confirmed — I ran the broker dry-run on a clean host and the line '+ (dry-run) existing config/unit files would be KEPT; new content written as <file>.new' printed three times (from :843-849, :879-885, :1023-:1030), none of which stats anything, and TestDryRunStatesTheConfigPolicy asserts only substring presence so it passes either way. The framing is refuted: 'existing config/unit files would be KEPT' is a CONDITIONAL policy statement, not an assertion that such files exist, so it is not a claim 'the same change falsified' — it is true on a host with none. What survives is a real but smaller defect: pointless triplication of one message and a preview that cannot distinguish the two outcomes, guarded by a near-tautological assertion.
- **对修法的批评**：The main proposal contradicts an explicit, commented design decision in the same file and should be rejected. enable_broker_units at :619-623 states the principle directly: 'a dry-run must preview the same intent on any build host (CI containers have no /run/systemd/system, and a host-dependent dry-run is unassertable)' — and TestInstallShBrokerDryRun / TestInstallShBrokerBannerSelfConsistent depend on that host-independence. Calling config_dest for real in the dry-run branches makes the preview depend on whatever happens to exist in /etc/tether on the developer's machine, and worse, config_dest APPENDS to KEPT_CONFIGS, so a dry-run would then print '⚠ EXISTING CONFIG KEPT — this run did NOT overwrite the following' about a run that wrote nothing. Collapsing the three copies into one emission and tightening the test to assert it appears exactly once are the parts worth taking.
- **建议修法**：Have the dry-run branches call config_dest for real (it only stats and assigns; the write is what dry-run skips) and print the per-file verdict — '+ (dry-run) /etc/tether/broker.yaml exists → would write /etc/tether/broker.yaml.new' — then emit report_kept_configs once at the end for both dry and real runs. That collapses the three copies into one and makes the preview true. Tighten TestDryRunStatesTheConfigPolicy to seed one existing file and assert only that file is named.

### K-F12 · `cmd/tether/node.go:436`

- **lane**：deploy and release surface (batch K): scripts/install.sh, internal/proto.SameRelease and its call sites, cmd/tether/node.go, cmd/tether/cluster_upgrade_drive.go, internal/clusterupgrade/plan.go, internal/broker/reexec.go, docs/broker-ops.md, docs/usage.md, test/p10/config_preservation_test.go
- **裁决**：CONFIRMED（原报 MINOR → 订正 MINOR）
- **主张**：The origin comment was pasted verbatim onto a site whose polarity is inverted: at this one SameRelease widens a REFUSAL, so it produces more 'UNCONFIRMED' verdicts, which is the exact harm the comment says it prevents.
- **失败场景**：The comment reads 'this is a CONFIRMATION check — a false negative reports a successful upgrade as unconfirmed'. But line 437 is `if newVersion != "" && proto.SameRelease(newVersion, startRelease) { … return UNCONFIRMED }` — matching MORE pairs here means refusing MORE upgrades. Concretely: a node currently running a locally-built binary that stamps 'v0.5.1' (Makefile:5 keeps the v) is upgraded to the released tarball, whose smoke-normalized NewVersion is '0.5.1'. Before this change the two differed, the poll ran, the node re-registered as '0.5.1' and line 481 committed it. Now line 437 matches, so `node upgrade --wait` returns exitUsage UNCONFIRMED before polling at all — and `--all` treats that as a canary failure and leaves the rest of the fleet on the old binary. The behaviour change may well be the intended semantics (same release ⇒ no re-register proof), but the comment justifying it argues the opposite direction, so the next reader cannot tell which was meant.
- **verifier 理由**：The comment-polarity claim is exactly right. node.go:437 is `if newVersion != "" && proto.SameRelease(newVersion, startRelease) { … return UNCONFIRMED }` — a REFUSAL predicate, so widening it produces more UNCONFIRMED verdicts, while the comment pasted above it argues the change exists because 'a false negative reports a successful upgrade as unconfirmed'. The other four sites (:481, drive.go:313 and :271, plan.go:61, reexec.go:55) are all accept-as-converged predicates where the same sentence reads correctly. In a repo whose CLAUDE.md §5 treats comments as the load-bearing record of 'why', a comment that argues the opposite of what its line does is a genuine defect — but a documentation one, hence MINOR.
- **对修法的批评**：Take the first branch of the proposed fix (site-specific rationale) and REJECT the second outright: 'drop SameRelease here if the widening was unintended' would reintroduce the S4/F3 defect this guard exists for. Because :481 was widened, a node whose stale pre-upgrade row still reads 'v0.5.1' now satisfies SameRelease(entry.ReleaseVersion, newVersion="0.5.1") on the very FIRST poll, so without the widened guard at :437 waitForUpgradeCommit would print '✔ registered as v0.5.1 — upgrade COMMITTED' off the stale row: a hollow COMMITTED, which is strictly worse than a conservative UNCONFIRMED. The two lines are a matched pair; the widening at :437 is required by the widening at :481. The proposed waitForUpgradeCommit test pinning the verdict is worth adding either way, since K-F2 shows this site is otherwise unwired-tested.
- **建议修法**：Replace the pasted comment at :436 with the site-specific rationale ('normalising here WIDENS the same-tag refusal on purpose: v0.5.1 and 0.5.1 name one release, so neither can supply re-register proof'), or drop SameRelease here if the widening was unintended. Either way add a waitForUpgradeCommit test for newVersion "0.5.1" / startRelease "v0.5.1" pinning the chosen verdict.

### K-F13 · `scripts/install.sh:1196`

- **lane**：deploy and release surface (batch K): scripts/install.sh, internal/proto.SameRelease and its call sites, cmd/tether/node.go, cmd/tether/cluster_upgrade_drive.go, internal/clusterupgrade/plan.go, internal/broker/reexec.go, docs/broker-ops.md, docs/usage.md, test/p10/config_preservation_test.go
- **裁决**：PARTIAL（原报 MINOR → 订正 MINOR）
- **主张**：B7's principle — never delete an irreplaceable key without a word — was applied to the agent and not to the broker sibling in the same file and the same change, where the deletion is larger.
- **失败场景**：`install.sh --role broker --uninstall` runs `rm -rf /etc/tether /var/lib/tether /var/log/tether /var/run/tether` with no warning and no opt-out. /etc/tether/secrets holds account.nk (the cluster ACCOUNT seed that signs the roster and every join bundle) and broker.nk; /var/lib/tether holds the SQLite DB, the raft log and the JetStream store. Losing account.nk is the broker-side equivalent of losing keys/default.nk: it cannot be regenerated and it orphans the cluster's signing identity. Meanwhile uninstall_agent, ten lines of new code above it, now prints a five-line warning before removing ~/.tether. An operator tearing down one broker to rebuild it loses the account seed and only finds out at the next `cluster join prepare`.
- **verifier 理由**：The asymmetry is real and verified: uninstall_agent gained a five-line warning at :519-527 in this very change, while uninstall_broker's `rm -rf /etc/tether /var/lib/tether /var/log/tether /var/run/tether` at :1196 is unchanged, unwarned and un-opt-outable; /etc/tether/secrets does hold account.nk and broker.nk (install.sh:835, cmd/tether/cluster_secrets.go:57-58). But the load-bearing premise is refuted. (1) 'It cannot be regenerated' is false — docs/cluster-runbook.md §2.1 documents account.nk + CA rotation as a supported (disruptive, full-fleet re-provision) procedure, which is precisely what makes it UNLIKE keys/default.nk. (2) account.nk is SHARED across brokers, so tearing down one host of an N>=3 cluster loses no unique material; only a single-broker teardown does, and there the raft DB in /var/lib/tether is the larger loss anyway. (3) B7's actual defect was a SCOPE violation — an
- **对修法的批评**：Print the warning: yes, and word it as 'forces the §2.1 full-fleet re-provision', not 'cannot be regenerated'. Reject the rest. A --keep-data flag, or excluding /etc/tether/secrets by default, breaks the full-symmetry uninstall that test/simcluster/drills/32-install-lifecycle.sh pins ('#76 uninstall DISABLES the units before removing them', '_uninstall_zeroed', 'reinstall broker idempotent after the full-symmetry uninstall') and, worse, leaves a fresh install to silently inherit a previous cluster's account seed and CA — the ghost-VOTER class of incident this fleet has already lived through once. A rebuild-in-place should back the secrets dir up explicitly, not be handed a default that makes 'clean' installs dirty.
- **建议修法**：Print the same shape of warning before the rm in uninstall_broker, naming /etc/tether/secrets/account.nk and /var/lib/tether explicitly, and add a --keep-data flag (or exclude /etc/tether/secrets by default the way --session narrows the agent) so a rebuild-in-place has a non-destructive path. Assert the warning text in a p10 dry-run uninstall test alongside TestInstallShBrokerUninstallSymmetry.

Cos
- **对修法的批评**：Correct and zero-risk. Merge into one parenthesised block with stdlib first and a blank line before the module import, matching every other file in the package.
